are volume polynomials associated with the stable graph
\Gamma

and with zero boundary lengths
L_{p}=0
(
p=1,\dots n
) for the bordered boundaries in the decomposed Riemann surfaces
specified by the univalent vertices in
\Gamma
. Then, the following proposition is proved.

Proposition 3.8
.

The constant term in the twisted volume polynomial

V^{\mathrm{M}(p)}_{g,n}\big[\mathsf{f}^{\mathrm{MV}}\big]

of the
(2,p)
minimal string normalized by
\beta_{g,n}
in equation (
3.5
),

\displaystyle\mathrm{Vol}\mathcal{Q}^{\mathrm{M}(p)}_{g,n}:=\beta_{g,n}V^{\mathrm{M}(p)}_{g,n}\big[\mathsf{f}^{\mathrm{MV}}\big](0,\dots,0),

(3.9)

is obtained, as a sum over stable graphs
\Gamma\in G_{g,n}
, by

\displaystyle\mathrm{Vol}\mathcal{Q}^{\mathrm{M}(p)}_{g,n}=

\displaystyle\sum_{\Gamma\in G_{g,n}}\frac{\alpha_{g,n}}{|\mathrm{Aut}\left(\Gamma\right)|}

\displaystyle\times\sum_{|\mathbf{d}|\leq 3g-3+n-k}(2d_{1},\dots,2d_{k})_{\Gamma}^{\mathrm{M}(p)}\frac{\prod_{i=1}^{k}(2d_{i}+1)!}{(6g-6+2n)!}\prod_{i=1}^{k}\zeta(2d_{i}+2),

(3.10)

in terms of
(2d_{1},\dots,2d_{k})_{\Gamma}^{\mathrm{M}(p)}
defined by equation (
3.8
) for the
(2,p)
minimal string,
where the normalization factor
\alpha_{g,n}
is the same as equation (
3.3
).

Remark 3.9
.

We refer to
\mathrm{Vol}\mathcal{Q}^{\mathrm{M}(p)}_{g,n}
as
a twisted volume of the
(2,p)
minimal string, since this is a natural combinatorial analogue of the Masur–Veech volume, although the geometric derivation of this volume is missing in the direct study of the quantum moduli space of the Liouville gravity.
A crucial difference between the combinatorial formulae of Theorem
3.2
and Proposition
3.8
is the degree constraint in the sum.
In the computation of the Masur–Veech volume, the imposed degree constraint is
|\mathbf{d}|=3g-3+n-k
. On the other hand for the
(2,p)
minimal string, the weaker degree constraint
|\mathbf{d}|\leq 3g-3+n-k
is imposed.
If the degree constraint for the Masur–Veech volume is instead imposed for the volume formula of the minimal string,
then the twisted volumes
\mathrm{Vol}\mathcal{Q}^{\mathrm{M}(p)}_{g,n}
reduce to the Masur–Veech volumes
\mathrm{Vol}\mathcal{Q}_{g,n}
.

Here we show combinatorial computations for
\mathrm{Vol}\mathcal{Q}^{\mathrm{M}(p)}_{1,1}
and
\mathrm{Vol}\mathcal{Q}^{\mathrm{M}(p)}_{0,4}
.

Example 3.10

(
g=1
,
n=1
)
.

By the weaker constraint
|\mathbf{d}|\leq 3g-3+n-k
in the sum in equation (
3.8
),
an extra contribution to the sum in equation (
3.8
) is found for
k=0
.
Namely for this contribution, there are no multicurves in
S_{1,1}
, and the factor
\prod_{i=1}^{k}(2d_{i}+1)!\zeta(2d_{i}+2)
is absent.
The corresponding stable graph
\Gamma_{1,1}^{(0)}
has one vertex labeled by
g=1
and one half-edge as described in Figure
5
(top).

Figure 5:
Top: A stable graph
\Gamma_{1,1}^{(0)}
from
(S_{1,1},\varnothing)
.
Bottom: A stable graph
\Gamma_{0,4}^{(0)}
from
(S_{0,4},\varnothing)
.

The polynomial factor obeys

(\varnothing)_{\Gamma_{1,1}^{(0)}}^{\mathrm{M}(p)}=V^{\mathrm{M}(p)}_{1,1}(0)=\pi^{2}s\big(1-1/p^{2}\big)/12

for this stable graph,
where a deformation parameter
s
is introduced by
\pi^{2}\to\pi^{2}s
.
The contributions to
\mathrm{Vol}\mathcal{Q}^{\mathrm{M}(p)}_{1,1}
from the stable graph
\Gamma_{1,1}^{(0)}
is

\displaystyle\frac{\alpha_{1,1}}{\big|\mathrm{Aut}\big(\Gamma_{1,1}^{(0)}\big)\big|}(\varnothing)_{\Gamma_{1,1}^{(0)}}^{\mathrm{M}(p)}\frac{1}{2!}=\frac{2\pi^{2}s}{3}\left(1-\frac{1}{p^{2}}\right).

Combining the contribution (
3.6
) from the stable graph
\Gamma_{1,1}^{(1)}
, one finds

\displaystyle\mathrm{Vol}\mathcal{Q}^{\mathrm{M}(p)}_{1,1}=\frac{2\pi^{2}s}{3}\left(1-\frac{1}{p^{2}}\right)+\frac{2\pi^{2}}{3}.

This agrees with

\beta_{1,1}V^{\mathrm{M}(p)}_{1,1}\big[\mathsf{f}^{\mathrm{MV}}\big](0)=2^{3}\cdot\pi^{2}\big(s-s/p^{2}+1\big)/12

in
equation (
3.9
)
(see (
C.7
)).

Example 3.11

(
g=0
,
n=4
)
.

We compute an extra contribution of
k=0
which comes
from the weaker constraint
|\mathbf{d}|\leq 3g-3+n-k
in the sum in equation (
3.8
).
The corresponding stable graph
\Gamma_{0,4}^{(0)}
has one vertex labeled by
g=0
and four half-edges as described in Figure
5
(bottom).
The polynomial factor obeys

(\varnothing)_{\Gamma_{0,4}^{(0)}}^{\mathrm{M}(p)}=V^{\mathrm{M}(p)}_{0,4}(0,0,0,0)=2\pi^{2}s\big(1-1/p^{2}\big)

for this stable graph.
The contributions to
\mathrm{Vol}\mathcal{Q}^{\mathrm{M}(p)}_{0,4}
from the stable graph
\Gamma_{0,4}^{(0)}
is

\displaystyle\frac{\alpha_{0,4}}{\big|\mathrm{Aut}\big(\Gamma_{0,4}^{(0)}\big)\big|}(\varnothing)_{\Gamma_{0,4}^{(0)}}^{\mathrm{M}(p)}\frac{1}{2!}=8\pi^{2}s\left(1-\frac{1}{p^{2}}\right).

Combining the contribution (
3.7
) from the stable graph
\Gamma_{0,4}^{(1)}
, one finds

\displaystyle\mathrm{Vol}\mathcal{Q}^{\mathrm{M}(p)}_{0,4}=8\pi^{2}s\left(1-\frac{1}{p^{2}}\right)+2\pi^{2}.

This agrees with

\beta_{0,4}V^{\mathrm{M}(p)}_{0,4}\big[\mathsf{f}^{\mathrm{MV}}\big](0,0,0,0)=2^{2}\cdot\pi^{2}\big(4s-4s/p^{2}+1\big)/2

in
equation (
3.9
)
(see (
C.7
)).

3.3
Generalization to the
\boldsymbol{(2,2p-2)}
minimal superstring

The combinatorial formula in Theorem
3.2
is also generalized to the volume polynomials for the supersymmetric models
which are associated with the BGW tau function
[
3
]
.
We consider the
(2,2p-2)
minimal superstring whose volume polynomials interpolate
the volume polynomials
V^{\mathrm{B}}_{g,n}(L_{1},\dots,L_{n})
for
p=1
and the super Weil–Petersson volumes
V^{\mathrm{SWP}}_{g,n}(L_{1},\dots,L_{n})
for
p=\infty
,
and find the following proposition.

Proposition 3.12
.

Let
\overline{G}_{g,n}
be the set of stable graphs which does not contain any vertices associated to connected bordered Riemann surfaces of
genus zero.
14
14

14

The volume polynomial
V^{\mathrm{SM}(p)}_{0,n}(0,\dots,0)
is zero for any
n
.

The constant term in the twisted volume polynomial

V^{\mathrm{SM}(p)}_{g,n}\big[\mathsf{f}^{\mathrm{MV}}\big]

for the
(2,2p-2)
minimal superstring
is obtained, as a sum over stable graphs
\Gamma\in\overline{G}_{g,n}
, by

\displaystyle V^{\mathrm{SM}(p)}_{g,n}\big[\mathsf{f}^{\mathrm{MV}}\big](0,\dots,0)={}

\displaystyle\sum_{\Gamma\in\overline{G}_{g,n}}\frac{1}{|\mathrm{Aut}\left(\Gamma\right)|}\sum_{|\mathbf{d}|\leq g-1-k}(2d_{1},\dots,2d_{k})_{\Gamma}^{\mathrm{SM}(p)}

\displaystyle\times\prod_{i=1}^{k}(2d_{i}+1)!\zeta(2d_{i}+2),

(3.11)

where
(2d_{1},\dots,2d_{k})_{\Gamma}^{\mathrm{SM}(p)}
is
defined from the twisted volume polynomial

V^{\mathrm{SM}(p)}_{g,n}\big[\mathsf{f}^{\mathrm{MV}}\big]

by equation (
3.8
).

Remark 3.13
.

If the degree constraint
|\mathbf{d}|\leq g-1-k
in equation (
3.11
) is replaced by the stronger condition
|\mathbf{d}|=g-1-k
,
then the twisted volume
V^{\mathrm{SM}(p)}_{g,n}\big[\mathsf{f}^{\mathrm{MV}}\big](0,\dots,0)
for an odd positive integer
p

reduces to
V^{\mathrm{B}}_{g,n}\big[\mathsf{f}^{\mathrm{MV}}\big](0,\dots,0)
.

Here we show combinatorial computations for
(g,n)=(2,1)
and
(3,1)
.

Example 3.14

(
g=2
,
n=1
)
.

One finds three stable graphs which correspond to decompositions (
2.36
) of
S_{2,1}
without
g=0
components as described in Figure
6
.

Figure 6:
Stable graphs
\Gamma_{2,1}^{(0)},\Gamma_{2,1}^{(1)},\Gamma_{2,1}^{(2)}\in\overline{G}_{2,1}
obtained from the
decompositions of
S_{2,1}
which do not involve
S_{0,n}
’s.

For the graph
\Gamma_{2,1}^{(0)}
, a contribution
in equation (
3.11
) is

\displaystyle\mathrm{vol}^{(0)}_{2,1}=\frac{1}{\big|\mathrm{Aut}\big(\Gamma_{2,1}^{(0)}\big)\big|}(\varnothing)_{\Gamma^{(0)}_{2,1}}^{\mathrm{SM}(p)}=\frac{9\pi^{2}s}{64}\left(1-\frac{1}{p^{2}}\right),\qquad\text{where}\quad(\varnothing)_{\Gamma^{(0)}_{2,1}}^{\mathrm{SM}(p)}=V^{\mathrm{SM}(p)}_{2,1}(0),

and a deformation parameter
s
is introduced by
\pi^{2}\to\pi^{2}s
.
For the graph
\Gamma_{2,1}^{(1)}
, a contribution
in equation (
3.11
) is

\displaystyle\mathrm{vol}^{(1)}_{2,1}=\frac{1}{\big|\mathrm{Aut}\big(\Gamma_{2,1}^{(1)}\big)\big|}(0)_{\Gamma^{(1)}_{2,1}}^{\mathrm{SM}(p)}\zeta(2)=\frac{\pi^{2}}{384},

where
(0)_{\Gamma^{(1)}_{2,1}}^{\mathrm{SM}(p)}
is
the coefficient of
\ell^{0}
in

V^{\mathrm{SM}(p)}_{1,2}(\ell,0)\cdot V^{\mathrm{SM}(p)}_{1,1}(\ell)=1/64
.
For the graph
\Gamma_{2,1}^{(2)}
, a contribution
in equation (
3.11
) is

\displaystyle\mathrm{vol}^{(2)}_{2,1}=\frac{1}{\big|\mathrm{Aut}\big(\Gamma_{2,1}^{(2)}\big)\big|}(0)_{\Gamma^{(2)}_{2,1}}^{\mathrm{SM}(p)}\zeta(2)=\frac{\pi^{2}}{48},

where
(0)_{\Gamma^{(2)}_{2,1}}^{\mathrm{SM}(p)}
is
the coefficient of
\ell^{0}
in

V^{\mathrm{SM}(p)}_{1,3}(\ell,\ell,0)=1/4
, and
\mathrm{Aut}\big(\Gamma_{2,1}^{(2)}\big)=\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}
.
Summing these three contributions, one obtains

\displaystyle V^{\mathrm{SM}(p)}_{2,1}\big[\mathsf{f}^{\mathrm{MV}}\big](0)=\mathrm{vol}^{(0)}_{2,1}+\mathrm{vol}^{(1)}_{2,1}+\mathrm{vol}^{(2)}_{2,1}=\frac{\pi^{2}}{128}\left(18s\left(1-\frac{1}{p^{2}}\right)+3\right),

which agrees with the constant term of the twisted volume polynomial
V^{\mathrm{SM}(p)}_{2,1}\big[\mathsf{f}^{\mathrm{MV}}\big]

(see (
C.10
)).

Example 3.15

(
g=3
,
n=1
)
.

One finds ten multicurves which decompose
S_{3,1}
without
g=0
components as described in Figure
7
.

Figure 7:
Ten stable graphs obtained from the decompositions of
S_{3,1}
which do not involve
S_{0,n}
’s.

Summing these ten contributions, one obtains

\displaystyle V^{\mathrm{SM}(p)}_{3,1}\big[\mathsf{f}^{\mathrm{MV}}\big](0)

\displaystyle\qquad=V^{\mathrm{SM}(p)}_{3,1}(0)+\left(V^{\mathrm{SM}(p)}_{2,1}(x)\cdot V^{\mathrm{SM}(p)}_{1,2}(x,0)+V^{\mathrm{SM}(p)}_{1,1}(x)\cdot V^{\mathrm{SM}(p)}_{2,2}(x,0)\right.

\displaystyle\left.\qquad\quad{}+\frac{1}{2}V^{\mathrm{SM}(p)}_{2,3}(x,x,0)\right)\bigg|_{x^{0}}\zeta(2)+\left(V^{\mathrm{SM}(p)}_{2,1}(x)\cdot V^{\mathrm{SM}(p)}_{1,2}(x,0)+V^{\mathrm{SM}(p)}_{1,1}(x)\cdot V^{\mathrm{SM}(p)}_{2,2}(x,0)\!\right.

\displaystyle\left.\qquad\quad{}+\frac{1}{2}V^{\mathrm{SM}(p)}_{2,3}(x,x,0)\right)\bigg|_{x^{2}}3!\zeta(4)+\biggl(V^{\mathrm{SM}(p)}_{1,1}(x)\cdot V^{\mathrm{SM}(p)}_{1,2}(x,y)\cdot V^{\mathrm{SM}(p)}_{1,2}(y,0)

\displaystyle\qquad\quad{}+\frac{1}{2}V^{\mathrm{SM}(p)}_{1,1}(x)V^{\mathrm{SM}(p)}_{1,3}(x,y,0)\cdot V^{\mathrm{SM}(p)}_{1,1}(y)

\displaystyle\qquad\quad{}+\frac{1}{2}V^{\mathrm{SM}(p)}_{1,2}(x,y)V^{\mathrm{SM}(p)}_{1,3}(x,y,0)+\frac{1}{2}V^{\mathrm{SM}(p)}_{1,3}(x,y,y)\cdot V^{\mathrm{SM}(p)}_{1,2}(x,0)

\displaystyle\qquad\quad{}+\frac{1}{2}V^{\mathrm{SM}(p)}_{1,1}(x)\cdot V^{\mathrm{SM}(p)}_{1,4}(x,y,y,0)+\frac{1}{2^{3}}V^{\mathrm{SM}(p)}_{1,5}(x,x,y,y,0)\biggr)\bigg|_{x^{0}y^{0}}\zeta(2)^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad=\frac{\pi^{4}}{1024}\left(\left(1-\frac{1}{p^{2}}\right)\left(6s^{2}\left(227-\frac{27}{p^{2}}\right)+255s\right)+23\right),

where
|_{x^{k}y^{l}}
picks up coefficients of
x^{k}y^{l}
in the polynomials.
This result agrees with the constant term of the twisted volume polynomial
V^{\mathrm{SM}(p)}_{3,1}\big[\mathsf{f}^{\mathrm{MV}}\big]

(see (
C.10
)).

4
CEO topological recursion

In this section, we apply the CEO topological recursion
[
16
,
37
]
, which is a Laplace dual formulation of the ABO topological recursion,
to the 2D gravity models in Table
1
.
In particular, in Section
4.4
we provide a direct proof of
Theorem
4.15
on the Laplace dual relation between
the ABO topological recursion with the Masur–Veech type twist
and the CEO topological recursion with the Masur–Veech type twist for the
(2,p)
minimal string and the
(2,2p-2)
minimal superstring.

4.1
Formulation

We briefly review the formulation of the CEO topological recursion, and
describe a Laplace dual relation with the ABO topological recursion.

Definition 4.1
.

A
spectral curve

\mathcal{C}=(\Sigma;\mathsf{x},\mathsf{y},B)
consists of a Riemann surface
\Sigma
, meromorphic functions
\mathsf{x},\mathsf{y}\colon\Sigma\to{\mathbb{C}}
such that the zeros of
{\rm d}\mathsf{x}

are different from the zeros of
{\rm d}\mathsf{y}
, and a bidifferential
B
on

\Sigma^{\otimes 2}
.

Definition 4.2

(CEO topological recursion
[
37
]
)
.

For a spectral curve
\mathcal{C}=(\Sigma;\mathsf{x},\mathsf{y},B)
such that the zeros of
{\rm d}\mathsf{x}
are simple,
the meromorphic multidifferentials
\omega_{g,n}(z_{1},\dots,z_{n})
,

z_{i}\in\Sigma
, labeled by
g\geq 0
,
n\geq 1
satisfying
2g-2+n\geq 0
,
are defined by the CEO topological recursion

\displaystyle\omega_{g,n}(z_{1},\dots,z_{n})={}

\displaystyle\sum_{\alpha\in\mathrm{Ram}}\mathop{\mathrm{Res}}\limits_{w=\alpha}K(z_{1},w)\mathcal{R}\omega_{g,n}(w,z_{K}),

where
K=\{2,\dots,n\}
,

\mathrm{Ram}
is the set of zeros of
{\rm d}\mathsf{x}
, and

K(z,w)
is the
recursion kernel
defined by

\displaystyle K(z,w)=\frac{\int_{w}^{\overline{w}}B(\cdot,z)}{2\left(\mathsf{y}(w){\rm d}\mathsf{x}(w)-\mathsf{y}(\overline{w}){\rm d}\mathsf{x}(\overline{w})\right)},

(4.1)

and

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}\omega_{g,n}(w,z_{K})=\omega_{g-1,n+1}(w,\overline{w},z_{K})+\mathop{\sum_{h+h^{\prime}=g}}\limits_{J\cup J^{\prime}=K}^{\mathrm{no(0,1)}}\omega_{h,1+|J|}(w,z_{J})\omega_{h^{\prime},1+|J^{\prime}|}(\overline{w},z_{J^{\prime}}).

(4.2)

Here
\overline{w}
is the conjugate point of
w
near
\alpha\in\mathrm{Ram}

such that
\overline{w}\neq w
and
x(\overline{w})=x(w)
.
The sum in equation (
4.2
) does not include
(g,n)=(0,1)
part and
contains the bidifferential

\displaystyle\omega_{0,2}(z_{1},z_{2})=B(z_{1},z_{2}),

and for
J=\{i_{1},\dots,i_{|J|}\}\subseteq K
,

z_{J}=\{z_{i_{1}},\dots,z_{i_{|J|}}\}
and

z_{J^{\prime}}=\{z_{i_{|J|+1}},\dots,z_{i_{n-1}}\}
.

In this paper, we focus on a class of spectral curves
\mathcal{C}=\big({\mathbb{P}}^{1};\mathsf{x},\mathsf{y},B\big)
with coordinate functions

\displaystyle\mathsf{x}=\mathsf{x}(z)=\frac{1}{2}z^{2},\qquad\mathsf{y}=\mathsf{y}(z),\qquad z\in{\mathbb{P}}^{1},

(4.3)

which
has a simple ramification point only at
z=0
(the solution to
{\rm d}\mathsf{x}(z)=0
)
and so
{\mathrm{Ram}=\{0\}}
.

\mathsf{y}(z)\in{\mathbb{C}}
is a meromorphic function of
z
,
and
\mathcal{C}
admits the global Galois covering by the conjugation
\overline{z}=-z
.
The bidifferential
B(z_{1},z_{2})
has a double pole at the diagonal locus
z_{1}=z_{2}
such that

\displaystyle B(z_{1},z_{2})=\frac{{\rm d}z_{1}\otimes{\rm d}z_{2}}{(z_{1}-z_{2})^{2}},\qquad z_{1},z_{2}\in{\mathbb{P}}^{1}.

(4.4)

For this class of spectral curves,
the recursion kernel for the CEO topological recursion is

\displaystyle K(z,w)=\frac{\int_{w}^{-w}B(\cdot,z)}{2w(\mathsf{y}(w)-\mathsf{y}(-w)){\rm d}w}=\frac{(-1){\rm d}z}{\big(z^{2}-w^{2}\big)(\mathsf{y}(w)-\mathsf{y}(-w)){\rm d}w}.

And we introduce correlation functions.

Definition 4.3
.

The correlation functions
W_{g,n}(z_{1},\dots,z_{n})
for
2g-2+n\geq 0
are

\displaystyle W_{g,n}(z_{1},\dots,z_{n})\otimes_{i=1}^{n}{\rm d}z_{i}=\omega_{g,n}(z_{1},\dots,z_{n}).

From the general argument in
[
9
,
10
]
,
one finds the following claim.

Theorem 4.4

(Laplace transform of the volume polynomial
[
9
,
10
]
)
.

For the physical
2
D gravity models in Table
2
,
the correlation functions
W_{g,n}
for
2g-2+n>0
are related to the volume polynomials
V_{g,n}
in Definition
2.1
by Laplace transform

\displaystyle W_{g,n}(z_{1},\dots,z_{n})=\mathcal{L}\{V_{g,n}\}(z_{1},\dots,z_{n}),

(4.5)

where the operator
\mathcal{L}
acts on a function
f(z_{1},\dots,z_{n})
as

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}\{f\}(z_{1},\dots,z_{n}):=\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n}}f(L_{1},\dots,L_{n})\mathrm{e}^{-\sum_{i=1}^{n}z_{i}L_{i}}\prod_{i=1}^{n}L_{i}{\rm d}L_{i}.

(4.6)

In particular, for the expansion (
2.3
), we find

\displaystyle W_{g,n}(z_{1},\dots,z_{n})=\sum_{a_{1},\dots,a_{n}\geq 0}F^{(g)}_{a_{1},\dots,a_{n}}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\frac{1}{z_{i}^{2a_{i}+2}}.

(4.7)

Remark 4.5
.

In Appendix
B
,
based on the Laplace dual relation (
4.5
)
we derive the kernel functions (
2.19
) and (
2.31
), which are not known before in the literature,
of the
(2,p)
minimal string and the
(2,2p-2)
minimal superstring from
the spectral curves in equations (
4.25
)
and (
4.40
).
Note that the “local” initial data
(\mathsf{A}^{a_{1}}_{a_{2},a_{3}},\mathsf{B}^{a_{1}}_{a_{2},a_{3}},\mathsf{C}^{a_{1}}_{a_{2},a_{3}},\mathsf{D}^{a_{1}})
in equations (
2.6
) and (
2.7
) are found in
[
9
,
84
]
.

In Sections
4.2
and
4.3
,
we will discuss the correlation functions
W_{g,n}
defined by the CEO topological recursion for the physical 2D gravity models
and show some computational results of
W_{g,n}
explicitly for our use in other sections and our checks of consistency of computations.

4.2
Bosonic models

Here we consider the bosonic models in Table
1
.

4.2.1
Airy and KdV

For the Airy spectral curve
\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{A}}=\big({\mathbb{P}}^{1};\mathsf{x},\mathsf{y}^{\mathrm{A}},B\big)
with
coordinate functions

\displaystyle\mathsf{x}(z)=\frac{1}{2}z^{2},\qquad\mathsf{y}^{\mathrm{A}}(z)=z,

(4.8)

and the bidifferential
B
in equation (
4.4
),
the CEO topological recursion defines the meromorphic multidifferentials

\displaystyle\omega_{g,n}^{\mathrm{A}}(z_{1},\dots,z_{n})=\mathop{\mathrm{Res}}\limits_{w=0}K^{\mathrm{A}}(z_{1},w)\mathcal{R}\omega_{g,n}^{\mathrm{A}}(w,z_{K})=\sum_{a_{1},\dots,a_{n}\geq 0}F^{\mathrm{A}(g)}_{a_{1},\dots,a_{n}}\otimes_{i=1}^{n}\frac{{\rm d}z_{i}}{z_{i}^{2a_{i}+2}},

(4.9)

which give the Airy volume coefficients
F^{\mathrm{A}(g)}_{a_{1},\dots,a_{n}}
in equation (
2.18
), where

\displaystyle K^{\mathrm{A}}(z_{1},w)=\frac{(-1){\rm d}z_{1}}{2\left(z_{1}^{2}-w^{2}\right)w{\rm d}w},

(4.10)

is the recursion kernel for
\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{A}}
.
From equation (
4.9
), some of the correlation functions are

\displaystyle W_{0,3}^{\mathrm{A}}(z_{1},z_{2},z_{3})=\prod_{i=1}^{3}\frac{1}{z_{i}^{2}},\qquad W_{1,1}^{\mathrm{A}}(z_{1})=\frac{1}{8z_{1}^{4}},

\displaystyle W_{0,4}^{\mathrm{A}}(z_{1},\dots,z_{4})=\left(\sum_{i=1}^{4}\frac{3}{z_{i}^{2}}\right)\prod_{i=1}^{4}\frac{1}{z_{i}^{2}},\qquad W_{1,2}^{\mathrm{A}}(z_{1},z_{2})=\left(\sum_{i=1}^{2}\frac{5}{8z_{i}^{4}}+\frac{3}{8z_{1}^{2}z_{2}^{2}}\right)\prod_{i=1}^{2}\frac{1}{z_{i}^{2}},

\displaystyle W_{0,5}^{\mathrm{A}}(z_{1},\dots,z_{5})=\Bigg(\sum_{i=1}^{5}\frac{15}{z_{i}^{4}}+\sum_{1\leq i<j\leq 5}\frac{18}{z_{i}^{2}z_{j}^{2}}\Bigg)\prod_{i=1}^{5}\frac{1}{z_{i}^{2}},

\displaystyle W_{1,3}^{\mathrm{A}}(z_{1},z_{2},z_{3})=\Bigg(\sum_{i=1}^{3}\frac{35}{8z_{i}^{6}}+\sum_{1\leq i,j\leq 3}\frac{15}{4z_{i}^{2}z_{j}^{4}}+\frac{9}{4z_{1}^{2}z_{2}^{2}z_{3}^{2}}\Bigg)\prod_{i=1}^{3}\frac{1}{z_{i}^{2}},\qquad W_{2,1}^{\mathrm{A}}(z_{1})=\frac{105}{128z_{1}^{10}}.

We now introduce the KdV spectral curve which deforms
the Airy spectral curve.

Definition 4.6

(KdV spectral curve)
.

The KdV spectral curve
\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{KdV}}=\big({\mathbb{P}}^{1};\mathsf{x},\mathsf{y}^{\mathrm{KdV}},B\big)
is defined by

\displaystyle\mathsf{x}(z)=\frac{1}{2}z^{2},\qquad\mathsf{y}^{\mathrm{KdV}}(z)=z+\sum_{a\geq 2}\mathsf{u}_{a}z^{a},

(4.11)

and the bidifferential
B
in equation (
4.4
),
where
\mathsf{u}_{a}
are time variables.
The KdV spectral curve
\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{KdV}}
yields the Airy spectral curve
\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{A}}
when
\mathsf{u}_{a}=0
.

The correlation functions

\displaystyle W_{g,n}^{\mathrm{KdV}}(z_{1},\dots,z_{n})=\sum_{a_{1},\dots,a_{n}\geq 0}F^{\mathrm{KdV}(g)}_{a_{1},\dots,a_{n}}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\frac{1}{z_{i}^{2a_{i}+2}},

(4.12)

obtained from the CEO topological recursion for
\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{KdV}}

obey the following proposition
[
31
]
.

Proposition 4.7

(
[
31
]
)
.

The coefficients
F^{\mathrm{KdV}(g)}_{a_{1},\dots,a_{n}}
in equation (
4.12
) are written in terms of
the volume coefficients
F^{\mathrm{A}(g)}_{a_{1},\dots,a_{n}}
in equation (
4.9
)

(
or equation (
2.18
)
)
as

\displaystyle F^{\mathrm{KdV}(g)}_{a_{1},\dots,a_{n}}=\sum_{m\geq 0}\frac{(-1)^{m}}{m!}\sum_{b_{1},\dots,b_{m}\geq 2}\Bigg(\prod_{j=1}^{m}\frac{\mathsf{u}_{2b_{j}-1}}{2b_{j}+1}\Bigg)F^{\mathrm{A}(g)}_{a_{1},\dots,a_{n},b_{1},\dots,b_{m}},

(4.13)

where the sum over
m
and
b_{j}
satisfies

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}a_{i}=3g-3+n+m-\sum_{j=1}^{m}b_{j},

(4.14)

by the homogeneity condition (
2.17
) for the volume coefficients.

Proof.

The CEO topological recursion for the KdV spectral curve
\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{KdV}}
gives

\displaystyle\omega_{g,n}^{\mathrm{KdV}}(z_{1},\dots,z_{n})={}

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{Res}}\limits_{w=0}\frac{(-1){\rm d}z_{1}}{\big(z_{1}^{2}-w^{2}\big)\big(\mathsf{y}^{\mathrm{KdV}}(w)-\mathsf{y}^{\mathrm{KdV}}(-w)\big){\rm d}w}\mathcal{R}\omega_{g,n}^{\mathrm{KdV}}(w,z_{K})

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{Res}}\limits_{w=0}K^{\mathrm{A}}(z_{1},w)Y^{\mathrm{KdV}}(w)\mathcal{R}\omega_{g,n}^{\mathrm{KdV}}(w,z_{K}),

(4.15)

where
K^{\mathrm{A}}(z_{1},w)
is the recursion kernel (
4.10
) for the Airy spectral curve
\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{A}}
, and

\displaystyle Y^{\mathrm{KdV}}(w)=\frac{2w}{\mathsf{y}^{\mathrm{KdV}}(w)-\mathsf{y}^{\mathrm{KdV}}(-w)}=\sum_{m\geq 0}(-1)^{m}\Bigg(\sum_{b\geq 2}\mathsf{u}_{2b-1}w^{2b-2}\Bigg)^{m}=1+\mathcal{O}\big(w^{2}\big)

is a regular even function of
w
at
w=0
.

A key formula to prove the proposition is
[
31
]
,

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{Res}}\limits_{w_{1}=0}\frac{(-1)Y(w_{1})R(w_{1})}{2\big(w_{0}^{2}-w_{1}^{2}\big)w_{1}}

\displaystyle=\mathop{\mathrm{Res}}\limits_{w_{1}=0}\big(\mathop{\mathrm{Res}}\limits_{w_{2}=w_{1}}+\mathop{\mathrm{Res}}\limits_{w_{2}=-w_{1}}\big)\frac{(-1){\rm d}w_{2}}{2\big(w_{0}^{2}-w_{2}^{2}\big)w_{2}}\frac{w_{2}^{2}Y(w_{2})R(w_{1})}{\big(w_{2}^{2}-w_{1}^{2}\big)w_{1}}

\displaystyle=\mathop{\mathrm{Res}}\limits_{w_{2}=0}\frac{w_{2}^{2}Y(w_{2}){\rm d}w_{2}}{\big(w_{0}^{2}-w_{2}^{2}\big)w_{2}}\mathop{\mathrm{Res}}\limits_{w_{1}=0}\frac{(-1)R(w_{1})}{2\big(w_{2}^{2}-w_{1}^{2}\big)w_{1}},

(4.16)

where
Y(w)
is a regular function of
w
at
w=0
and

R(w)
is a meromorphic differential of
w
.
Using this formula recursively, we rewrite equation (
4.15
) as

\displaystyle\omega_{g,n}^{\mathrm{KdV}}(z_{1},\dots,z_{n})={}

\displaystyle\sum_{m\geq 0}(-1)^{m}\mathop{\mathrm{Res}}\limits_{w_{m}=0}\frac{\mathsf{u}(w_{m}){\rm d}z_{1}}{\big(z_{1}^{2}-w_{m}^{2}\big)w_{m}}\mathop{\mathrm{Res}}\limits_{w_{m-1}=0}\frac{\mathsf{u}(w_{m-1}){\rm d}w_{m}}{\big(w_{m}^{2}-w_{m-1}^{2}\big)w_{m-1}}\cdots

\displaystyle\times\mathop{\mathrm{Res}}\limits_{w_{1}=0}\frac{\mathsf{u}(w_{1}){\rm d}w_{2}}{\big(w_{2}^{2}-w_{1}^{2}\big)w_{1}}\mathop{\mathrm{Res}}\limits_{w=0}K^{\mathrm{A}}(w_{1},w)\mathcal{R}\omega_{g,n}^{\mathrm{KdV}}(w,z_{K}),

(4.17)

where

\displaystyle\mathsf{u}(w)=\sum_{b\geq 2}\mathsf{u}_{2b-1}w^{2b},

and the residue operators

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{Res}}\limits_{w_{j}=0}\frac{\mathsf{u}(w_{j}){\rm d}w}{\big(w^{2}-w_{j}^{2}\big)w_{j}},

acting on meromorphic differentials of
w_{j}
are referred to as the
Airy dilaton leaves
.
Therefore, the KdV meromorphic multidifferentials
\omega_{g,n}^{\mathrm{KdV}}

are regarded as
the Airy meromorphic multidifferentials
\omega_{g,n}^{\mathrm{A}}

decorated by the Airy dilaton leaves.

To find such decorations,
we compare a meromorphic even differential

R(w)=\sum_{a}R_{a}{\rm d}w/w^{2a}

of
w

decorated by an Airy dilaton leaf,

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{Res}}\limits_{w_{j}=0}\frac{\mathsf{u}(w_{j}){\rm d}wR(w_{j})}{\big(w^{2}-w_{j}^{2}\big)w_{j}}=\sum_{d\geq 0}\frac{{\rm d}w}{w^{2d+2}}\sum_{b\geq 2}\mathsf{u}_{2b-1}R_{d+b},

(4.18)

with the
(g,n)=(0,2)
part in the CEO topological recursion for
\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{A}}
,

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{Res}}\limits_{w_{j}=0}K^{\mathrm{A}}(w,w_{j})\left(\frac{B(w_{j},v)}{dv}R(-w_{j})+\frac{B(-w_{j},v)}{dv}R(w_{j})\right)

\displaystyle\qquad=\mathop{\mathrm{Res}}\limits_{w_{j}=0}\frac{R(w_{j}){\rm d}w}{2\big(w^{2}-w_{j}^{2}\big)w_{j}}\left(\frac{1}{(v-w_{j})^{2}}+\frac{1}{(v+w_{j})^{2}}\right)=\sum_{d\geq 0}\frac{{\rm d}w}{w^{2d+2}}\sum_{b\geq 0}\frac{2b+1}{v^{2b+2}}R_{d+b}.

(4.19)

By this comparison, it is found that the decoration (
4.18
) of the Airy dilaton leaf is translated into a decorated
(g,n)=(0,2)
part in the CEO topological recursion for
\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{A}}
.
In this translation, the Airy meromorphic differential acquires an extra marked point
v=1
,
and we find the sum in equation (
4.18
) by replacing the sum in equation (
4.19
) by a weighted sum such that

\displaystyle\sum_{b\geq 0}\frac{2b+1}{v^{2b+2}}R_{d+b}\;\to\;\sum_{b\geq 0}\frac{2b+1}{v^{2b+2}}\mathsf{wt}_{b}(\mathsf{u})R_{d+b}=\sum_{b\geq 2}\mathsf{u}_{2b-1}R_{d+b},

with weight factors

\displaystyle\mathsf{wt}_{b}(\mathsf{u})=\begin{cases}0&\textrm{for}\ b=0,1,\\
\dfrac{\mathsf{u}_{2b-1}}{2b+1}&\textrm{for}\ b\geq 2.\end{cases}

(4.20)

In the following, we refer to this translation for the Airy dilaton leaf which decorates the
{(g,n)=(0,2)}
part in the multidifferential
as the
Airy translation
. In the following discussions,
we adopt the Airy translation to prove the formula (
4.13
)
by the mathematical induction on
2g-2+n\geq 1
.

For
(g,n)=(0,3)
, equation (
4.17
) gives

\displaystyle\omega_{0,3}^{\mathrm{KdV}}(z_{1},z_{2},z_{3})={}

\displaystyle\sum_{m\geq 0}(-1)^{m}\mathop{\mathrm{Res}}\limits_{w_{m}=0}\frac{\mathsf{u}(w_{m}){\rm d}z_{1}}{\big(z_{1}^{2}-w_{m}^{2}\big)w_{m}}\cdots\mathop{\mathrm{Res}}\limits_{w_{1}=0}\frac{\mathsf{u}(w_{1}){\rm d}w_{2}}{\big(w_{2}^{2}-w_{1}^{2}\big)w_{1}}\omega_{0,3}^{\mathrm{A}}(w_{1},z_{2},z_{3})

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\omega_{0,3}^{\mathrm{A}}(z_{1},z_{2},z_{3}),

where the Airy dilaton leaves do not contribute in this case.
For
(g,n)=(1,1)
, equation (
4.17
) gives

\displaystyle\omega_{1,1}^{\mathrm{KdV}}(z_{1})

\displaystyle=\sum_{m\geq 0}(-1)^{m}\mathop{\mathrm{Res}}\limits_{w_{m}=0}\frac{\mathsf{u}(w_{m}){\rm d}z_{1}}{\big(z_{1}^{2}-w_{m}^{2}\big)w_{m}}\cdots\mathop{\mathrm{Res}}\limits_{w_{1}=0}\frac{\mathsf{u}(w_{1}){\rm d}w_{2}}{\big(w_{2}^{2}-w_{1}^{2}\big)w_{1}}\omega_{1,1}^{\mathrm{A}}(w_{1})

\displaystyle=\omega_{1,1}^{\mathrm{A}}(z_{1})+\sum_{a_{1}\geq 0,b_{1}\geq 2}\frac{\mathsf{u}_{2b_{1}-1}}{2b_{1}+1}F^{\mathrm{A}(1)}_{a_{1},b_{1}}\frac{{\rm d}z_{1}}{z_{1}^{2a_{1}+2}},

where only one Airy dilaton leaf contributes and the Airy translation is adopted.
Thus, the equations (
4.13
) for
(g,n)=(0,3),(1,1)
are obtained.

Next, under the assumption that the formula (
4.13
) is correct for any
(g,n)
with

2g-2+n\leq k
,
we consider equation (
4.17
) for
(g,n)
with
2g-2+n=k+1
.
The coefficients of

\otimes_{i=2}^{n}{\rm d}z_{i}/z_{i}^{2a_{i}+2}

in the factor
\mathcal{R}\omega_{g,n}^{\mathrm{KdV}}(w,z_{K})
in
equation (
4.17
) are rewritten
under this assumption as

\displaystyle\sum_{a,b\geq 0}\frac{(-1){\rm d}w\otimes{\rm d}w}{w^{2a+2b+4}}\Bigg(F^{\mathrm{KdV}(g-1)}_{a,b,a_{2},\dots,a_{n}}+\mathop{\sum_{h+h^{\prime}=g}^{\prime}}\limits_{J\subseteq K}F^{\mathrm{KdV}(h)}_{a,a_{i_{1}},\dots,a_{i_{|J|}}}F^{\mathrm{KdV}(h^{\prime})}_{b,a_{i_{|J|+1}},\dots,a_{i_{n-1}}}\Bigg)

\displaystyle\qquad=\sum_{a,b\geq 0}\frac{(-1){\rm d}w\otimes{\rm d}w}{w^{2a+2b+4}}\sum_{m\geq 0}\frac{(-1)^{m}}{m!}\sum_{b_{1},\dots,b_{m}\geq 2}\Bigg(\prod_{j=1}^{m}\mathsf{wt}_{b_{j}}(\mathsf{u})\Bigg)\Bigg(F^{\mathrm{A}(g-1)}_{a,b,a_{2},\dots,a_{n},b_{1},\dots,b_{m}}

\displaystyle\qquad\quad+\mathop{\sum_{h+h^{\prime}=g}^{\prime}}\limits_{J\subseteq K}\sum_{\ell=0}^{m}\binom{m}{\ell}F^{\mathrm{A}(h)}_{a,a_{i_{1}},\dots,a_{i_{|J|}},b_{1},\dots,b_{\ell}}F^{\mathrm{A}(h^{\prime})}_{b,a_{i_{|J|+1}},\dots,a_{i_{n-1}},b_{\ell+1},\dots,b_{m}}\Bigg),

(4.21)

where the sum
\sum_{h+h^{\prime}=g}^{\prime}
does not include

(h,J)=(0,\varnothing)
,
(g,K)
.
The symmetric factor
m!
for the insertion of
m
Airy dilaton leaves arises, since extra marked points at
v=1
are indistinguishable.

Plugging equation (
4.21
) into the right-hand side of equation (
4.17
), we see that the Airy dilaton leaves in equation (
4.17
) compensate
the
(h,h^{\prime},J)=(0,g,\varnothing)
,
(g,0,K)
parts in
equation (
4.21
) by Airy translations.
As a result, we obtain

\displaystyle\omega_{g,n}^{\mathrm{KdV}}(z_{1},\dots,z_{n})

\displaystyle\qquad=\sum_{a_{1},\dots,a_{n}\geq 0}\otimes_{i=1}^{n}\frac{{\rm d}z_{i}}{z_{i}^{2a_{i}+2}}\sum_{m\geq 0}\frac{(-1)^{m}}{m!}\sum_{b_{1},\dots,b_{m}\geq 2}\Bigg(\prod_{j=1}^{m}\mathsf{wt}_{b_{j}}(\mathsf{u})\Bigg)

\displaystyle\ \qquad\quad{}\times\Bigg(F^{\mathrm{A}(g-1)}_{a,b,a_{2},\dots,a_{n},b_{1},\dots,b_{m}}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}h+h^{\prime}=g\\
J\subseteq K\\
0\leq\ell\leq m\end{subarray}}^{\prime\prime}\binom{m}{\ell}F^{\mathrm{A}(h)}_{a,a_{i_{1}},\dots,a_{i_{|J|}},b_{1},\dots,b_{\ell}}F^{\mathrm{A}(h^{\prime})}_{b,a_{i_{|J|+1}},\dots,a_{i_{n-1}},b_{\ell+1},\dots,b_{m}}\Bigg)

\displaystyle\qquad=\sum_{a_{1},\dots,a_{n}\geq 0}\otimes_{i=1}^{n}\frac{{\rm d}z_{i}}{z_{i}^{2a_{i}+2}}\sum_{m\geq 0}\frac{(-1)^{m}}{m!}\sum_{b_{1},\dots,b_{m}\geq 2}\Bigg(\prod_{j=1}^{m}\frac{\mathsf{u}_{2b_{j}-1}}{2b_{j}+1}\Bigg)F^{\mathrm{A}(g)}_{a_{1},\dots,a_{n},b_{1},\dots,b_{m}},

where the sum
\sum_{h+h^{\prime}=g}^{\prime\prime}
does not include

(h,J,\ell)=(0,\varnothing,0)
,
(g,K,m)
.
Thus, the induction is completed and the claim is proved.
∎

4.2.2
Weil–Petersson volumes

For the Weil–Petersson spectral curve
\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{WP}}=\big({\mathbb{P}}^{1};\mathsf{x},\mathsf{y}^{\mathrm{WP}},B\big)

defined by
[
38
]
,

\displaystyle\mathsf{x}(z)=\frac{1}{2}z^{2},\qquad\mathsf{y}^{\mathrm{WP}}(z)=\frac{1}{2\pi}\sin(2\pi z)=\sum_{a\geq 1}\frac{\bigl(-2\pi^{2}\bigr)^{a-1}}{(2a-1)!!(a-1)!}z^{2a-1},

(4.22)

and the bidifferential
B
in equation (
4.4
),
the CEO topological recursion computes
the Weil–Petersson volume coefficients (
2.14
),
which give the Weil–Petersson volumes
V_{g,n}^{\mathrm{WP}}
in equation (
2.13
), by

\displaystyle W_{g,n}^{\mathrm{WP}}(z_{1},\dots,z_{n})=\sum_{a_{1},\dots,a_{n}\geq 0}F^{\mathrm{WP}(g)}_{a_{1},\dots,a_{n}}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\frac{1}{z_{i}^{2a_{i}+2}}.

(4.23)

The coordinate function
\mathsf{y}^{\mathrm{WP}}(z)
in equation (
4.22
) is
found from a specialization
15
15

15

This specialization is also found in the physics literatures
[
29
,
81
]
.

of the coordinate function
\mathsf{y}^{\mathrm{KdV}}(z)
in equation (
4.11
) of the KdV spectral curve as

\displaystyle\mathsf{u}_{2a}=0,\qquad\mathsf{u}_{2a+1}=\frac{\bigl(-2\pi^{2}\bigr)^{a}}{(2a+1)!!a!},

(4.24)

and Proposition
4.7
implies a formula

\displaystyle F^{\mathrm{WP}(g)}_{a_{1},\dots,a_{n}}=\sum_{m\geq 0}\frac{(-1)^{m}}{m!}\sum_{b_{1},\dots,b_{m}\geq 2}\left(\prod_{j=1}^{m}\frac{\bigl(-2\pi^{2}\bigr)^{b_{j}-1}}{(2b_{j}+1)!!(b_{j}-1)!}\right)F^{\mathrm{A}(g)}_{a_{1},\dots,a_{n},b_{1},\dots,b_{m}}

with the condition (
4.14
).

Remark 4.8
.

The deformation parameter
s
in Remark
2.3

is implemented to the spectral curve
\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{WP}}
by
changing
\pi\to\pi\sqrt{s}
for the coordinate function
\mathsf{y}^{\mathrm{KdV}}(z)
in equation (
4.22
).

4.2.3
\boldsymbol{(2,p)}
minimal string

The spectral curve for the
(2,p)
minimal string is found from the disk partition function with the FZZT boundary condition
[
39
,
92
]
.
For an odd positive integer
p
,
the
(2,p)
minimal string spectral curve

\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{M}(p)}=\big({\mathbb{P}}^{1};\mathsf{x},\mathsf{y}^{\mathrm{M}(p)},B\big)
is defined by

\displaystyle\mathsf{x}(z)=\frac{1}{2}z^{2},

\displaystyle\mathsf{y}^{\mathrm{M}(p)}(z)=\frac{(-1)^{\frac{p-1}{2}}}{2\pi}T_{p}\biggl(\frac{2\pi}{p}z\biggr)=\frac{1}{2\pi}\sin\left(\frac{p}{2}\arccos\left(1-\frac{8\pi^{2}z^{2}}{p^{2}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\phantom{\mathsf{y}^{\mathrm{M}(p)}(z)}{}=\sum_{a=1}^{\frac{p+1}{2}}\frac{\bigl(-2\pi^{2}\bigr)^{a-1}}{(2a-1)!!(a-1)!}\prod_{i=1}^{a-1}\left(1-\frac{(2i-1)^{2}}{p^{2}}\right)z^{2a-1},

(4.25)

and the bidifferential
B
in equation (
4.4
),
where
T_{p}(z)
denotes the Chebyshev polynomial of the first kind defined by
T_{p}(\cos\theta)=\cos(p\theta)
.
The minimal string spectral curve
\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{M}(p)}

interpolates the Airy spectral curve
\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{A}}
and
the Weil–Petersson spectral curve
\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{WP}}
by

\displaystyle\mathsf{y}^{\mathrm{M}(1)}(z)=\mathsf{y}^{\mathrm{A}}(z)\ \textrm{in equation~\eqref{sp_curve_kw}},\qquad\mathsf{y}^{\mathrm{M}(\infty)}(z)=\mathsf{y}^{\mathrm{WP}}(z)\ \textrm{in equation~\eqref{sp_curve_mir}}.

The coordinate function
\mathsf{y}^{\mathrm{M}(p)}(z)
in equation (
4.25
) is
found from a specialization of the coordinate function

\mathsf{y}^{\mathrm{KdV}(p)}(z)
in equation (
4.11
) of the KdV spectral curve as

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\mathsf{u}_{2a}=0\ &\textrm{for}\ a\geq 1,\\
\displaystyle\mathsf{u}_{2a+1}=\frac{(-2\pi^{2})^{a}}{(2a+1)!!a!}\prod_{i=1}^{a}\left(1-\frac{(2i-1)^{2}}{p^{2}}\right)\ &\textrm{for}\ 1\leq a\leq\dfrac{p-1}{2},\\
\mathsf{u}_{2a+1}=0\ &\textrm{for}\ a\geq\dfrac{p+1}{2}.\end{cases}

(4.26)

Proposition
4.7
then implies that
the
(2,p)
minimal string volume coefficients in the correlation functions

\displaystyle W_{g,n}^{\mathrm{M}(p)}(z_{1},\dots,z_{n})=\sum_{a_{1},\dots,a_{n}\geq 0}F^{\mathrm{M}(p)(g)}_{a_{1},\dots,a_{n}}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\frac{1}{z_{i}^{2a_{i}+2}},

(4.27)

obey a formula

\displaystyle F^{\mathrm{M}(p)(g)}_{a_{1},\dots,a_{n}}={}

\displaystyle\sum_{m\geq 0}\frac{(-1)^{m}}{m!}\sum_{b_{1},\dots,b_{m}\geq 2}\Bigg(\prod_{j=1}^{m}\frac{\bigl(-2\pi^{2}\bigr)^{b_{j}-1}}{(2b_{j}+1)!!(b_{j}-1)!}\prod_{i=1}^{b_{j}-1}\left(1-\frac{(2i-1)^{2}}{p^{2}}\right)\Bigg)

\displaystyle\times F^{\mathrm{A}(g)}_{a_{1},\dots,a_{n},b_{1},\dots,b_{m}},

(4.28)

with the condition (
4.14
).

4.3
Supersymmetric models

Here we consider the supersymmetric models in Table
1
.

4.3.1
Bessel and BGW

For the Bessel spectral curve
\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{B}}=\big({\mathbb{P}}^{1};\mathsf{x},\mathsf{y}^{\mathrm{B}},B\big)
with coordinate functions
[
30
]
,

\displaystyle\mathsf{x}(z)=\frac{1}{2}z^{2},\qquad\mathsf{y}^{\mathrm{B}}(z)=\frac{1}{z},

(4.29)

and the bidifferential
B
in equation (
4.4
),
the CEO topological recursion defines the meromorphic multidifferentials

\displaystyle\omega_{g,n}^{\mathrm{B}}(z_{1},\dots,z_{n})=\mathop{\mathrm{Res}}\limits_{w=0}K^{\mathrm{B}}(z_{1},w)\mathcal{R}\omega_{g,n}^{\mathrm{B}}(w,z_{K})=\sum_{a_{1},\dots,a_{n}\geq 0}F^{\mathrm{B}(g)}_{a_{1},\dots,a_{n}}\otimes_{i=1}^{n}\frac{{\rm d}z_{i}}{z_{i}^{2a_{i}+2}},

(4.30)

and the Bessel volume coefficients
F^{\mathrm{B}(g)}_{a_{1},\dots,a_{n}}
in
equation (
2.30
) are obtained,
where

\displaystyle K^{\mathrm{B}}(z_{1},w)=\frac{(-1)w{\rm d}z_{1}}{2\left(z_{1}^{2}-w^{2}\right){\rm d}w},

(4.31)

is the recursion kernel for the CEO topological recursion on the Bessel spectral curve
\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{B}}
.
Here, note the relation
\mathsf{y}^{\mathrm{B}}(z)=\partial_{\mathsf{x}}\mathsf{y}^{\mathrm{A}}(z)

with the coordinate function
\mathsf{y}^{\mathrm{A}}(z)
in equation (
4.8
) of
the Airy spectral curve.
From equation (
4.30
), some of the correlation functions are

\displaystyle W_{0,n}^{\mathrm{B}}(z_{1},\dots,z_{n})=0,\qquad W_{1,n}^{\mathrm{B}}(z_{1},\dots,z_{n})=\frac{(n-1)!}{8}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\frac{1}{z_{i}^{2}},

\displaystyle W_{2,1}^{\mathrm{B}}(z_{1})=\frac{9}{128z_{1}^{4}},\qquad W_{2,2}^{\mathrm{B}}(z_{1},z_{2})=\left(\sum_{i=1}^{2}\frac{27}{128z_{i}^{2}}\right)\prod_{i=1}^{2}\frac{1}{z_{i}^{2}},

\displaystyle W_{2,3}^{\mathrm{B}}(z_{1},z_{2},z_{3})=\left(\sum_{i=1}^{3}\frac{27}{32z_{i}^{2}}\right)\prod_{i=1}^{3}\frac{1}{z_{i}^{2}},\qquad W_{3,1}^{\mathrm{B}}(z_{1})=\frac{225}{1024z_{1}^{6}},

\displaystyle W_{2,4}^{\mathrm{B}}(z_{1},\dots,z_{4})=\left(\sum_{i=1}^{4}\frac{135}{32z_{i}^{2}}\right)\prod_{i=1}^{4}\frac{1}{z_{i}^{2}},

\displaystyle W_{3,2}^{\mathrm{B}}(z_{1},z_{2})=\left(\sum_{i=1}^{2}\frac{1125}{1024z_{i}^{4}}+\frac{567}{512z_{1}^{2}z_{2}^{2}}\right)\prod_{i=1}^{2}\frac{1}{z_{i}^{2}}.

Let us introduce the BGW spectral curve which deforms the Bessel spectral curve.

Definition 4.9

(BGW spectral curve)
.

The BGW spectral curve
\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{BGW}}=\big({\mathbb{P}}^{1};\mathsf{x},\mathsf{y}^{\mathrm{BGW}},B\big)
is
defined by

\displaystyle\mathsf{x}(z)=\frac{1}{2}z^{2},\qquad\mathsf{y}^{\mathrm{BGW}}(z)=\frac{1}{z}+\sum_{a\geq 0}\mathsf{v}_{a}z^{a},

(4.32)

and the bidifferential
B
in equation (
4.4
),
where
\mathsf{v}_{a}
are time variables.
The BGW spectral curve
\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{BGW}}
for
\mathsf{v}_{a}=0
yields the Bessel spectral curve
\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{B}}
.

Similar to Proposition
4.7
, the correlation functions

\displaystyle W_{g,n}^{\mathrm{BGW}}(z_{1},\dots,z_{n})=\sum_{a_{1},\dots,a_{n}\geq 0}F^{\mathrm{BGW}(g)}_{a_{1},\dots,a_{n}}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\frac{1}{z_{i}^{2a_{i}+2}},

(4.33)

obtained from the CEO topological recursion for
\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{BGW}}

obey the following proposition.

Proposition 4.10
.

The coefficients
F^{\mathrm{BGW}(g)}_{a_{1},\dots,a_{n}}
in equation (
4.33
) are written in terms of
the volume coefficients
F^{\mathrm{B}(g)}_{a_{1},\dots,a_{n}}
in equation (
4.30
)
(
or equation (
2.30
)
)
as

\displaystyle F^{\mathrm{BGW}(g)}_{a_{1},\dots,a_{n}}=\sum_{m\geq 0}\frac{(-1)^{m}}{m!}\sum_{b_{1},\dots,b_{m}\geq 1}\Bigg(\prod_{j=1}^{m}\frac{\mathsf{v}_{2b_{j}-1}}{2b_{j}+1}\Bigg)F^{\mathrm{B}(g)}_{a_{1},\dots,a_{n},b_{1},\dots,b_{m}},

(4.34)

where the sum over
m
and
b_{j}
satisfies

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}a_{i}=g-1-\sum_{j=1}^{m}b_{j},

(4.35)

by the homogeneity condition (
2.29
).

Proof.

The statement can be shown in the parallel way as the proof of Proposition
4.7
.
The CEO topological recursion for the BGW spectral curve
\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{BGW}}
gives

\displaystyle\omega_{g,n}^{\mathrm{BGW}}(z_{1},\dots,z_{n})={}

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{Res}}\limits_{w=0}\frac{(-1){\rm d}z_{1}}{\big(z_{1}^{2}-w^{2}\big)\left(\mathsf{y}^{\mathrm{BGW}}(w)-\mathsf{y}^{\mathrm{BGW}}(-w)\right){\rm d}w}\mathcal{R}\omega_{g,n}^{\mathrm{BGW}}(w,z_{K})

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{Res}}\limits_{w=0}K^{\mathrm{B}}(z_{1},w)Y^{\mathrm{BGW}}(w)\mathcal{R}\omega_{g,n}^{\mathrm{BGW}}(w,z_{K}),

(4.36)

where
K^{\mathrm{B}}(z_{1},w)
is the recursion kernel (
4.31
)
for the Bessel spectral curve
\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{B}}
, and

\displaystyle Y^{\mathrm{BGW}}(w)=\frac{2}{w\big(\mathsf{y}^{\mathrm{BGW}}(w)-\mathsf{y}^{\mathrm{BGW}}(-w)\big)}=\sum_{m\geq 0}(-1)^{m}\Bigg(\sum_{b\geq 1}\mathsf{v}_{2b-1}w^{2b}\Bigg)^{m}=1+\mathcal{O}\big(w^{2}\big),

is a regular even function of
w
around
w=0
.
Using the formula (
4.16
) recursively, we rewrite equation (
4.36
) as

\displaystyle\omega_{g,n}^{\mathrm{BGW}}(z_{1},\dots,z_{n})={}

\displaystyle\sum_{m\geq 0}(-1)^{m}\mathop{\mathrm{Res}}\limits_{w_{m}=0}\frac{\mathsf{v}(w_{m})w_{m}{\rm d}z_{1}}{\big(z_{1}^{2}-w_{m}^{2}\big)}\mathop{\mathrm{Res}}\limits_{w_{m-1}=0}\frac{\mathsf{v}(w_{m-1})w_{m-1}{\rm d}w_{m}}{\big(w_{m}^{2}-w_{m-1}^{2}\big)}\cdots

\displaystyle\times\mathop{\mathrm{Res}}\limits_{w_{1}=0}\frac{\mathsf{v}(w_{1})w_{1}{\rm d}w_{2}}{\big(w_{2}^{2}-w_{1}^{2}\big)}\mathop{\mathrm{Res}}\limits_{w=0}K^{\mathrm{B}}(w_{1},w)\mathcal{R}\omega_{g,n}^{\mathrm{BGW}}(w,z_{K}),

where

\displaystyle\mathsf{v}(w)=\sum_{b\geq 1}\mathsf{v}_{2b-1}w^{2b},

and the residue operators

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{Res}}\limits_{w_{j}=0}\frac{\mathsf{v}(w_{j})w_{j}{\rm d}w}{\big(w^{2}-w_{j}^{2}\big)},

acting on meromorphic differentials of
w_{j}
are
referred to as the
Bessel dilaton leaves
.
In such a way, the BGW meromorphic multidifferentials
\omega_{g,n}^{\mathrm{BGW}}

are obtained as
the Bessel meromorphic multidifferentials
\omega_{g,n}^{\mathrm{B}}

decorated by the Bessel dilaton leaves.
Just like the Airy translation for an Airy dilaton leaf which decorates
(g,n)=(0,2)
part in
the CEO topological recursion for
\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{A}}
with weights (
4.20
),
a Bessel dilaton leaf is translated into a decoration of the
(g,n)=(0,2)
part in
the CEO topological recursion for
\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{B}}
with weights

\displaystyle\begin{cases}0&\textrm{for}\ b=0,\\
\dfrac{\mathsf{v}_{2b-1}}{2b+1}&\textrm{for}\ b\geq 1.\end{cases}

Repeating the same analysis as the proof of Proposition
4.7
,
we find that equation (
4.34
) holds.∎

4.3.2
Super Weil–Petersson volumes

For the super Weil–Petersson spectral curve
\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{SWP}}=\big({\mathbb{P}}^{1};\mathsf{x},\mathsf{y}^{\mathrm{SWP}},B\big)

defined by
[
79
,
91
]
,

\displaystyle\mathsf{x}(z)=\frac{1}{2}z^{2},\qquad\mathsf{y}^{\mathrm{SWP}}(z)=\frac{1}{z}\cos(2\pi z)=\sum_{a\geq 0}\frac{\bigl(-2\pi^{2}\bigr)^{a}}{(2a-1)!!a!}z^{2a-1},

(4.37)

and the bidifferential
B
in equation (
4.4
),
the CEO topological recursion computes the super Weil–Petersson volume coefficients (
2.26
), which give the super Weil–Petersson volumes, by

\displaystyle W_{g,n}^{\mathrm{SWP}}(z_{1},\dots,z_{n})=\sum_{a_{1},\dots,a_{n}\geq 0}F^{\mathrm{SWP}(g)}_{a_{1},\dots,a_{n}}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\frac{1}{z_{i}^{2a_{i}+2}}.

(4.38)

Here, note the relation
\mathsf{y}^{\mathrm{SWP}}(z)=\partial_{\mathsf{x}}\mathsf{y}^{\mathrm{WP}}(z)

with the coordinate function
\mathsf{y}^{\mathrm{WP}}(z)
in equation (
4.22
) of the Weil–Petersson spectral curve.
The coordinate function
\mathsf{y}^{\mathrm{SWP}}(z)
in equation (
4.37
) is found from a specialization of
the coordinate function
\mathsf{y}^{\mathrm{BGW}}(z)
in equation (
4.32
) of the BGW spectral curve as

\displaystyle\mathsf{v}_{2a}=0,\qquad\mathsf{v}_{2a+1}=\frac{\bigl(-2\pi^{2}\bigr)^{a+1}}{(2a+1)!!(a+1)!},

(4.39)

and Proposition
4.10
implies a formula

\displaystyle F^{\mathrm{SWP}(g)}_{a_{1},\dots,a_{n}}=\sum_{m\geq 0}\frac{(-1)^{m}}{m!}\sum_{b_{1},\dots,b_{m}\geq 1}\left(\prod_{j=1}^{m}\frac{\bigl(-2\pi^{2}\bigr)^{b_{j}}}{(2b_{j}+1)!!b_{j}!}\right)F^{\mathrm{B}(g)}_{a_{1},\dots,a_{n},b_{1},\dots,b_{m}}

with the condition (
4.35
).

4.3.3
\boldsymbol{(2,2p-2)}
minimal superstring

A spectral curve

\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{SM}(p)}=\big({\mathbb{P}}^{1};\mathsf{x},\mathsf{y}^{\mathrm{SM}(p)},B\big)

for the
(2,2p-2)
minimal superstring
with an odd positive integer
p
, which is heuristically introduced by

\mathsf{y}^{\mathrm{SM}(p)}(z)=\partial_{\mathsf{x}}\mathsf{y}^{\mathrm{M}(p)}(z)

from the coordinate function
\mathsf{y}^{\mathrm{M}(p)}(z)

in equation (
4.25
) of the
(2,p)
minimal string spectral curve
(see Appendix
A.5
), consists of

\displaystyle\mathsf{x}(z)=\frac{1}{2}z^{2},

\displaystyle\mathsf{y}^{\mathrm{SM}(p)}(z)=\frac{(-1)^{\frac{p-1}{2}}}{z}U_{p-1}\biggl(\frac{2\pi}{p}z\biggr)=\frac{4\pi}{p\sqrt{1-\left(1-\frac{8\pi^{2}z^{2}}{p^{2}}\right)^{2}}}\cos\left(\frac{p}{2}\arccos\left(1-\frac{8\pi^{2}z^{2}}{p^{2}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\phantom{\mathsf{y}^{\mathrm{SM}(p)}(z)}{}=\sum_{a=0}^{\frac{p-1}{2}}\frac{\bigl(-2\pi^{2}\bigr)^{a}}{(2a-1)!!a!}\prod_{i=1}^{a}\left(1-\frac{(2i-1)^{2}}{p^{2}}\right)z^{2a-1},

(4.40)

and the bidifferential
B
in equation (
4.4
),
where
U_{p}(z)
is the Chebyshev polynomial of the second kind defined by

\displaystyle U_{p-1}(\cos\theta)=\frac{\sin(p\theta)}{\sin\theta}.

Notice that the spectral curve
\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{SM}(p)}

interpolates the Bessel spectral curve
\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{B}}
and
the super Weil–Petersson spectral curve
\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{SWP}}
by

\displaystyle\mathsf{y}^{\mathrm{SM}(1)}(z)=\mathsf{y}^{\mathrm{B}}(z)\ \textrm{in equation~\eqref{sp_curve_be}},\qquad\mathsf{y}^{\mathrm{SM}(\infty)}(z)=\mathsf{y}^{\mathrm{SWP}}(z)\ \textrm{in equation~\eqref{sp_curve_sw}}.

The coordinate function
\mathsf{y}^{\mathrm{SM}(p)}(z)
in equation (
4.40
) is found from
a specialization of the coordinate function
\mathsf{y}^{\mathrm{BGW}(p)}(z)
in
equation (
4.32
) of the BGW spectral curve as

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\mathsf{v}_{2a}=0\ &\textrm{for}\ a\geq 0,\\
\displaystyle\mathsf{v}_{2a+1}=\frac{(-2\pi^{2})^{a+1}}{(2a+1)!!(a+1)!}\prod_{i=1}^{a+1}\left(1-\frac{(2i-1)^{2}}{p^{2}}\right)\ &\textrm{for}\ 0\leq a\leq\dfrac{p-3}{2},\\
\mathsf{v}_{2a+1}=0\ &\textrm{for}\ a\geq\dfrac{p-1}{2}.\end{cases}

(4.41)

Adopting this specialization to Proposition
4.10
, we find that
the
(2,2p-2)
minimal superstring volume coefficients

F^{\mathrm{SM}(p)(g)}_{a_{1},\dots,a_{n}}
in the correlation functions

\displaystyle W_{g,n}^{\mathrm{SM}(p)}(z_{1},\dots,z_{n})=\sum_{a_{1},\dots,a_{n}\geq 0}F^{\mathrm{SM}(p)(g)}_{a_{1},\dots,a_{n}}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\frac{1}{z_{i}^{2a_{i}+2}},

(4.42)

obey a formula:

\displaystyle F^{\mathrm{SM}(p)(g)}_{a_{1},\dots,a_{n}}={}

\displaystyle\sum_{m\geq 0}\frac{(-1)^{m}}{m!}

\displaystyle\times\sum_{b_{1},\dots,b_{m}\geq 1}\Bigg(\prod_{j=1}^{m}\frac{\bigl(-2\pi^{2}\bigr)^{b_{j}}}{(2b_{j}+1)!!b_{j}!}\prod_{i=1}^{b_{j}}\left(1-\frac{(2i-1)^{2}}{p^{2}}\right)\Bigg)F^{\mathrm{B}(g)}_{a_{1},\dots,a_{n},b_{1},\dots,b_{m}},

(4.43)

with the condition (
4.35
).

4.4
Twisting

Definition 4.11
.

We refer to a
twisted spectral curve

\mathcal{C}[\mathsf{f}]=(\Sigma;\mathsf{x},\mathsf{y},B[\mathsf{f}])

as a spectral curve with a twisted bidifferential
B[\mathsf{f}]
on
C^{\otimes 2}
with the admissible test function
\mathsf{f}\colon\mathbb{R}_{+}\to\mathbb{C}
.

The twisted volume polynomial
V_{g,n}[\mathsf{f}](L_{1},\dots,L_{n})
with
an admissible test function
\mathsf{f}
is equivalent to
the multidifferential
\omega_{g,n}[\mathsf{f}](z_{1},\dots,z_{n})

which satisfies the CEO topological recursion
for a twisted spectral curve
[
8
,
10
]
.
These are related by the Laplace transform involving an action of

twist-elimination
explained below.

Here we consider a spectral curve
\mathcal{C}=(\Sigma;\mathsf{x},\mathsf{y},B)
such that
the zeros of
{\rm d}\mathsf{x}
are simple, and a local coordinate
p
near
a branch point
\alpha
obeying

\mathsf{x}=p^{2}/2+\mathsf{x}(\alpha).

For this set-up, we introduce a globally defined 1-form
\xi_{\alpha,a}(z)
on the spectral curve
\mathcal{C}
with
a\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
and a branch point
\alpha
by
[
9
]
,

\xi_{\alpha,a}(z)=\underset{w=\alpha}{\mathrm{Res}}\frac{{\rm d}p(w)}{p(w)^{2a+2}}\left(\int_{\alpha}^{w}B(\cdot,z)\right).

In the following discussion, we will focus on the spectral curve
\mathcal{C}
only with a single branch point
\alpha_{0}
, and
define
\xi_{a}(z)
by
16
16

16

For a spectral curve with the coordinate function
\mathsf{x}=z^{2}/2
,
z\in\mathbb{P}^{1}
in equation (
4.3
) and
the bidifferential
{B(z,w)={\rm d}z\otimes{\rm d}w/(z-w)^{2}}
,
z,w\in\mathbb{P}^{1}
in equation (
4.4
),
\xi_{a}(z)={\rm d}z/z^{2a+2}
.

\xi_{a}(z):=\xi_{\alpha_{0},a}(z)
.

The solutions
\omega_{g,n}(z_{1},\dots,z_{n})

(2g-2+n>0)
of the CEO topological recursion are represented by the 1-forms
\xi_{a_{i}}(z_{i})

(
i=1,\dots,n
) as the basis of the multidifferentials:

\displaystyle\omega_{g,n}(z_{1},\dots,z_{n})=\sum_{a_{1},\dots,a_{n}\geq 0}F^{(g)}_{a_{1},\dots,a_{n}}\otimes_{i=1}^{n}\xi_{a_{i}}(z_{i}).

For a twisted spectral curve
\mathcal{C}[\mathsf{f}]=(\Sigma;\mathsf{x},\mathsf{y},B[\mathsf{f}])
,
the solutions
\omega_{g,n}[\mathsf{f}](z_{1},\dots,z_{n})
of
the CEO topological recursion are also given on basis of

\displaystyle\xi_{a}[\mathsf{f}](z)=\underset{w=\alpha_{0}}{\mathrm{Res}}\;\frac{{\rm d}p(w)}{p(w)^{2a+2}}\left(\int_{\alpha_{0}}^{w}B[\mathsf{f}](\cdot,z)\right),

(4.44)

by

\displaystyle\omega_{g,n}[\mathsf{f}](z_{1},\dots,z_{n})=\sum_{a_{1},\dots,a_{n}\geq 0}F^{(g)}[\mathsf{f}]_{a_{1},\dots,a_{n}}\otimes_{i=1}^{n}\xi_{a_{i}}[\mathsf{f}](z_{i}).

(4.45)

Now we introduce twisted correlation functions and a twist-elimination map below.

Definition 4.12
.

The twisted correlation functions
W_{g,n}[\mathsf{f}](z_{1},\dots,z_{n})
for
2g-2+n\geq 0
are defined by

\displaystyle W_{g,n}[\mathsf{f}](z_{1},\dots,z_{n})\otimes_{i=1}^{n}{\rm d}z_{i}=\omega_{g,n}[\mathsf{f}](z_{1},\dots,z_{n}),

(4.46)

and the twisted recursion kernel
K[\mathsf{f}]
is defined by

\displaystyle K[\mathsf{f}](z,w)=\frac{\int_{w}^{\overline{w}}B[\mathsf{f}](\cdot,z)}{2\left(\mathsf{y}(w){\rm d}\mathsf{x}(w)-\mathsf{y}(\overline{w}){\rm d}\mathsf{x}(\overline{w})\right)}.

Definition 4.13

(twist-elimination map)
.

Let
\Omega(\mathcal{C})
be the space of meromorphic multidifferentials on a twisted spectral curve
\mathcal{C}[\mathsf{f}]=(\Sigma;\mathsf{x},\mathsf{y},B[\mathsf{f}])
, spanned by the symmetric tensors of the basis
\xi_{a_{i}}[\mathsf{f}](z_{i})
(
a_{i}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
,
i=1,\dots,n
),
the twisted bidifferential
B[\mathsf{f}]
and
the twisted recursion kernel
K[\mathsf{f}]
.
17
17

17

In this definition, the twisted recursion kernel
K[\mathsf{f}](z,w)
is regarded as
a meromorphic differential of the variable
z
.

The
twist-elimination map

\widehat{\mathcal{E}}[z_{I}]
with an index set
I=\{i_{1},\dots,i_{\ell}\}
is a map acting on
\Omega(\mathcal{C})

and prescribed by the following four properties:

(1)

for a 1-form
c_{a_{i}}\xi_{a_{i}}[\mathsf{f}](z_{i})\in\Omega(\mathcal{C})
with
coefficient
c_{a_{i}}
,

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{E}}[z_{I}]\left(c_{a_{i}}\xi_{a_{i}}[\mathsf{f}](z_{i})\right)=\begin{cases}c_{a_{i}}\xi_{a_{i}}(z_{i})&\textrm{if}\ i\in I\cap\{1,\dots,n\},\\
c_{a_{i}}\xi_{a_{i}}[\mathsf{f}](z_{i})&\textrm{if}\ i\notin I\cap\{1,\dots,n\},\end{cases}

(2)

for the twisted bidifferential
B[\mathsf{f}]
and the twisted recursion kernel
K[\mathsf{f}]
,

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{E}}[z]\left(B[\mathsf{f}](z,w)\right)=B(z,w),\qquad\widehat{\mathcal{E}}[z]\left(K[\mathsf{f}](z,w)\right)=K(z,w),

(4.47)

(3)

for a sum of multidifferentials
\omega^{(1)}[\mathsf{f}]
,
\omega^{(2)}[\mathsf{f}]\in\Omega(\mathcal{C})
,

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{E}}[z_{I}]\big(\omega^{(1)}[\mathsf{f}]+\omega^{(2)}[\mathsf{f}]\big)=\widehat{\mathcal{E}}[z_{I}]\big(\omega^{(1)}[\mathsf{f}]\big)+\widehat{\mathcal{E}}[z_{I}]\big(\omega^{(2)}[\mathsf{f}]\big),

(4.48)

(4)

for a tensor product of multidifferentials
\omega^{(1)}[\mathsf{f}]
,
\omega^{(2)}[\mathsf{f}]\in\Omega(\mathcal{C})
,

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{E}}[z_{I}]\big(\omega^{(1)}[\mathsf{f}]\otimes\omega^{(2)}[\mathsf{f}]\big)=\widehat{\mathcal{E}}[z_{I}]\big(\omega^{(1)}[\mathsf{f}]\big)\otimes\widehat{\mathcal{E}}[z_{I}]\big(\omega^{(2)}[\mathsf{f}]\big).

(4.49)

The twist-elimination map
\mathcal{E}[z_{I}]
acting
on the twisted correlation functions (
4.46
) is induced by

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}[z_{I}]\left\{W_{g,n}[\mathsf{f}]\right\}(z_{1},\dots,z_{n})\otimes_{i=1}^{n}{\rm d}z_{i}=\widehat{\mathcal{E}}[z_{I}]\left(\omega_{g,n}[\mathsf{f}](z_{1},\dots,z_{n})\right).

For the case
I=\{i_{1},\dots,i_{\ell}\}\subseteq\{1,\dots,n\}
,
we obtain partially untwisted correlation functions for
2g-2+n>0
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}[z_{i_{1}},\dots,z_{i_{\ell}}]\left\{W_{g,n}[\mathsf{f}]\right\}(z_{1},\dots,z_{n})\otimes_{i=1}^{n}{\rm d}z_{i}

\displaystyle\qquad=\sum_{a_{1},\dots,a_{n}\geq 0}F^{(g)}[\mathsf{f}]_{a_{1},\dots,a_{n}}\otimes_{m=1}^{\ell}\xi_{a_{i_{m}}}(z_{i_{m}})\otimes_{k\in\{1,\dots,n\}\setminus\{i_{1},\dots,i_{\ell}\}}\xi_{a_{k}}[\mathsf{f}](z_{k}).

(4.50)

More generally, if
\{i_{1},\dots,i_{\ell}\}\nsubseteq\{1,\dots,n\}
,
the partially untwisted correlation functions are
given by
the maximal subset
\{j_{1},\dots,j_{\ell^{\prime}}\}\subseteq\{i_{1},\dots,i_{\ell}\}

which obeys

\{j_{1},\dots,j_{\ell^{\prime}}\}\subseteq\{1,\dots,n\}
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}[z_{i_{1}},\dots,z_{i_{\ell}}]\left\{W_{g,n}[\mathsf{f}]\right\}(z_{1},\dots,z_{n})\otimes_{i=1}^{n}{\rm d}z_{i}

\displaystyle\qquad=\sum_{a_{1},\dots,a_{n}\geq 0}F^{(g)}[\mathsf{f}]_{a_{1},\dots,a_{n}}\otimes_{m=1}^{\ell^{\prime}}\xi_{a_{j_{m}}}(z_{j_{m}})\otimes_{k\in\{1,\dots,n\}\setminus\{j_{1},\dots,j_{\ell^{\prime}}\}}\xi_{a_{k}}[\mathsf{f}](z_{k}).

Figure 8:
In the geometrical interpretation,
the action of the twist-elimination map
\mathcal{E}[z_{1},\dots,z_{n}]
on the correlation function
W_{g,n}[\mathsf{f}^{\mathrm{MV}}](z_{1},\dots,z_{n})

is depicted as eliminations of the closed geodesics on
n
boundaries of the bordered Riemann surface,
and recovers the combinatorial aspects of twisted volumes in Section
3
.

Remark 4.14
.

In Figure
8
, a geometrical interpretation of the action of the twist-elimination map
\mathcal{E}[z_{1},\dots,z_{n}]
on the correlation function
W_{g,n}[\mathsf{f}^{\mathrm{MV}}](z_{1},\dots,z_{n})

with the Masur–Veech type twist is depicted.
In this picture, the correlation function is described by a capped Riemann surface where caps are glued along all boundaries in a bordered Riemann surface.
In this interpretation, the closed geodesics on the bordered boundaries and bulk in the Riemann surface
represent the Masur–Veech type twists on the bases
\xi_{a}[\mathsf{f}^{\mathrm{MV}}]
and
coefficients
F^{(g)}\big[\mathsf{f}^{\mathrm{MV}}\big]_{a_{1},\dots,a_{n}}
in the multidifferential
\omega_{g,n}[\mathsf{f}^{\mathrm{MV}}]
of equation (
4.50
), respectively.
In the computation of twisted volumes by the combinatorial method discussed in Section
3
,
we only enumerate the multicurves wrapping around the closed geodesics in the bulk
18
18

18

The “closed geodesics in the bulk” means the “non-boundary closed geodesics”.
of bordered Riemann surfaces.
To get the twisted volume polynomial
V_{g,n}[\mathsf{f}^{\mathrm{MV}}](L_{1},\dots,L_{n})
from the correlation function
W_{g,n}[\mathsf{f}^{\mathrm{MV}}](z_{1},\dots,z_{n})
,
we need to eliminate the effects of twists coming from the boundaries, while keep those from the closed geodesics in the bulk.
This geometrical interpretation of the twist is used in the physical interpretation discussed in Appendix
A.2
.

In the following, we consider a class of twisted spectral curves with
the coordinate functions in equation (
4.3
) and
twisted bidifferential

[
8
]
,

\displaystyle B\big[\mathsf{f}^{\mathrm{MV}}\big](z_{1},z_{2})=\frac{{\rm d}z_{1}\otimes{\rm d}z_{2}}{(z_{1}-z_{2})^{2}}+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\mathfrak{m}\in\mathbb{Z}^{*}}\frac{{\rm d}z_{1}\otimes{\rm d}z_{2}}{(z_{1}-z_{2}+\mathfrak{m})^{2}}=\zeta_{\mathrm{H}}(2;z_{1}-z_{2}){\rm d}z_{1}\otimes{\rm d}z_{2},

(4.51)

with the Masur–Veech type twist function
\mathsf{f}^{\mathrm{MV}}

in equation (
2.42
), where

\displaystyle\zeta_{\mathrm{H}}(2d;z)={}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{z^{2d}}+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\mathfrak{m}\in\mathbb{Z}^{*}}\frac{1}{(z+\mathfrak{m})^{2d}}=\frac{1}{z^{2d}}+\sum_{k\geq 0}\binom{2k+2d-1}{2k}\zeta(2k+2d)z^{2k},

(4.52)

is the Hurwitz zeta function.
For this twisted bidifferential
B\big[\mathsf{f}^{\mathrm{MV}}\big]
,
the recursion kernel (
4.1
) of the CEO topological recursion yields

\displaystyle K[\mathsf{f}^{\mathrm{MV}}](z,w)={}

\displaystyle\frac{(-1){\rm d}z}{\left(\mathsf{y}(w)-\mathsf{y}(-w)\right){\rm d}w}\left(\frac{1}{z^{2}-w^{2}}+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\mathfrak{m}\in\mathbb{Z}^{*}}\frac{1}{(z+\mathfrak{m})^{2}-w^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\frac{(-1){\rm d}z}{\left(\mathsf{y}(w)-\mathsf{y}(-w)\right){\rm d}w}\sum_{d\geq 0}\zeta_{\mathrm{H}}(2d+2;z)w^{2d},

and the twisted 1-form (
4.44
) is

\displaystyle\xi_{a}\big[\mathsf{f}^{\mathrm{MV}}\big](z)=\zeta_{\mathrm{H}}(2a+2;z){\rm d}z.

(4.53)

By acting the twist-elimination map
\mathcal{E}
involved with the properties of equations (
4.47
), (
4.48
) and (
4.49
) on the CEO topological recursion for the twisted spectral curve, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}[z_{1},z_{K}]\left\{W_{g,n}\big[\mathsf{f}^{\mathrm{MV}}\big]\right\}(z_{1},z_{K})

\displaystyle\qquad=\big[w^{0}\big]\sum_{d\geq 0}\frac{w^{2d+1}}{z_{1}^{2d+2}\left(\mathsf{y}(w)-\mathsf{y}(-w)\right)}\Biggl(\sum_{m=2}^{n}\mathcal{W}_{0,2}(w,z_{m})\mathcal{E}[z_{K\setminus\{m\}}]

\displaystyle\qquad\quad{}\times\left\{W_{g,n-1}\big[\mathsf{f}^{\mathrm{MV}}\big]\right\}(w,z_{K\setminus\{m\}})+\mathcal{E}[z_{K}]\left\{Q_{g,n}\big[\mathsf{f}^{\mathrm{MV}}\big]\right\}(w,-w,z_{K})\biggr),

(4.54)

where
K=\{2,\dots,n\}
, and

\big[w^{0}\big]
implies to pick up all zeroth order terms in the expansion around
w=0
. Here

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathcal{W}_{0,2}(w,z_{m})={}&W_{0,2}(w,z_{m})+W_{0,2}(-w,z_{m})=2\sum_{d\geq 0}(2d+1)\frac{w^{2d}}{z_{m}^{2d+2}},\end{split}

(4.55)

and

\displaystyle Q_{g,n}\big[\mathsf{f}^{\mathrm{MV}}\big](w,-w,z_{K})={}

\displaystyle W_{g-1,n+1}\big[\mathsf{f}^{\mathrm{MV}}\big](w,-w,z_{K})

\displaystyle+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}h+h^{\prime}=g\\
J\sqcup J^{\prime}=K\end{subarray}}^{\mathrm{stable}}W_{h,1+|J|}\big[\mathsf{f}^{\mathrm{MV}}\big](w,z_{J})W_{h^{\prime},1+|J^{\prime}|}\big[\mathsf{f}^{\mathrm{MV}}\big](-w,z_{J^{\prime}}).

We refer to the recursion (
4.54
)
as the
partially twist-eliminated CEO topological recursion
.

In the rest of this section, we provide a direct proof of the following claim.

Theorem 4.15

(Laplace transform of the twisted volume polynomial
[
8
,
10
]
)
.

For the
(2,p)
minimal string and the
(2,2p-2)
minimal superstring,
the twisted correlation function
W_{g,n}\big[\mathsf{f}^{\mathrm{MV}}\big](z_{1},\allowbreak\dots,z_{n})
with the action of the twist-elimination map
\mathcal{E}

agrees with the Laplace transform
\mathcal{L}

of the twisted volume polynomial
V_{g,n}\big[\mathsf{f}^{\mathrm{MV}}\big](L_{1},\dots,L_{n})
:

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}\left\{V_{g,n}\big[\mathsf{f}^{\mathrm{MV}}\big]\right\}(z_{1},\dots,z_{n})=\mathcal{E}[z_{1},\dots,z_{n}]\left\{W_{g,n}\big[\mathsf{f}^{\mathrm{MV}}\big]\right\}(z_{1},\dots,z_{n}),

(4.56)

where the operator
\mathcal{L}
is defined by equation (
4.6
).
19
19

19

This relation is derived formally in the general set-up in
[
8
]
.
In this article, we give a direct proof specialized for
the physical 2D gravity models.

4.4.1
Laplace dual relation for the
\boldsymbol{(2,p)}
minimal string

We will show that the Laplace dual relation (
4.56
) holds for the
(2,p)
minimal string manifestly.
Accordingly, by the specializations
p=\infty
and
1
,
we also find that the Laplace dual relation holds for the Weil–Petersson volumes and the Kontsevich–Witten symplectic volumes, respectively.

Proposition 4.16
.

For the
(2,p)
minimal string, the Laplace transform of
the Mirzakhani type ABO topological recursion (
2.8
)
with the Masur–Veech type twist,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n}}\Biggl[\sum_{m=2}^{n}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\left(R(L_{1},L_{m},x)+L_{1}\mathsf{f}^{\mathrm{MV}}(x)\right)xV_{g,n-1}\big[\mathsf{f}^{\mathrm{MV}}\big](x,L_{K\setminus\{m\}}){\rm d}x

\displaystyle\qquad{}+\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2}}\left(D(L_{1},x,y)+R(L_{1},x,y)\mathsf{f}^{\mathrm{MV}}(y)+R(L_{1},y,x)\mathsf{f}^{\mathrm{MV}}(x)+L_{1}\mathsf{f}^{\mathrm{MV}}(x)\mathsf{f}^{\mathrm{MV}}(y)\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\phantom{+}{}\times xyP_{g,n}\big[\mathsf{f}^{\mathrm{MV}}\big](x,y,L_{K}){\rm d}x{\rm d}y\Biggr]\mathrm{e}^{-z_{1}L_{1}}{\rm d}L_{1}\prod_{i=2}^{n}\mathrm{e}^{-z_{i}L_{i}}L_{i}{\rm d}L_{i},

(4.57)

where
P_{g,n}\big[\mathsf{f}^{\mathrm{MV}}\big](x,y,L_{K})
is given by equation (
2.2
)
for the twisted volume polynomials,
agrees with the partially twist-eliminated CEO topological recursion (
4.54
) for
2g-2+n>1
.

To prove this proposition,
we prepare some key integration formulae involving the kernel function
H^{\mathrm{M}(p)}(x,y)
in equation (
2.19
)
for the
(2,p)
minimal string. (See
[
78
, equations (2.2) and (2.3)]
for analogous formulae of Mirzakhani’s recursion for the Weil–Petersson volumes.)
For
k\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
, one finds

\displaystyle h_{2k+1}^{\mathrm{M}(p)}(t):={}

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\frac{x^{2k+1}}{(2k+1)!}H^{\mathrm{M}(p)}(x,t){\rm d}x

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\frac{t^{2k+2}}{(2k+2)!}-2\sum_{j=1}^{(p-1)/2}(-1)^{j}\cos\left(\frac{\pi}{p}j\right)\sum_{\ell=0}^{k}u_{j}^{2\ell-2k-2}\frac{t^{2\ell}}{(2\ell)!}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\sum_{\ell=0}^{k+1}s_{\ell}\frac{t^{2k+2-2\ell}}{(2k+2-2\ell)!},

(4.58)

where
u_{j}=(p/2\pi)\sin(j\pi/p)
in equation (
2.20
), and

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2}}\frac{x^{2a+1}y^{2b+1}}{(2a+1)!(2b+1)!}H^{\mathrm{M}(p)}(x+y,t){\rm d}x{\rm d}y=h_{2a+2b+3}^{\mathrm{M}(p)}(t).

(4.59)

The coefficients
s_{\ell}
’s in equation (
4.58
) agree with
those in the following expansion for
1/\mathsf{y}^{\mathrm{M}(p)}(z)

of the
(2,p)
minimal string spectral curve:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\mathsf{y}^{\mathrm{M}(p)}(z)}={}

\displaystyle(-1)^{\frac{p-1}{2}}\frac{2\pi}{T_{p}\big(\frac{2\pi}{p}z\big)}=\frac{1}{z}+\sum_{j=1}^{(p-1)/2}(-1)^{j}\cos\left(\frac{\pi}{p}j\right)\left(\frac{1}{z-u_{j}}+\frac{1}{z+u_{j}}\right)

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\sum_{\ell\geq 0}s_{\ell}z^{2\ell-1}.

(4.60)

In addition, we will use an integration formula involving the Masur–Veech type twist function,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\mathsf{f}^{\mathrm{MV}}(x)x^{2k+1}{\rm d}x=(2k+1)!\zeta(2k+2).

(4.61)

Lemma 4.17
.

In equation (
4.57
),
the
x^{2k+1}
term in
xV_{g,n-1}\big[\mathsf{f}^{\mathrm{MV}}\big](x,L_{K\setminus\{m\}})
obeys

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{3}}\big(R(L_{1},L_{m},x)+L_{1}\mathsf{f}^{\mathrm{MV}}(x)\big)x^{2k+1}\mathrm{e}^{-z_{1}L_{1}-z_{m}L_{m}}{\rm d}x{\rm d}L_{1}L_{m}{\rm d}L_{m}

\displaystyle\qquad=\frac{1}{2}\big[w^{0}\big]\sum_{d\geq 0}\frac{1}{z_{1}^{2d+2}}\frac{w^{2d+1}}{\mathsf{y}^{\mathrm{M}(p)}(w)}\mathcal{W}_{0,2}(w,z_{m})\zeta_{\mathrm{H}}(2k+2;w)(2k+1)!.

(4.62)

Proof.

We perform the integrations on the left-hand side of equation (
4.62
) using (
2.10
), (
4.58
) and (
4.61
):

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{3}}\left[\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{L_{1}}\big(H^{\mathrm{M}(p)}(x,t+L_{m})+H^{\mathrm{M}(p)}(x,t-L_{m})\big){\rm d}t+L_{1}\mathsf{f}^{\mathrm{MV}}(x)\right]x^{2k+1}

\displaystyle\qquad{}\times\mathrm{e}^{-z_{1}L_{1}-z_{m}L_{m}}{\rm d}x{\rm d}L_{1}L_{m}{\rm d}L_{m}

\displaystyle\qquad\phantom{\times}{}=(2k+1)!\left(\sum_{\ell=0}^{k+1}\sum_{d=0}^{k+1-\ell}s_{\ell}\cdot(2k+3-2\ell-2d)\frac{1}{z_{1}^{2d+2}}\frac{1}{z_{m}^{2k+4-2\ell-2d}}\right.

\displaystyle\left.\qquad\phantom{\times=}{}+\frac{1}{z_{1}^{2}}\frac{1}{z_{m}^{2}}\zeta(2k+2)\right),

(4.63)

where we also used
a formula of the Laplace transform:

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}L^{n}\mathrm{e}^{-zL}{\rm d}L=\frac{n!}{z^{n+1}}.

(4.64)

On the other hand, by equation (
4.55
) the right-hand side of equation (
4.62
) is

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\big[w^{0}\big]\sum_{d\geq 0}\frac{1}{z_{1}^{2d+2}}\frac{w^{2d+1}}{\mathsf{y}^{\mathrm{M}(p)}(w)}\left(2\sum_{d^{\prime}\geq 0}(2d^{\prime}+1)\frac{w^{2d^{\prime}}}{z_{m}^{2d^{\prime}+2}}\right)\zeta_{\mathrm{H}}(2k+2;w)(2k+1)!.

(4.65)

Adopting the formula (
4.52
) for
\zeta_{\mathrm{H}}(2k+2;w)

and the expansion (
4.60
) for
1/\mathsf{y}^{\mathrm{M}(p)}(w)
to this expression,
we find the agreement between equations (
4.63
) and (
4.65
).
∎

Lemma 4.18
.

In equation (
4.57
),
the
x^{2a+1}y^{2b+1}
term in
xyP_{g,n}\big[\mathsf{f}^{\mathrm{MV}}\big](x,y,L_{K})
obeys

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{3}}\big(D(L_{1},x,y)+R(L_{1},x,y)\mathsf{f}^{\mathrm{MV}}(y)+R(L_{1},y,x)\mathsf{f}^{\mathrm{MV}}(x)+L_{1}\mathsf{f}^{\mathrm{MV}}(x)\mathsf{f}^{\mathrm{MV}}(y)\big)

\displaystyle\qquad{}\times x^{2a+1}y^{2b+1}\mathrm{e}^{-z_{1}L_{1}}{\rm d}x{\rm d}y{\rm d}L_{1}

\displaystyle\qquad\phantom{\times}{}=\frac{1}{2}\big[w^{0}\big]\sum_{d\geq 0}\frac{1}{z_{1}^{2d+2}}\frac{w^{2d+1}}{\mathsf{y}^{\mathrm{M}(p)}(w)}\left(\zeta_{\mathrm{H}}(2a+2;w)(2a+1)!\right)\left(\zeta_{\mathrm{H}}(2b+2;w)(2b+1)!\right).\!\!\!

(4.66)

Proof.

We perform the integrations on the left-hand side of equation (
4.66
) using (
2.10
),
(
4.58
), (
4.59
) and (
4.61
).
The term involving
D(L_{1},x,y)
yields

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{3}}\left(\int_{0}^{L_{1}}H^{\mathrm{M}(p)}(x+y,t){\rm d}t\right)x^{2a+1}y^{2b+1}\mathrm{e}^{-z_{1}L_{1}}{\rm d}x{\rm d}y{\rm d}L_{1}

\displaystyle\qquad=\frac{1}{2}(2a+1)!(2b+1)!\sum_{\ell=0}^{a+b+2}\frac{s_{\ell}}{z_{1}^{2a+2b-2\ell+4}}.

(4.67)

The term involving
R(L_{1},x,y)\mathsf{f}^{\mathrm{MV}}(y)
yields:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{3}}\left(\int_{0}^{L_{1}}\big(H^{\mathrm{M}(p)}(x,t+y)+H^{\mathrm{M}(p)}(x,t-y)\big){\rm d}t\right)x^{2a+1}y^{2b+1}\mathsf{f}^{\mathrm{MV}}(y)\mathrm{e}^{-z_{1}L_{1}}{\rm d}x{\rm d}y{\rm d}L_{1}

\displaystyle\quad=\frac{1}{2}(2a+1)!\sum_{\ell=0}^{a+1}\sum_{d=0}^{a+1-\ell}s_{\ell}\frac{(2a+2b+3-2\ell-2d)!}{(2a+2-2\ell-2d)!}\zeta(2a+2b+4-2\ell-2d)\frac{1}{z_{1}^{2d+2}},

(4.68)

and we find the term involving
R(L_{1},y,x)\mathsf{f}^{\mathrm{MV}}(x)
by replacing the role of parameters
a
and
b
in equation (
4.68
):

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}(2b+1)!\sum_{\ell=0}^{b+1}\sum_{d=0}^{b+1-\ell}s_{\ell}\frac{(2a+2b+3-2\ell-2d)!}{(2b+2-2\ell-2d)!}\zeta(2a+2b+4-2\ell-2d)\frac{1}{z_{1}^{2d+2}}.

Finally the term involving
\mathsf{f}^{\mathrm{MV}}(x)\mathsf{f}^{\mathrm{MV}}(y)
yields

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2z_{1}^{2}}(2a+1)!(2b+1)!\zeta(2a+2)\zeta(2b+2).

(4.69)

On the other hand,
adopting equations (
4.52
) and (
4.60
) on the right-hand side of equation (
4.66
),
we correctly recover the sum of four terms (
4.67
) – (
4.69
).
∎

Combining the claims in Lemmas
4.17
and
4.18
as well as
the expansions (

) and (

) with (
4.53
),
we find the claim of Proposition
4.16
.

Laplace dual relation for the

) holds for the

Proposition 4.19
.

For the
(2,2p-2)
minimal superstring, the Laplace transform of
the Mirzakhani type ABO topological recursion (
2.8
)
with the Masur–Veech type twist,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n}}\Biggl[\sum_{m=2}^{n}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}R(L_{1},L_{m},x)xV_{g,n-1}\big[\mathsf{f}^{\mathrm{MV}}\big](x,L_{K\setminus\{m\}}){\rm d}x

\displaystyle\qquad{}+\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2}}\left(D(L_{1},x,y)+R(L_{1},x,y)\mathsf{f}^{\mathrm{MV}}(y)+R(L_{1},y,x)\mathsf{f}^{\mathrm{MV}}(x)\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\phantom{+}{}\times xyP_{g,n}\big[\mathsf{f}^{\mathrm{MV}}\big](x,y,L_{K}){\rm d}x{\rm d}y\biggr]\mathrm{e}^{-z_{1}L_{1}}{\rm d}L_{1}\prod_{i=2}^{n}\mathrm{e}^{-z_{i}L_{i}}L_{i}{\rm d}L_{i},

(4.70)

agrees with the partially twist-eliminated CEO topological recursion (
4.54
) for
2g-2+n>1
.

To prove Proposition
4.19
, we use
an integration formula

\displaystyle h_{2k+1}^{\mathrm{SM}(p)}(t):={}

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\frac{x^{2k+1}}{(2k+1)!}H^{\mathrm{SM}(p)}(x,t){\rm d}x

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\frac{t^{2k+1}\delta_{p,1}}{(2k+1)!}-\frac{1}{\pi}\sum_{j=1}^{(p-1)/2}(-1)^{j}\cos^{2}\left(\frac{\pi}{p}\left(j-\frac{1}{2}\right)\right)\sum_{\ell=0}^{k}(u^{\prime}_{j})^{2\ell-2k-1}\frac{t^{2\ell+1}}{(2\ell+1)!}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\sum_{\ell=0}^{k}s^{\prime}_{\ell}\frac{t^{2k+1-2\ell}}{(2k+1-2\ell)!},

(4.71)

involving the kernel function
H^{\mathrm{SM}(p)}(x,y)
in equation (
2.31
) for the
(2,2p-2)
minimal superstring,
where
u_{j}^{\prime}=(p/2\pi)\sin((j-1/2)\pi/p)
in equation (
2.32
).
(See
[
79
, Section 5.4]
for an analogous formula of Stanford–Witten’s recursion for the super Weil–Petersson volumes.)
The following expansion for the
(2,2p-2)
minimal superstring spectral curve
also gives the coefficients
s^{\prime}_{\ell}
’s in equation (
4.71
):

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\mathsf{y}^{\mathrm{SM}(p)}(z)}={}

\displaystyle(-1)^{\frac{p-1}{2}}\frac{z}{U_{p-1}\left(\frac{2\pi}{p}z\right)}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle z\delta_{p,1}+\frac{z}{2\pi}\sum_{j=1}^{(p-1)/2}(-1)^{j}\cos^{2}\left(\frac{\pi}{p}\left(j-\frac{1}{2}\right)\right)\left(\frac{1}{z-u^{\prime}_{j}}-\frac{1}{z+u^{\prime}_{j}}\right)

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\sum_{\ell\geq 0}s^{\prime}_{\ell}z^{2\ell+1}.

(4.72)

Lemma 4.20
.

In equation (
4.70
),
the
x^{2k+1}
term in
xV_{g,n-1}\big[\mathsf{f}^{\mathrm{MV}}\big](x,L_{K\setminus\{m\}})
obeys

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{3}}R(L_{1},L_{m},x)x^{2k+1}\mathrm{e}^{-z_{1}L_{1}-z_{m}L_{m}}{\rm d}x{\rm d}L_{1}L_{m}{\rm d}L_{m}

\displaystyle\qquad=\frac{1}{2}\big[w^{0}\big]\sum_{d\geq 0}\frac{1}{z_{1}^{2d+2}}\frac{w^{2d+1}}{\mathsf{y}^{\mathrm{SM}(p)}(w)}\mathcal{W}_{0,2}(w,z_{m})\zeta_{\mathrm{H}}(2k+2;w)(2k+1)!.

(4.73)

Proof.

The equation (
4.73
) is verified in the parallel way as equation (

).

)
by equations (

), (

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{3}}\big(H^{\mathrm{SM}(p)}(x,L_{1}+L_{m})+H^{\mathrm{SM}(p)}(x,L_{1}-L_{m})\big)x^{2k+1}\mathrm{e}^{-z_{1}L_{1}-z_{m}L_{m}}{\rm d}x{\rm d}L_{1}L_{m}{\rm d}L_{m}

\displaystyle\qquad=(2k+1)!\sum_{\ell=0}^{k}\sum_{d=0}^{k-\ell}s^{\prime}_{\ell}\cdot(2k-2\ell-2d+1)\frac{1}{z_{1}^{2d+2}}\frac{1}{z_{m}^{2k-2\ell-2d+2}}.

The right-hand side of equation (

) is in the same form (
4.65
) as the
(2,p)
minimal string.
By the formula (
4.52
) for
\zeta_{\mathrm{H}}(2k+2;w)

and the expansion (

,

Lemma 4.21
.

In equation (

),

5
Virasoro constraints

In this section, we first overview an algebraic formulation, called the quantum Airy structures
[
9
,
60
]
,
of the ABO topological recursion and the CEO topological recursion.
In particular, we will see the equivalence between
the quantum Airy structures and the Virasoro constraints
for the physical 2D gravity models in Table
1
.
We then discuss explicit computation of the volume coefficients

F^{(g)}_{a_{1},\dots,a_{n}}
as well as the twisted volume coefficients

F^{(g)}\big[\mathsf{f}^{\mathrm{MV}}\big]_{a_{1},\dots,a_{n}}
using
the cut-and-join equations in
[
2
,
3
]

derived from the Virasoro constraints and homogeneity conditions,
and a group action in
[
9
]
which is associated with the twist action.

5.1
Formulation

Consider the generating function of the volume coefficients

F^{(g)}_{a_{1},\dots,a_{n}}
in equation (
2.3
) of the ABO topological recursion
or equation (
4.7
) of the CEO topological recursion for
2g-2+n>0
:

\displaystyle Z(\hbar;\textbf{t})=\mathrm{e}^{F(\hbar;\textbf{t})},

\displaystyle F(\hbar;\textbf{t})=\sum_{g\geq 0}\hbar^{2g-2}F_{g}(\textbf{t})=\sum_{g\geq 0,n\geq 1}\hbar^{2g-2}\sum_{a_{1},\dots,a_{n}\geq 0}F^{(g)}_{a_{1},\dots,a_{n}}\frac{t_{a_{1}}\cdots t_{a_{n}}}{n!}.

(5.1)

Here
\textbf{t}=\{t_{0},t_{1},t_{2},\dots\}
is the set of variables
t_{a}

which are related to the length variables
L_{i}
in equation (
2.3
)
and the spectral curve variables
z_{i}
in equation (
4.7
) by

\displaystyle\frac{t_{a_{1}}\cdots t_{a_{n}}}{n!}\ \ \longleftrightarrow\ \ \frac{L_{1}^{2a_{1}}}{(2a_{1}+1)!}\cdots\frac{L_{n}^{2a_{n}}}{(2a_{n}+1)!}\ \ \longleftrightarrow\ \ \frac{1}{z_{1}^{2a_{1}+2}}\cdots\frac{1}{z_{n}^{2a_{n}+2}}.

In this set of variables, the recursion (
2.6
) leads to

\displaystyle\partial_{k}F(\hbar;\textbf{t})={}

\displaystyle\sum_{a,b\geq 0}\mathsf{B}^{k}_{a,b}t_{a}\partial_{b}F(\hbar;\textbf{t})+\frac{\hbar^{2}}{2}\sum_{a,b\geq 0}\mathsf{C}^{k}_{a,b}(\partial_{a}\partial_{b}F(\hbar;\textbf{t})+\partial_{a}F(\hbar;\textbf{t})\partial_{b}F(\hbar;\textbf{t}))

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2\hbar^{2}}\sum_{a,b\geq 0}\mathsf{A}^{k}_{a,b}t_{a}t_{b}+\mathsf{D}^{k},

where
\partial_{a}=\partial/\partial t_{a}
,
and the following proposition is obtained.

Proposition 5.1

(
[
9
]
)
.

The generating function (
5.1
) satisfies constraint equations,

\displaystyle\widehat{L}_{k}Z(\hbar;\textbf{t})=0,\qquad k\geq-1,

(5.2)

where

\displaystyle\widehat{L}_{k}=-\frac{1}{2}\partial_{k+1}+\frac{1}{4\hbar^{2}}\sum_{a,b\geq 0}\mathsf{A}^{k+1}_{a,b}t_{a}t_{b}+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{a,b\geq 0}\mathsf{B}^{k+1}_{a,b}t_{a}\partial_{b}+\frac{\hbar^{2}}{4}\sum_{a,b\geq 0}\mathsf{C}^{k+1}_{a,b}\partial_{a}\partial_{b}+\frac{1}{2}\mathsf{D}^{k+1}.

(5.3)

When the differential operators
\widehat{L}_{k}
satisfy

\displaystyle\big[\widehat{L}_{k},\widehat{L}_{\ell}\big]=\sum_{a\geq-1}f^{a}_{k,\ell}\widehat{L}_{a},\qquad k,\ell\geq-1,

(5.4)

where
f^{a}_{k,\ell}
are scalars, the operators
\widehat{L}_{k}
define
a so called
quantum Airy structure
on the space of the variables
t_{a}

[
9
,
60
]
.
The quantum Airy structure is shown to be a sufficient condition for
the existence of the solution to the constraint equations (
5.2
)
[
9
]
.
In particular, when the differential operators
\widehat{L}_{k}
satisfy
the
Virasoro relations

\displaystyle\big[\widehat{L}_{k},\widehat{L}_{\ell}\big]=\left(k-\ell\right)\widehat{L}_{k+\ell},\qquad k,\ell\geq-1,

(5.5)

the constraint equations (
5.2
) are referred to as
the
Virasoro constraints
.
As we will show below, the generating functions
of the volume coefficients for the physical 2D gravity models in Table
1
, with or without twist,
satisfy the Virasoro constraints.

5.2
Bosonic models

Here we discuss the bosonic models in Table
1
.

5.2.1
Airy and KdV

From equation (
2.15
),
the Airy initial data for the Kontsevich–Witten symplectic volumes of moduli spaces of stable curves are

\displaystyle\mathsf{A}^{a_{1}}_{a_{2},a_{3}}=\delta_{a_{1},a_{2},a_{3},0},\qquad

\displaystyle\mathsf{B}^{a_{1}}_{a_{2},a_{3}}=(2a_{2}+1)\delta_{a_{1}+a_{2},a_{3}+1},

\displaystyle\mathsf{C}^{a_{1}}_{a_{2},a_{3}}=\delta_{a_{1},a_{2}+a_{3}+2},\qquad

\displaystyle\mathsf{D}^{a_{1}}=\frac{1}{8}\delta_{a_{1},1},

(5.6)

and the differential operators for
k\geq-1
in equation (
5.3
),

\displaystyle\widehat{L}_{k}^{\mathrm{A}}=-\frac{1}{2}\partial_{k+1}+\sum_{a\geq 0}\left(a+\frac{1}{2}\right)t_{a}\partial_{a+k}+\frac{\hbar^{2}}{4}\mathop{\sum_{a,b\geq 0}}\limits_{a+b=k-1}\partial_{a}\partial_{b}+\frac{1}{4\hbar^{2}}t_{0}^{2}\delta_{k,-1}+\frac{1}{16}\delta_{k,0},

(5.7)

satisfy the Virasoro relations

\displaystyle\big[\widehat{L}_{k}^{\mathrm{A}},\widehat{L}_{\ell}^{\mathrm{A}}\big]=(k-\ell)\widehat{L}_{k+\ell}^{\mathrm{A}},\qquad k,\ell\geq-1.

Then, the constraint equations (
5.2
) provide
the Virasoro constraints
[
28
,
41
]
,

\displaystyle\widehat{L}_{k}^{\mathrm{A}}Z^{\mathrm{A}}(\hbar;\textbf{t})=0,\qquad k\geq-1,

(5.8)

for the generating function of the Airy volume coefficients (
2.18
):

\displaystyle\log Z^{\mathrm{A}}(\hbar;\textbf{t})=\sum_{g\geq 0}\hbar^{2g-2}F^{\mathrm{A}}_{g}(\textbf{t})=\sum_{g\geq 0,n\geq 1}\hbar^{2g-2}\mathop{\sum_{a_{1},\dots,a_{n}\geq 0}}\limits_{|\mathbf{a}|=3g-3+n}F^{\mathrm{A}(g)}_{a_{1},\dots,a_{n}}\frac{t_{a_{1}}\cdots t_{a_{n}}}{n!},

(5.9)

which satisfies the homogeneity condition (
2.17
).

Remark 5.2
.

The Virasoro operators
\widehat{L}_{k}^{\mathrm{A}}
in equation (
5.7
) admits
the free field realization
[
28
,
41
]
:

\displaystyle T^{\mathrm{A}}(x)=:\partial\phi^{\mathrm{A}}(x)\partial\phi^{\mathrm{A}}(x)\!:+\frac{1}{16x^{2}}=\sum_{n\in{\mathbb{Z}}}\widehat{L}_{n}^{\mathrm{A}}x^{-n-2},

by a chiral bosonic field with the anti-periodic boundary condition:

\displaystyle\partial\phi^{\mathrm{A}}(x)=\frac{1}{\hbar}\sum_{n\geq 0}\left(n+\frac{1}{2}\right)\left(t_{n}-\frac{1}{3}\delta_{n,1}\right)x^{n-\frac{1}{2}}+\frac{\hbar}{2}\sum_{n\geq 0}\partial_{n}x^{-n-\frac{3}{2}}=\sum_{n\in{\mathbb{Z}}}\alpha_{n+\frac{1}{2}}^{\mathrm{A}}x^{-n-\frac{3}{2}}.

From the Virasoro constraints (
5.8
) with
the homogeneity condition (
2.17
),
a cut-and-join representation of the Airy generating function (
5.9
)
is derived in
[
2
]
following
[
75
]
.

Proposition 5.3

(
[
2
]
)
.

The Airy generating function (
5.9
), which is a solution of the Virasoro constraints (
5.8
),
is given by

\displaystyle Z^{\mathrm{A}}(x\hbar;x\textbf{t})=\sum_{k\geq 0}x^{k}Z^{\mathrm{A}}_{k}(\hbar;\textbf{t})=\mathrm{e}^{x\widehat{W}^{\mathrm{A}}}\cdot 1,

(5.10)

where the cut-and-join operator
\widehat{W}^{\mathrm{A}}
is

\displaystyle\widehat{W}^{\mathrm{A}}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{3}\sum_{a,b\geq 0}\left(2a+1\right)\left(2b+1\right)t_{a}t_{b}\partial_{a+b-1}+\frac{\hbar^{2}}{6}\sum_{a,b\geq 0}\left(2a+2b+5\right)t_{a+b+2}\partial_{a}\partial_{b}+\frac{t_{0}^{3}}{6\hbar^{2}}+\frac{t_{1}}{8},

where
\partial_{-1}=0
.

Proof.

From the Virasoro constraints (
5.8
), we find

\displaystyle 0=\frac{2}{3}\sum_{k\geq 0}(2k+1)t_{k}\widehat{L}_{k-1}^{\mathrm{A}}Z^{\mathrm{A}}(\hbar;\textbf{t})=-\widehat{D}^{\mathrm{A}}Z^{\mathrm{A}}(\hbar;\textbf{t})+\widehat{W}^{\mathrm{A}}Z^{\mathrm{A}}(\hbar;\textbf{t}),

(5.11)

where
\widehat{D}^{\mathrm{A}}
denotes the Euler operator

\displaystyle\widehat{D}^{\mathrm{A}}=\frac{1}{3}\sum_{k\geq 0}(2k+1)t_{k}\partial_{k}.

The homogeneity condition (
2.17
) leads to
the action of the Euler operator on
Z^{\mathrm{A}}
such that

\displaystyle\widehat{D}^{\mathrm{A}}Z^{\mathrm{A}}_{k}(\hbar;\textbf{t})=kZ^{\mathrm{A}}_{k}(\hbar;\textbf{t}),

and equation (
5.11
) gives

\displaystyle\sum_{k\geq 0}x^{k+1}\widehat{W}^{\mathrm{A}}Z^{\mathrm{A}}_{k}(\hbar;\textbf{t})=\sum_{k\geq 1}x^{k}kZ^{\mathrm{A}}_{k}(\hbar;\textbf{t}).

(5.12)

From
x^{k}
terms in equation (
5.12
),
a recursion relation

\widehat{W}^{\mathrm{A}}Z^{\mathrm{A}}_{k-1}(\hbar;\textbf{t})=kZ^{\mathrm{A}}_{k}(\hbar;\textbf{t})

is found, and we obtain a solution
Z^{\mathrm{A}}_{k}(\hbar;\textbf{t})
by adopting
the recursion relation iteratively,

\displaystyle Z^{\mathrm{A}}_{k}(\hbar;\textbf{t})=\frac{1}{k}\widehat{W}^{\mathrm{A}}Z^{\mathrm{A}}_{k-1}(\hbar;\textbf{t})=\cdots=\frac{1}{k!}\big(\widehat{W}^{\mathrm{A}}\big)^{k}\cdot 1,

(5.13)

where
Z^{\mathrm{A}}_{0}(\hbar;\textbf{t})=1
.
And finally, we find the cut-and-join representation (
5.10
) by taking a generating series of
Z^{\mathrm{A}}_{k}(\hbar;\textbf{t})
in equation (
5.13
).
∎

By the iterative use of the cut-and-join equation (
5.10
),
we obtain
the first few volume coefficients in equation (
5.9
) as:

\displaystyle F^{\mathrm{A}}_{0}(\textbf{t})=\frac{t_{0}^{3}}{6}+\frac{t_{0}^{3}t_{1}}{2}+\left(\frac{5}{8}t_{0}^{4}t_{2}+\frac{3}{2}t_{0}^{3}t_{1}^{2}\right)+\left(\frac{7}{8}t_{0}^{5}t_{3}+\frac{45}{8}t_{0}^{4}t_{1}t_{2}+\frac{9}{2}t_{0}^{3}t_{1}^{3}\right)+\cdots,

\displaystyle F^{\mathrm{A}}_{1}(\textbf{t})=\frac{t_{1}}{8}+\left(\frac{5t_{0}t_{2}}{8}+\frac{3t_{1}^{2}}{16}\right)+\left(\frac{35}{16}t_{0}^{2}t_{3}+\frac{15}{4}t_{0}t_{1}t_{2}+\frac{3}{8}t_{1}^{3}\right)+\cdots,

\displaystyle F^{\mathrm{A}}_{2}(\textbf{t})=\frac{105t_{4}}{128}+\left(\frac{1155t_{0}t_{5}}{128}+\frac{945t_{1}t_{4}}{128}+\frac{1015t_{2}t_{3}}{128}\right)+\cdots,

\displaystyle F^{\mathrm{A}}_{3}(\textbf{t})=\frac{25025t_{7}}{1024}+\left(\frac{425425t_{8}t_{0}}{1024}+\frac{375375t_{1}t_{7}}{1024}+\frac{385385t_{2}t_{6}}{1024}+\frac{193655t_{3}t_{5}}{512}+\frac{191205t_{4}^{2}}{1024}\right)

\displaystyle\hphantom{F^{\mathrm{A}}_{3}({t})= }{}+\cdots.

(5.14)

The Airy generating function (
5.9
) generates the coefficients
F^{\mathrm{KdV}(g)}_{a_{1},\dots,a_{n}}
in equation (
4.12
) by the following proposition.

Proposition 5.4
.

The generating function of the coefficients
F^{\mathrm{KdV}(g)}_{a_{1},\dots,a_{n}}
in equation (
4.12
),

\displaystyle\log Z^{\mathrm{KdV}}(\hbar;\textbf{t})=\sum_{g\geq 0,n\geq 1}\hbar^{2g-2}\mathop{\sum_{a_{1},\dots,a_{n}\geq 0}}\limits_{|\mathbf{a}|\leq 3g-3+n}F^{\mathrm{KdV}(g)}_{a_{1},\dots,a_{n}}\frac{t_{a_{1}}\cdots t_{a_{n}}}{n!},

(5.15)

is obtained by shifting the variables in the Airy generating function (
5.9
) as

\displaystyle t_{a}\to t_{a}-\frac{\mathsf{u}_{2a-1}}{2a+1}\qquad\textrm{for}\quad a\geq 2.

(5.16)

Proof.

The shift (
5.16
) for the Airy generating function (
5.9
) gives

\displaystyle\mathop{\sum_{a_{1},\dots,a_{n+m}\geq 0}}\limits_{|\mathbf{a}|=3g-3+n+m}\frac{1}{(n+m)!}F^{\mathrm{A}(g)}_{a_{1},\dots,a_{n+m}}\prod_{i=1}^{n+m}\left(t_{a_{i}}-\frac{\mathsf{u}_{2a_{i}-1}}{2a_{i}+1}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad=\mathop{\sum_{a_{1},\dots,a_{n+m}\geq 0}}\limits_{|\mathbf{a}|=3g-3+n+m}\frac{1}{(n+m)!}\binom{n+m}{m}F^{\mathrm{A}(g)}_{a_{1},\dots,a_{n+m}}\Bigg(\prod_{i=1}^{n}t_{a_{i}}\Bigg)\Bigg(\prod_{j=1}^{m}(-1)\frac{\mathsf{u}_{2a_{n+j}-1}}{2a_{n+j}+1}\Bigg)+\cdots,

where
\mathsf{u}_{-1}=\mathsf{u}_{1}=0
.
This leads to the right-hand side of the formula (
4.13
) in
Proposition
4.7
, and the claim is proved.
∎

Proposition
5.4
implies that
the KdV generating function (

) also satisfies the Virasoro constraints and can be computed by the cut-and-join equation (

)
with the shift (
