\displaystyle F^{\mathrm{B}}_{0}(\textbf{t})=0,\qquad F^{\mathrm{SWP}}_{1}(\textbf{t})=F^{\mathrm{B}}_{1}(\textbf{t})=-\frac{1}{8}\log(1-t_{0}),

\displaystyle F^{\mathrm{SWP}}_{2}(\textbf{t})={}

\displaystyle\frac{9t_{1}}{128(1-t_{0})^{3}}+\frac{3\pi^{2}}{64}\left(\frac{1}{(1-t_{0})^{3}}-1\right)

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\left(\frac{9\pi^{2}t_{0}}{64}+\frac{9t_{1}}{128}\right)+\left(\frac{9}{32}\pi^{2}t_{0}^{2}+\frac{27}{128}t_{1}t_{0}\right)+\left(\frac{15}{32}\pi^{2}t_{0}^{3}+\frac{27}{64}t_{1}t_{0}^{2}\right)+\cdots,

\displaystyle F^{\mathrm{SWP}}_{3}(\textbf{t})={}

\displaystyle\frac{225t_{2}}{1024(1-t_{0})^{5}}+\frac{567t_{1}^{2}}{1024(1-t_{0})^{6}}+\frac{189\pi^{2}t_{1}}{256(1-t_{0})^{6}}+\frac{3\pi^{4}(37+5t_{0})}{512(1-t_{0})^{6}}-\frac{111\pi^{4}}{512}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\left(\frac{681}{512}\pi^{4}t_{0}+\frac{189}{256}\pi^{2}t_{1}+\frac{225}{1024}t_{2}\right)

\displaystyle+\left(\frac{2421}{512}\pi^{4}t_{0}^{2}+\frac{567}{128}\pi^{2}t_{1}t_{0}+\frac{1125}{1024}t_{2}t_{0}+\frac{567}{1024}t_{1}^{2}\right)+\cdots.

5.3.3
\boldsymbol{(2,2p-2)}
minimal superstring

From Proposition
5.6
,
the generating function of the
(2,2p-2)
minimal superstring free energies
F^{\mathrm{SM}(p)(g)}_{a_{1},\dots,a_{n}}
in equation (
4.42
)
labeled by an odd positive integer
p
,

\displaystyle\log Z^{\mathrm{SM}(p)}(\hbar;\textbf{t})=\sum_{g\geq 0}\hbar^{2g-2}F^{\mathrm{SM}(p)}_{g}(\textbf{t})=\sum_{g\geq 0,n\geq 1}\hbar^{2g-2}\mathop{\sum_{a_{1},\dots,a_{n}\geq 0}}\limits_{|\mathbf{a}|\leq g-1}F^{\mathrm{SM}(p)(g)}_{a_{1},\dots,a_{n}}\frac{t_{a_{1}}\cdots t_{a_{n}}}{n!},

(5.31)

is given by the shift of variables in the Bessel generating function (
5.24
),

\displaystyle t_{a}\to t_{a}-\frac{\bigl(-2\pi^{2}\bigr)^{a}}{(2a+1)!!a!}\prod_{i=1}^{a}\left(1-\frac{(2i-1)^{2}}{p^{2}}\right)\qquad\textrm{for}\quad 1\leq a\leq\frac{p-1}{2}.

(5.32)

This shift is found from the specialization
(
4.41
) of the time variables
\mathsf{v}_{a}
of the BGW spectral curve
to obtain the
(2,2p-2)
minimal superstring spectral curve
\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{SM}(p)}
.

Adopting the shift (
5.32
) to equation (
5.27
),
we find the first few volume coefficients in equation (
5.31
) such that

\displaystyle F^{\mathrm{SM}(p)}_{0}(\textbf{t})={}

\displaystyle F^{\mathrm{B}}_{0}(\textbf{t})=0,\qquad F^{\mathrm{SM}(p)}_{1}(\textbf{t})=F^{\mathrm{B}}_{1}(\textbf{t})=-\frac{1}{8}\log(1-t_{0}),

\displaystyle F^{\mathrm{SM}(p)}_{2}(\textbf{t})={}

\displaystyle\frac{9t_{1}}{128(1-t_{0})^{3}}+\left(\frac{3\pi^{2}}{64(1-t_{0})^{3}}-\frac{3\pi^{2}}{64}\right)P_{1}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\left(\left(\frac{9}{64}-\frac{9}{64p^{2}}\right)t_{0}\pi^{2}+\frac{9t_{1}}{128}\right)+\left(\left(\frac{9}{32}-\frac{9}{32p^{2}}\right)t_{0}^{2}\pi^{2}+\frac{27t_{1}t_{0}}{128}\right)+\cdots,

\displaystyle F^{\mathrm{SM}(p)}_{3}(\textbf{t})={}

\displaystyle\frac{225t_{2}}{1024(1-t_{0})^{5}}+\frac{567t_{1}^{2}}{1024(1-t_{0})^{6}}+\frac{189\pi^{2}t_{1}P_{1}}{256(1-t_{0})^{6}}

\displaystyle+\frac{3\pi^{4}P_{1}}{512}\left(\frac{42P_{1}-5P_{2}+5t_{0}P_{2}}{(1-t_{0})^{6}}-42P_{1}+5P_{2}\right)

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\left(\left(\frac{681t_{0}}{512}-\frac{381t_{0}}{256p^{2}}+\frac{81t_{0}}{512p^{4}}\right)\pi^{4}+\left(\frac{189t_{1}}{256}-\frac{189t_{1}}{256p^{2}}\right)\pi^{2}+\frac{225t_{2}}{1024}\right)

\displaystyle+\left(\left(\frac{2421}{512}-\frac{1521}{256p^{2}}+\frac{621}{512p^{4}}\right)t_{0}^{2}\pi^{4}+\left(\frac{567}{128}-\frac{567}{128p^{2}}\right)t_{0}t_{1}\pi^{2}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\frac{1125t_{2}t_{0}}{1024}+\frac{567t_{1}^{2}}{1024}\right)+\cdots,

where
P_{1}=1-1/p^{2}
and
P_{2}=1-9/p^{2}
.
Note that the minimal superstring volume coefficients interpolate
the Bessel volume coefficients at
p=1
and
the super Weil–Petersson volume coefficients at
p=\infty
,

\displaystyle F^{\mathrm{SM}(1)}_{g}(\textbf{t})=F^{\mathrm{B}}_{g}(\textbf{t}),\qquad F^{\mathrm{SM}(\infty)}_{g}(\textbf{t})=F^{\mathrm{SWP}}_{g}(\textbf{t}).

5.4
Twisting

The twisted initial data (
2.40
) of the ABO topological recursion
defines the generating function
Z[\mathsf{f}]
of
the twisted volume coefficients
F^{(g)}[\mathsf{f}]_{a_{1},\dots,a_{n}}
,

\displaystyle Z[\mathsf{f}](\hbar;\textbf{t})=\exp\bigg(\sum_{g\geq 0,n\geq 1}\hbar^{2g-2}\sum_{a_{1},\dots,a_{n}\geq 0}F^{(g)}[\mathsf{f}]_{a_{1},\dots,a_{n}}\frac{t_{a_{1}}\cdots t_{a_{n}}}{n!}\bigg),

which satisfies constraint equations

\displaystyle\widehat{L}[\mathsf{f}]_{k}Z[\mathsf{f}](\hbar;\textbf{t})=0,\qquad k\geq-1,

where
\widehat{L}[\mathsf{f}]_{k}
are twisted differential operators

\displaystyle\widehat{L}[\mathsf{f}]_{k}={}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\partial_{k+1}+\frac{1}{4\hbar^{2}}\sum_{a,b\geq 0}\mathsf{A}[\mathsf{f}]^{k+1}_{a,b}t_{a}t_{b}+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{a,b\geq 0}\mathsf{B}[\mathsf{f}]^{k+1}_{a,b}t_{a}\partial_{b}

\displaystyle+\frac{\hbar^{2}}{4}\sum_{a,b\geq 0}\mathsf{C}[\mathsf{f}]^{k+1}_{a,b}\partial_{a}\partial_{b}+\frac{1}{2}\mathsf{D}[\mathsf{f}]^{k+1}.

The following proposition is then established.

Proposition 5.7

(
[
9
]
)
.

The generating functions
Z
and
Z[\mathsf{f}]
, and
the differential operators
\widehat{L}_{k}
and
\widehat{L}[\mathsf{f}]_{k}

are related by the group action of

\displaystyle\widehat{U}[\mathsf{f}]=\exp\bigg(\frac{\hbar^{2}}{2}\sum_{a,b\geq 0}u[\mathsf{f}]_{a,b}\partial_{a}\partial_{b}\bigg),

(5.33)

defined by the twist function
u[\mathsf{f}]_{a,b}
in equation (
2.41
), as

\displaystyle Z[\mathsf{f}](\hbar;\textbf{t})=\widehat{U}[\mathsf{f}]Z(\hbar;\textbf{t}),\qquad\widehat{L}[\mathsf{f}]_{k}=\widehat{U}[\mathsf{f}]\widehat{L}_{k}\widehat{U}[\mathsf{f}]^{-1}.

(5.34)

When the differential operators
\widehat{L}_{k}
satisfy the constraint equations (
5.4
)
of the quantum Airy structure,
it is found from the group actions (
5.34
) that the twisted differential operators
\widehat{L}[\mathsf{f}]_{k}
also
satisfy

\displaystyle\big[\widehat{L}[\mathsf{f}]_{k},\widehat{L}[\mathsf{f}]_{\ell}\big]=\sum_{a\geq-1}f^{a}_{k,\ell}\widehat{L}[\mathsf{f}]_{a},\qquad k,\ell\geq-1.

In particular, when the differential operators
\widehat{L}_{k}
satisfy
the Virasoro relations (
5.5
), the twisted operators
\widehat{L}[\mathsf{f}]_{k}
also satisfy the Virasoro relations

\displaystyle\big[\widehat{L}[\mathsf{f}]_{k},\widehat{L}[\mathsf{f}]_{\ell}\big]=(k-\ell)\widehat{L}[\mathsf{f}]_{k+\ell},\qquad k,\ell\geq-1.

In the case of the Masur–Veech type twist,

u_{a,b}^{\mathrm{MV}}
in equation (
2.43
),
the operator (
5.33
) of the group action yields

\displaystyle\widehat{U}^{\mathrm{MV}}=\widehat{U}\big[\mathsf{f}^{\mathrm{MV}}\big]=\exp\bigg(\frac{\hbar^{2}}{2}\sum_{a,b\geq 0}\frac{(2a+2b+1)!}{(2a+1)!(2b+1)!}\zeta(2a+2b+2)\partial_{a}\partial_{b}\bigg).

(5.35)

Remark 5.8
.

The twist action
\widehat{U}[\mathsf{f}]
is regarded as an exponentiated quadratic operator of Bogoliubov’s transformation type.
It preserves Virasoro algebra of differential operators acting on a partition function, but can be harmful for integrable hierarchy equations.
21
21

21

This aspect of the twist action is pointed out by an anonymous referee.

5.5
Masur–Veech type twist

In the following, we discuss the Virasoro constraints with the Masur–Veech type twist for the physical 2D gravity models in Table
1
.

5.5.1
Masur–Veech polynomials

The initial data for the Masur–Veech polynomials are found from
the Airy initial data (
5.6
) twisted by
equations (
2.40
) and (
2.43
):

\displaystyle\mathsf{A}\big[\mathsf{f}^{\mathrm{MV}}\big]^{a_{1}}_{a_{2},a_{3}}=\delta_{a_{1},a_{2},a_{3},0},

\displaystyle\mathsf{B}\big[\mathsf{f}^{\mathrm{MV}}\big]^{a_{1}}_{a_{2},a_{3}}=(2a_{2}+1)\delta_{a_{1}+a_{2},a_{3}+1}+\zeta(2a_{3}+2)\delta_{a_{1},a_{2},0},

\displaystyle\mathsf{C}\big[\mathsf{f}^{\mathrm{MV}}\big]^{a_{1}}_{a_{2},a_{3}}=\delta_{a_{1},a_{2}+a_{3}+2}+\zeta(2a_{2}+2)\zeta(2a_{3}+2)\delta_{a_{1},0}

\displaystyle\phantom{\mathsf{C}\big[\mathsf{f}^{\mathrm{MV}}\big]^{a_{1}}_{a_{2},a_{3}}=}{}+\left(\binom{2a_{2}+2a_{3}-2a_{1}+3}{2a_{2}+1}+\binom{2a_{2}+2a_{3}-2a_{1}+3}{2a_{3}+1}\right)

\displaystyle\phantom{\mathsf{C}\big[\mathsf{f}^{\mathrm{MV}}\big]^{a_{1}}_{a_{2},a_{3}}=+}{}\times\zeta(2a_{2}+2a_{3}-2a_{1}+4),

\displaystyle\mathsf{D}\big[\mathsf{f}^{\mathrm{MV}}\big]^{a_{1}}=\frac{1}{8}\delta_{a_{1},1}+\frac{\zeta(2)}{2}\delta_{a_{1},1}.

By Proposition
5.7
,
the twisted differential operators

\displaystyle\widehat{L}_{k}^{\mathrm{MV}}=\widehat{L}^{\mathrm{A}}\big[\mathsf{f}^{\mathrm{MV}}\big]_{k}=\widehat{U}^{\mathrm{MV}}\widehat{L}_{k}^{\mathrm{A}}\big(\widehat{U}^{\mathrm{MV}}\big)^{-1},

of the Airy Virasoro operators
\widehat{L}_{k}^{\mathrm{A}}
in equation (
5.7
)
by the twist (
5.35
)
also satisfy the Virasoro relations
22
22

22

These Virasoro relations can also be checked directly by
verifying the commutation relations or
using the free field realization
in Remark
5.9
below.

\displaystyle\big[\widehat{L}_{k}^{\mathrm{MV}},\widehat{L}_{\ell}^{\mathrm{MV}}\big]=\left(k-\ell\right)\widehat{L}_{k+\ell}^{\mathrm{MV}},\qquad k,\ell\geq-1,

(5.36)

and provide the Virasoro constraints

\displaystyle\widehat{L}_{k}^{\mathrm{MV}}Z^{\mathrm{MV}}(\hbar;\textbf{t})=0,\qquad k\geq-1,

for the generating function of the Masur–Veech polynomials

\displaystyle\log Z^{\mathrm{MV}}(\hbar;\textbf{t})={}

\displaystyle\log Z^{\mathrm{A}}\big[\mathsf{f}^{\mathrm{MV}}\big](\hbar;\textbf{t})=\log\big(\widehat{U}^{\mathrm{MV}}Z^{\mathrm{A}}(\hbar;\textbf{t})\big)

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\sum_{g\geq 0}\hbar^{2g-2}F^{\mathrm{MV}}_{g}(\textbf{t})=\sum_{g\geq 0,n\geq 1}\hbar^{2g-2}\mathop{\sum_{a_{1},\dots,a_{n}\geq 0}}\limits_{|\mathbf{a}|\leq 3g-3+n}F^{\mathrm{MV}(g)}_{a_{1},\dots,a_{n}}\frac{t_{a_{1}}\cdots t_{a_{n}}}{n!}.

(5.37)

Remark 5.9
.

The twisted Virasoro operators
\widehat{L}_{k}^{\mathrm{MV}}
in equation (
5.36
) admit
the free field realization

\displaystyle T^{\mathrm{MV}}(x)={}{:}\partial\phi^{\mathrm{MV}}(x)\partial\phi^{\mathrm{MV}}(x){:}+\frac{1}{16x^{2}}+\frac{\zeta(2)}{4x}=\sum_{n\in{\mathbb{Z}}}\widehat{L}_{n}^{\mathrm{MV}}x^{-n-2},

by a “twisted” chiral bosonic field with the anti-periodic boundary condition

\displaystyle\partial\phi^{\mathrm{MV}}(x)={}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\hbar}\sum_{n\geq 0}\left(n+\frac{1}{2}\right)\Bigg(\left(t_{n}-\frac{1}{3}\delta_{n,1}\right)+\hbar^{2}\sum_{a\geq 0}u_{a,n}^{\mathrm{MV}}\partial_{a}\Bigg)x^{n-\frac{1}{2}}+\frac{\hbar}{2}\sum_{n\geq 0}\partial_{n}x^{-n-\frac{3}{2}}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\sum_{n\in{\mathbb{Z}}}\alpha_{n+\frac{1}{2}}^{\mathrm{MV}}x^{-n-\frac{3}{2}}.

Note that the Virasoro relation (
5.36
) follows
from this free field realization immediately.

By Proposition
5.3
,
the Masur–Veech generating function (
5.37
) is computed by

\displaystyle Z^{\mathrm{MV}}(x\hbar;x\textbf{t})={}

\displaystyle\widehat{U}^{\mathrm{MV}}Z^{\mathrm{A}}(x\hbar;x\textbf{t})=\widehat{U}^{\mathrm{MV}}\mathrm{e}^{x\widehat{W}^{\mathrm{A}}}\cdot 1,

and we obtain the first few volume coefficients such that

\displaystyle F^{\mathrm{MV}}_{0}(\textbf{t})={}

\displaystyle\frac{t_{0}^{3}}{6}+\left(\frac{\pi^{2}}{48}t_{0}^{4}+\frac{1}{2}t_{1}t_{0}^{3}\right)+\left(\frac{\pi^{4}}{160}t_{0}^{5}+\frac{\pi^{2}}{8}t_{0}^{4}t_{1}+\frac{5}{8}t_{0}^{4}t_{2}+\frac{3}{2}t_{1}^{2}t_{0}^{3}\right)+\cdots,

\displaystyle F^{\mathrm{MV}}_{1}(\textbf{t})={}

\displaystyle\left(\frac{\pi^{2}t_{0}}{12}+\frac{t_{1}}{8}\right)+\left(\frac{\pi^{4}}{32}t_{0}^{2}+\frac{\pi^{2}}{4}t_{0}t_{1}+\frac{5}{8}t_{0}t_{2}+\frac{3}{16}t_{1}^{2}\right)+\left(\frac{11}{576}\pi^{6}t_{0}^{3}+\frac{3}{16}\pi^{4}t_{0}^{2}t_{1}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\frac{65}{96}\pi^{2}t_{0}^{2}t_{2}+\frac{3}{4}\pi^{2}t_{0}t_{1}^{2}+\frac{35}{16}t_{0}^{2}t_{3}+\frac{15}{4}t_{0}t_{1}t_{2}+\frac{3}{8}t_{1}^{3}\right)+\cdots,

\displaystyle F^{\mathrm{MV}}_{2}(\textbf{t})={}

\displaystyle\left(\frac{29}{2560}\pi^{8}t_{0}+\frac{1}{32}\pi^{6}t_{1}+\frac{119}{1152}\pi^{4}t_{2}+\frac{35}{96}\pi^{2}t_{3}+\frac{105}{128}t_{4}\right)+\cdots.

(5.38)

Proposition
5.4
leads to the following proposition.

Proposition 5.10
.

The generating function of the twisted KdV volume coefficients,

\displaystyle\log Z^{\mathrm{KdV}}\big[\mathsf{f}^{\mathrm{MV}}\big](\hbar;\textbf{t})=\sum_{g\geq 0,n\geq 1}\hbar^{2g-2}\mathop{\sum_{a_{1},\dots,a_{n}\geq 0}}\limits_{|\mathbf{a}|\leq 3g-3+n}F^{\mathrm{KdV}(g)}\big[\mathsf{f}^{\mathrm{MV}}\big]_{a_{1},\dots,a_{n}}\frac{t_{a_{1}}\cdots t_{a_{n}}}{n!},

5.5.3
Super Masur–Veech polynomials

By Proposition
5.7
, the twisted differential operators

\displaystyle\widehat{L}_{k}^{\mathrm{SMV}}=\widehat{L}^{\mathrm{B}}\big[\mathsf{f}^{\mathrm{MV}}\big]_{k}=\widehat{U}^{\mathrm{MV}}\widehat{L}_{k}^{\mathrm{B}}\big(\widehat{U}^{\mathrm{MV}}\big)^{-1},

\displaystyle\widehat{L}_{k}^{\mathrm{SMV}}Z^{\mathrm{SMV}}(\hbar;\textbf{t})=0,\qquad k\geq 0,

for the generating function of the super Masur–Veech polynomials

\displaystyle\log Z^{\mathrm{SMV}}(\hbar;\textbf{t})={}

\displaystyle\log Z^{\mathrm{B}}\big[\mathsf{f}^{\mathrm{MV}}\big](\hbar;\textbf{t})=\log\big(\widehat{U}^{\mathrm{MV}}Z^{\mathrm{B}}(\hbar;\textbf{t})\big)

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\sum_{g\geq 0}\hbar^{2g-2}F^{\mathrm{SMV}}_{g}(\textbf{t})=\sum_{g\geq 0,n\geq 1}\hbar^{2g-2}\mathop{\sum_{a_{1},\dots,a_{n}\geq 0}}\limits_{|\mathbf{a}|\leq g-1}F^{\mathrm{SMV}(g)}_{a_{1},\dots,a_{n}}\frac{t_{a_{1}}\cdots t_{a_{n}}}{n!}.

(5.40)

By Proposition
5.5
, the super Masur–Veech generating function
is computed by

\displaystyle Z^{\mathrm{SMV}}(x\hbar;x\textbf{t})={}

\displaystyle\widehat{U}^{\mathrm{MV}}Z^{\mathrm{B}}(x\hbar;x\textbf{t})=\widehat{U}^{\mathrm{MV}}\mathrm{e}^{x\widehat{W}^{\mathrm{B}}}\cdot 1,

and we obtain the first few volume coefficients such that

\displaystyle F^{\mathrm{SMV}}_{0}(\textbf{t})={}

\displaystyle F^{\mathrm{B}}_{0}(\textbf{t})=0,\qquad F^{\mathrm{SMV}}_{1}(\textbf{t})=F^{\mathrm{B}}_{1}(\textbf{t})=-\frac{1}{8}\log(1-t_{0}),

\displaystyle F^{\mathrm{SMV}}_{2}(\textbf{t})={}

\displaystyle\frac{9t_{1}}{128(1-t_{0})^{3}}+\frac{3\pi^{2}}{256}\left(\frac{1}{(1-t_{0})^{2}}-1\right)

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\left(\frac{3\pi^{2}t_{0}}{128}+\frac{9t_{1}}{128}\right)+\left(\frac{9}{256}\pi^{2}t_{0}^{2}+\frac{27}{128}t_{1}t_{0}\right)+\left(\frac{3}{64}\pi^{2}t_{0}^{3}+\frac{27}{64}t_{1}t_{0}^{2}\right)+\cdots,

\displaystyle F^{\mathrm{SMV}}_{3}(\textbf{t})={}

\displaystyle\frac{225t_{2}}{1024(1-t_{0})^{5}}+\frac{567t_{1}^{2}}{1024(1-t_{0})^{6}}+\frac{153\pi^{2}t_{1}}{2048(1-t_{0})^{5}}+\frac{23\pi^{4}}{4096}\left(\frac{1}{(1-t_{0})^{4}}-1\right)

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\left(\frac{23}{1024}\pi^{4}t_{0}+\frac{153}{2048}\pi^{2}t_{1}+\frac{225}{1024}t_{2}\right)

\displaystyle+\left(\frac{115}{2048}\pi^{4}t_{0}^{2}+\frac{765}{2048}\pi^{2}t_{1}t_{0}+\frac{1125}{1024}t_{2}t_{0}+\frac{567}{1024}t_{1}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle+\left(\frac{115}{1024}\pi^{4}t_{0}^{3}+\frac{2295}{2048}\pi^{2}t_{1}t_{0}^{2}+\frac{3375}{1024}t_{2}t_{0}^{2}+\frac{1701}{512}t_{1}^{2}t_{0}\right)+\cdots.

(5.41)

Proposition
5.6
leads to the following proposition.

Proposition 5.11
.

The generating function of the twisted BGW volume coefficients,

\displaystyle\log Z^{\mathrm{BGW}}\big[\mathsf{f}^{\mathrm{MV}}\big](\hbar;\textbf{t})=\sum_{g\geq 0,n\geq 1}\hbar^{2g-2}\mathop{\sum_{a_{1},\dots,a_{n}\geq 0}}\limits_{|\mathbf{a}|\leq g-1}F^{\mathrm{BGW}(g)}\big[\mathsf{f}^{\mathrm{MV}}\big]_{a_{1},\dots,a_{n}}\frac{t_{a_{1}}\cdots t_{a_{n}}}{n!},

is obtained by shifting the variables
t_{a}
as equation (
5.28
) in the super Masur–Veech
(
i.e., twisted Bessel
)
generating function (
5.40
).

5.5.4
Twisted
\boldsymbol{(2,2p-2)}
minimal superstring volume polynomials

The generating function of twisted volume coefficients for the
(2,2p-2)
minimal superstring,

\displaystyle\log Z^{\mathrm{SM}(p)}\big[\mathsf{f}^{\mathrm{MV}}\big](\hbar;\textbf{t})={}

\displaystyle\sum_{g\geq 0}\hbar^{2g-2}F^{\mathrm{SM}(p)}_{g}\big[\mathsf{f}^{\mathrm{MV}}\big](\textbf{t}),

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\sum_{g\geq 0,n\geq 1}\hbar^{2g-2}\mathop{\sum_{a_{1},\dots,a_{n}\geq 0}}\limits_{|\mathbf{a}|\leq g-1}F^{\mathrm{SM}(p)(g)}\big[\mathsf{f}^{\mathrm{MV}}\big]_{a_{1},\dots,a_{n}}\frac{t_{a_{1}}\cdots t_{a_{n}}}{n!},

is obtained by shifting the variables
t_{a}
in the super Masur–Veech
generating function (
5.40
) as

\displaystyle t_{a}\to t_{a}-\frac{\bigl(-2\pi^{2}s\bigr)^{a}}{(2a+1)!!a!}\prod_{i=1}^{a}\left(1-\frac{(2i-1)^{2}}{p^{2}}\right)\qquad\textrm{for}\quad 1\leq a\leq\frac{p-1}{2},

(5.42)

where a deformation parameter
s
is introduced.

Adopting the shift (
5.42
) to the super Masur–Veech generating functions (
5.41
),
we find the first few volume coefficients such that

\displaystyle F^{\mathrm{SM}(p)}_{0}\big[\mathsf{f}^{\mathrm{MV}}\big](\textbf{t})={}

\displaystyle F^{\mathrm{B}}_{0}(\textbf{t})=0,\qquad F^{\mathrm{SM}(p)}_{1}\big[\mathsf{f}^{\mathrm{MV}}\big](\textbf{t})=F^{\mathrm{B}}_{1}(\textbf{t})=-\frac{1}{8}\log(1-t_{0}),

\displaystyle F^{\mathrm{SM}(p)}_{2}\big[\mathsf{f}^{\mathrm{MV}}\big](\textbf{t})={}

\displaystyle\frac{9t_{1}}{128(1-t_{0})^{3}}+\left(\frac{3\pi^{2}}{64(1-t_{0})^{3}}-\frac{3\pi^{2}}{64}\right)sP_{1}+\frac{3\pi^{2}}{256(1-t_{0})^{2}}-\frac{3\pi^{2}}{256}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\left(\left(\frac{9st_{0}}{64}-\frac{9st_{0}}{64p^{2}}+\frac{3t_{0}}{128}\right)\pi^{2}+\frac{9t_{1}}{128}\right)

\displaystyle+\left(\left(\frac{9st_{0}^{2}}{32}-\frac{9st_{0}^{2}}{32p^{2}}+\frac{9t_{0}^{2}}{256}\right)\pi^{2}+\frac{27t_{1}t_{0}}{128}\right)+\cdots,

\displaystyle F^{\mathrm{SM}(p)}_{3}\big[\mathsf{f}^{\mathrm{MV}}\big](\textbf{t})={}

\displaystyle\frac{225t_{2}}{1024(1-t_{0})^{5}}+\frac{567t_{1}^{2}}{1024(1-t_{0})^{6}}+\frac{189\pi^{2}t_{1}sP_{1}}{256(1-t_{0})^{6}}+\frac{153\pi^{2}t_{1}}{2048(1-t_{0})^{5}}

\displaystyle+\frac{23\pi^{4}}{4096}\left(\frac{1}{(1-t_{0})^{4}}-1\right)+\frac{3\pi^{4}s^{2}P_{1}}{512}\left(\frac{42P_{1}-5P_{2}+5t_{0}P_{2}}{(1-t_{0})^{6}}\right.

\displaystyle\left.-42P_{1}+5P_{2}\right)+\frac{51\pi^{4}sP_{1}}{1024}\left(\frac{1}{(1-t_{0})^{5}}-1\right)

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\left(\left(\frac{681s^{2}t_{0}}{512}-\frac{381s^{2}t_{0}}{256p^{2}}+\frac{81s^{2}t_{0}}{512p^{4}}+\frac{255st_{0}}{1024}-\frac{255st_{0}}{1024p^{2}}+\frac{23t_{0}}{1024}\right)\pi^{4}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\left(\frac{189st_{1}}{256}-\frac{189st_{1}}{256p^{2}}+\frac{153t_{1}}{2048}\right)\pi^{2}+\frac{225t_{2}}{1024}\right)

\displaystyle+\left(\left(\frac{2421s^{2}t_{0}^{2}}{512}-\frac{1521s^{2}t_{0}^{2}}{256p^{2}}+\frac{621s^{2}t_{0}^{2}}{512p^{4}}+\frac{765st_{0}^{2}}{1024}-\frac{765st_{0}^{2}}{1024p^{2}}+\frac{115t_{0}^{2}}{2048}\right)\pi^{4}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\left(\frac{567st_{1}t_{0}}{128}-\frac{567st_{1}t_{0}}{128p^{2}}+\frac{765t_{1}t_{0}}{2048}\right)\pi^{2}+\frac{1125t_{2}t_{0}}{1024}+\frac{567t_{1}^{2}}{1024}\right)+\cdots,

where
P_{1}=1-1/p^{2}
and
P_{2}=1-9/p^{2}
.
These volume coefficients reduce to the super Masur–Veech volume coefficients (
5.41
) at
p=1

and the twisted super Weil–Petersson volume coefficients at
p=\infty
.

Appendix A
Physical derivations of spectral curves

and the Masur–Veech twist

In this appendix, we discuss a physical derivation of spectral curves for the JT gravity and the
(2,p)
minimal string
as well as their supersymmetric generalizations.
And we also discuss a physical interpretation of
the Masur–Veech type twist in terms of the JT gravity.

A.1
JT gravity and Weil–Petersson volume

Here we summarize basic results of the JT gravity in
[
88
]
which is necessary for our physical derivation of the Masur–Veech type twist.

The JT gravity is the two-dimensional dilaton gravity which appears in a model of
\mathrm{AdS}_{2}/\mathrm{CFT}_{1}\!
correspondence.
In this gravity theory, the dilaton function plays a role of the Lagrange multiplier setting a hyperbolic constraint
R=-2
for the Ricci curvature of a two-dimensional surface.

From the physical duality conjecture of the
\mathrm{AdS_{2}/CFT_{1}}
correspondence, it is found that the partition function
Z_{0,1}^{\mathrm{JT}}(\beta)
of the JT gravity on the Euclidean
\mathrm{AdS}_{2}
homeomorphic to a hyperbolic disk with a wiggly boundary is dual to the thermal partition function
\langle Z(\beta)\rangle=\langle\mathrm{e}^{-\beta H_{\mathrm{SYK}}}\rangle
of the Sachdev–Ye–Kitaev (SYK) model on the boundary circle in the low energy limit which is described by the Schwarzian theory.
On the boundary of the disk parametrized by a proper length coordinate
u
in Figure
9
(left),

Figure 9:
Left: A disk with a wiggly boundary parametrized by a proper length coordinate
u
.
Right: A double trumpet with two wiggly boundaries.

the metric
g_{uu}
and the dilaton field
\phi
of the JT gravity obey the
wiggly
boundary conditions below with a parameter
\gamma
:

\displaystyle g_{uu}\big|_{\mathrm{bdy}}=\frac{1}{\epsilon^{2}},\qquad\phi\big|_{\mathrm{bdy}}=\frac{\gamma}{\epsilon},\qquad\epsilon\to 0,

(A.1)

and it is necessary to perform the path integral over the boundary graviton modes to get the partition function of the JT gravity.
In
[
90
]
, a direct computation of the disk partition function
Z_{0,1}^{\mathrm{JT}}(\beta)
is performed, and the following striking formula is obtained

\displaystyle Z_{0,1}^{\mathrm{JT}}(\beta)=\frac{\gamma^{\frac{3}{2}}}{(2\pi)^{\frac{1}{2}}\beta^{\frac{3}{2}}}\mathrm{e}^{\frac{2\pi^{2}\gamma}{\beta}}.

Further evidence of the duality conjecture is observed for the partition function of the JT gravity on the hyperbolic
double trumpet
with wiggly boundaries which is homeomorphic to the cylinder in Figure
9
(right).
The double trumpet partition function of the JT gravity is shown to be dual to the spectral form factor
\langle Z(\beta+\mathrm{i}T)Z(\beta-\mathrm{i}T)\rangle
of the SYK model in the ramp region.

Figure 10:
Left: A hyperbolic trumpet with a wiggly boundary and a geodesic boundary
of length
L
.
Right: A double trumpet made of two hyperbolic trumpets.

The double trumpet is divided into two hyperbolic trumpets by cutting along the waist curve (see Figure
10
).
Each hyperbolic trumpet is also homeomorphic to a cylinder, and
one of two boundaries is the wiggly boundary which obeys the boundary condition (
A.1
) for the JT gravity fields.
Another boundary is the geodesic boundary, and we choose its length to be
L
.
In
[
88
,
91
]
, a striking formula of the trumpet partition function
Z_{\mathrm{trumpet}}^{\mathrm{JT}}(\beta,L)
is obtained

\displaystyle Z_{\mathrm{trumpet}}^{\mathrm{JT}}(\beta,L)=\frac{\gamma^{\frac{1}{2}}}{(2\pi)^{\frac{1}{2}}\beta^{\frac{1}{2}}}\mathrm{e}^{-\frac{\gamma}{2}\frac{L^{2}}{\beta}}.

Gluing two hyperbolic trumpets along the geodesic boundaries, one obtains the double trumpet, and
the gluing formula for the double trumpet partition function
Z_{0,2}^{\mathrm{JT}}(\beta,L)
is

\displaystyle Z_{0,2}^{\mathrm{JT}}(\beta_{1},\beta_{2})=\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}Z^{\mathrm{JT}}_{\mathrm{trumpet}}(\beta_{1},L)Z^{\mathrm{JT}}_{\mathrm{trumpet}}(\beta_{2},L)L{\rm d}L=\frac{\sqrt{\beta_{1}\beta_{2}}}{2\pi(\beta_{1}+\beta_{2})}.

(A.2)

The gluing formula is generalized to the genus
g
partition function with
n
boundaries in Figure
11
.

Figure 11:
A hyperbolic Riemann surface with genus
g
and
n
wiggly boundaries, which is constructed
by gluing
n
hyperbolic trumpets to the bordered hyperbolic Riemann surface.

The JT gravity partition function
Z_{g,n}^{\mathrm{JT}}(\beta_{1},\dots,\beta_{n})
of a genus
g
hyperbolic bordered Riemann surface with
n
wiggly boundaries obeys

\displaystyle Z_{g,n}^{\mathrm{JT}}(\beta_{1},\dots,\beta_{n})=\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n}}\left(\prod_{i=1}^{n}Z^{\mathrm{JT}}_{\mathrm{trumpet}}(\beta_{i},L_{i})\right)V_{g,n}^{\mathrm{WP}}(L_{1},\dots,L_{n})\prod_{i=1}^{n}L_{i}{\rm d}L_{i},

(A.3)

where
V_{g,n}^{\mathrm{WP}}(L_{1},\dots,L_{n})
denotes the Weil–Petersson volume of genus
g
bordered Riemann surface with boundary lengths
L_{1},\dots,L_{n}
.
In the path integral of the JT gravity, the Weil–Petersson volume arises from the path integral with respect to the metric
g_{\mu\nu}
and the dilaton field
\phi
on the bulk of bordered Riemann surfaces.

Weil–Petersson spectral curve from JT gravity.
The JT gravity partition function (
A.3
) is directly related to the connected correlation function
W_{g,n}^{\mathrm{WP}}(z_{1},\dots,z_{n})

of the CEO topological recursion for the Weil–Petersson volume.
Here we will focus on the derivation of the basic data, the
\mathsf{y}
-coordinate function in equation (
4.22
) and
the bidifferential
B
in equation (
4.4
) of
the Weil–Petersson spectral curve

\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{WP}}=\big({\mathbb{P}}^{1};\mathsf{x},\mathsf{y}^{\mathrm{WP}},B\big)

from the disk and double trumpet partition functions.

The
\mathsf{y}
-coordinate function is found from the disk partition function
Z_{0,1}^{\mathrm{JT}}(\beta)
rewritten in the form

\displaystyle Z_{0,1}^{\mathrm{JT}}(\beta)=\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\rho_{0}^{\mathrm{JT}}(E)\mathrm{e}^{-\beta E}{\rm d}E,

where
\rho_{0}^{\mathrm{JT}}(E)
denotes the genus zero density of states

\displaystyle\rho_{0}^{\mathrm{JT}}(E)=\frac{\gamma}{2\pi^{2}}\sinh\big(2\pi\sqrt{2\gamma E}\big).

By a change of variable
E=-z^{2}
and the analytic continuation, one finds
the
\mathsf{y}
-coordinate function
[
88
]

\displaystyle\mathsf{y}^{\mathrm{JT}}(z)=-\pi\mathrm{i}\rho_{0}^{\mathrm{JT}}(E)=\frac{\gamma}{2\pi}\sin\big(2\pi\sqrt{2\gamma}z\big).

Putting
\gamma=1/2
, we find the
\mathsf{y}
-coordinate function
\mathsf{y}^{\mathrm{WP}}(z)
in equation (
4.22
) up to an overall constant factor.

We now use a formula which relate the correlation function
W_{g,n}^{\mathrm{WP}}(z_{1},\dots,z_{n})
for
2g+2-n>0
of the CEO topological recursion for the Weil–Petersson volume
and the JT gravity partition function
Z_{g,n}^{\mathrm{JT}}(\beta_{1},\dots,\beta_{n})
with
\gamma=1/2
:

\displaystyle W_{g,n}^{\mathrm{WP}}(z_{1},\dots,z_{n})=2^{n}z_{1}\cdots z_{n}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n}}Z_{g,n}^{\mathrm{JT}}(\beta_{1},\dots,\beta_{n})\mathrm{e}^{-\sum_{i=1}^{n}\beta_{i}z_{i}^{2}}\prod_{i=1}^{n}{\rm d}\beta_{i}.

(A.4)

This formula is found from the Laplace dual relation between the Weil–Petersson volume
V^{\mathrm{WP}}(L_{1},\dots,L_{n})
and the correlation function
W_{g,n}^{\mathrm{WP}}(z_{1},\dots,z_{n})
. Here the integral formula involving
Z_{\mathrm{trumpet}}^{\mathrm{JT}}(\beta,L)
,

\displaystyle 2z\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}Z_{\mathrm{trumpet}}^{\mathrm{JT}}(\beta,L)\mathrm{e}^{-\beta z^{2}}{\rm d}\beta=2z\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\frac{\gamma^{\frac{1}{2}}}{(2\pi)^{\frac{1}{2}}\beta^{\frac{1}{2}}}\mathrm{e}^{-\beta z^{2}-\frac{\gamma}{2}\frac{L^{2}}{\beta}}{\rm d}\beta=(2\gamma)^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathrm{e}^{-\sqrt{2\gamma}Lz},

(A.5)

is applied to equation (
A.3
).
The bidifferential
B
in equation (
4.4
) is found by applying the above formula to equation (
A.2
),

\displaystyle 2^{2}z_{1}z_{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2}}Z_{0,2}^{\mathrm{JT}}(\beta_{1},\beta_{2})\mathrm{e}^{-\beta_{1}z_{1}^{2}-\beta_{2}z_{2}^{2}}{\rm d}\beta_{1}{\rm d}\beta_{2}=\frac{1}{(z_{1}+z_{2})^{2}},

(A.6)

and this agrees with the regularized
(g,n)=(0,2)
correlation function
[
36
]
given by

\displaystyle B(z_{1},z_{2})-\frac{{\rm d}\mathsf{x}(z_{1})\otimes{\rm d}\mathsf{x}(z_{2})}{\left(\mathsf{x}(z_{1})-\mathsf{x}(z_{2})\right)^{2}}=\frac{{\rm d}z_{1}\otimes{\rm d}z_{2}}{(z_{1}-z_{2})^{2}}-\frac{4z_{1}z_{2}{\rm d}z_{1}\otimes{\rm d}z_{2}}{(z_{1}^{2}-z_{2}^{2})^{2}}=\frac{{\rm d}z_{1}\otimes{\rm d}z_{2}}{(z_{1}+z_{2})^{2}}.

Thus the basic data of the Weil–Petersson spectral curve
\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{WP}}=\big({\mathbb{P}}^{1};\mathsf{x},\mathsf{y}^{\mathrm{WP}},B\big)
are found from the JT gravity partition functions.

A.2
Including a scalar field and the Masur–Veech type twist

Here we introduce an extra scalar field with mass
m
coupled to the JT gravity fields.
The partition function of the scalar field
Z_{\mathrm{scalar}}(L;\Delta)
on the hyperbolic trumpet with a geodesic boundary of length
L
is found in
[
54
]
via the heat kernel method,

\displaystyle Z_{\mathrm{scalar}}(L;\Delta)={}

\displaystyle\prod_{p\geq 0}\frac{1}{1-\mathrm{e}^{-L(\Delta+p)}}=\exp\Bigg(\sum_{w\geq 1}\frac{\mathrm{e}^{-wL\Delta}}{w\big(1-\mathrm{e}^{-wL}\big)}\Bigg),

(A.7)

where
23
23

23

When the bulk scalar field is associated to an operator defined in the boundary field theory on the Riemann surface,
the quantity
\Delta
is identified with its scaling dimension (see, e.g.,
[
1
]
).

\displaystyle\Delta=\frac{1}{2}+\sqrt{\frac{1}{4}+m^{2}}.

The partition function of the scalar coupled JT gravity is computed by introducing the scalar partition factor

Z_{\mathrm{scalar}}(L;\Delta)
for each of the closed geodesics of the hyperbolic bordered Riemann surface with wiggly boundaries in the gluing formula.
Namely,
Z_{\mathrm{scalar}}(L)
is regarded as a twist function of the ABO topological recursion.
In particular, we focus on a sector of the scalar field path integral
in the exponential formula of equation (
A.7
),

\displaystyle\mathsf{f}_{w}(L;\Delta):=\frac{\mathrm{e}^{-\Delta wL}}{w(1-\mathrm{e}^{-wL})}=\frac{1}{w}\sum_{k\geq 0}\mathrm{e}^{-(k+\Delta)wL}.

This sector in the scalar partition function comes from
path integral contributions of the closed world lines of the scalar field wrapping
w
times around a closed geodesic of length
L
.
In particular, by considering the
w=1
sector for
a massless scalar field with
\Delta=1
(
m=0
),
we find the Masur–Veech type twist function in equation (
2.42
),

\displaystyle\mathsf{f}_{w=1}(L;1)=\mathsf{f}^{\mathrm{MV}}(L)=\frac{1}{\mathrm{e}^{L}-1}.

Thus we get a physical interpretation of the Masur–Veech type twist from the physical arguments based on the JT gravity.

Remark A.1
.

The generalized Masur–Veech type twist function
\mathsf{f}^{\mathrm{g\mathchar 45MV}}
in equation (
2.44
),
normalized by the winding number
w
, is
also found as a sector of equation (
A.7
)
for the massless scalar field,

\displaystyle\mathsf{f}_{w}(L;1)=\frac{1}{w}\mathsf{f}^{\mathrm{g\mathchar 45MV}}(L;w)=\frac{1}{w(\mathrm{e}^{wL}-1)}.

Twisted Weil–Petersson spectral curve from scalar coupled JT gravity.
Based on the above physical interpretation in the JT gravity,
we will find the bidifferential

B[\mathsf{f}^{\mathrm{MV}}](z_{1},z_{2})
in equation (
4.51
)
with the Masur–Veech type twist, and recover
the twisted Weil–Petersson spectral curve
\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{WP}}\big[\mathsf{f}^{\mathrm{MV}}\big]=\big({\mathbb{P}}^{1};\mathsf{x},\mathsf{y}^{\mathrm{WP}},B\big[\mathsf{f}^{\mathrm{MV}}\big]\big)
.
We start from the scalar coupled JT gravity partition function
Z_{0,2}^{\mathrm{JT\mathchar 45scalar}}(\beta_{1},\beta_{2})
on the double trumpet
that is found by gluing two partition functions for the hyperbolic trumpets and introducing the scalar partition function along a simple closed geodesic with geodesic length
L
as depicted in Figure
12

[
54
]
,

Figure 12:
A simple closed geodesic with geodesic length
L
in a hyperbolic double trumpet.

\displaystyle Z_{0,2}^{\mathrm{JT\mathchar 45scalar}}(\beta_{1},\beta_{2})=\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}Z_{\mathrm{trumpet}}^{\mathrm{JT}}(\beta_{1},L)Z_{\mathrm{scalar}}(L;\Delta)Z_{\mathrm{trumpet}}^{\mathrm{JT}}(\beta_{2},L)L{\rm d}L.

(A.8)

We now replace
Z_{\mathrm{scalar}}(L;\Delta)
by the Masur–Veech type twist function
\mathsf{f}^{\mathrm{MV}}(L)
for the
L
-integral in the right-hand side of equation (
A.8
).
The sum of the twisted and untwisted partition functions denoted by
Z_{0,2}^{\mathrm{JT}}\big[\mathsf{f}^{\mathrm{MV}}\big](\beta_{1},\beta_{2})
is

\displaystyle Z_{0,2}^{\mathrm{JT}}\big[\mathsf{f}^{\mathrm{MV}}\big](\beta_{1},\beta_{2})=\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}Z_{\mathrm{trumpet}}^{\mathrm{JT}}(\beta_{1},L)\left(1+\mathsf{f}^{\mathrm{MV}}(L)\right)Z_{\mathrm{trumpet}}^{\mathrm{JT}}(\beta_{2},L)L{\rm d}L.

(A.9)

Adopting equation (
A.5
) with
\gamma=1/2
,
for
\mathrm{Re}(z_{1}+z_{2})>0
, we find

\displaystyle 2^{2}z_{1}z_{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2}}Z_{0,2}^{\mathrm{JT}}\big[\mathsf{f}^{\mathrm{MV}}\big](\beta_{1},\beta_{2})\mathrm{e}^{-\beta_{1}z_{1}^{2}-\beta_{2}z_{2}^{2}}{\rm d}\beta_{1}{\rm d}\beta_{2}

\displaystyle\qquad=\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\mathrm{e}^{-L(z_{1}+z_{2})}\left(1+\frac{1}{\mathrm{e}^{L}-1}\right)L{\rm d}L=\frac{1}{(z_{1}+z_{2})^{2}}+\sum_{\mathfrak{m}\geq 1}\frac{1}{(z_{1}+z_{2}+\mathfrak{m})^{2}}

\displaystyle\qquad=\zeta(2;z_{1}+z_{2}),

where
\zeta(2;z)
is known as the generalized zeta function. By an analytic continuation to
z\in\mathbb{C}\setminus\mathbb{Z}
,
\zeta(2;z)
is replaced by the Hurwitz zeta function
\zeta_{\mathrm{H}}(2;z)
in equation (
4.52
).
By changing the signature
z_{2}\to-z_{2}
to obtain the correlation function between two points in the same branch of the double cover of the spectral curve,
we find the twisted bidifferential

B[\mathsf{f}^{\mathrm{MV}}](z_{1},z_{2})
in equation (
4.51
).
Thus, the basic data of the twisted Weil–Petersson spectral curve
\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{WP}}\big[\mathsf{f}^{\mathrm{MV}}\big]=\big({\mathbb{P}}^{1};\mathsf{x},\mathsf{y}^{\mathrm{WP}},B\big[\mathsf{f}^{\mathrm{MV}}\big]\big)
are also found from the physical arguments of the scalar coupled JT gravity.

As a generalization of the double trumpet partition function (
A.9
), we consider the twisted JT gravity partition function of a genus
g
hyperbolic bordered Riemann surface with
n
wiggly boundaries (see Figure
13
) twisted by the Masur–Veech type twist function:

Figure 13:
Closed geodesics on a hyperbolic Riemann surface with genus
g
and
n
wiggly boundaries.

\displaystyle Z_{g,n}^{\mathrm{JT}}\big[\mathsf{f}^{\mathrm{MV}}\big](\beta_{1},\dots,\beta_{n})={}

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n}}\left(\prod_{i=1}^{n}\mathsf{f}^{\mathrm{MV}}(L_{i})Z^{\mathrm{JT}}_{\mathrm{trumpet}}(\beta_{i},L_{i})\right)

\displaystyle\times V^{\mathrm{WP}}_{g,n}\big[\mathsf{f}^{\mathrm{MV}}\big](L_{1},\dots,L_{n})\prod_{i=1}^{n}L_{i}{\rm d}L_{i},

(A.10)

where
V^{\mathrm{WP}}_{g,n}\big[\mathsf{f}^{\mathrm{MV}}\big](L_{1},\dots,L_{n})
is the solution of the ABO topological recursion of the Weil–Petersson volume with the Masur–Veech type twist, i.e., the twisted Weil–Petersson volume.
The ABO topological recursion twisted by
\mathsf{f}^{\mathrm{MV}}
is equivalent to the Laplace dual of the CEO topological recursion for the twisted Weil–Petersson spectral curve
\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{WP}}\big[\mathsf{f}^{\mathrm{MV}}\big]
.

Remark A.2
.

Here we comment on a physical observation of the twist-elimination map introduced in Section
4.4
in terms of the twisted JT gravity partition functions.
To extract the twisted Weil–Petersson volume from the twisted JT gravity partition function,
we should eliminate the twist factors
\mathsf{f}^{\mathrm{MV}}(L_{i})
and the trumpet partition functions
Z^{\mathrm{JT}}_{\mathrm{trumpet}}(\beta_{i},L_{i})
in equation (
A.10
).
Such manipulation is geometrically interpreted as follows.
By the physical argument, similar to the relation (
A.4
),
the twisted JT gravity partition function
Z_{g,n}^{\mathrm{JT}}\big[\mathsf{f}^{\mathrm{MV}}\big]
and the twisted correlation function
W_{g,n}^{\mathrm{WP}}\big[\mathsf{f}^{\mathrm{MV}}\big]
of the CEO topological recursion are
related by an integral transform
\mathcal{I}\left(Z_{g,n}^{\mathrm{WP}}\big[\mathsf{f}^{\mathrm{MV}}\big]\right)=W_{g,n}^{\mathrm{WP}}\big[\mathsf{f}^{\mathrm{MV}}\big]
such that

\displaystyle W_{g,n}^{\mathrm{WP}}\big[\mathsf{f}^{\mathrm{MV}}\big](z_{1},\dots,z_{n})=2^{n}z_{1}\cdots z_{n}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n}}Z_{g,n}^{\mathrm{JT}}\big[\mathsf{f}^{\mathrm{MV}}\big](\beta_{1},\dots,\beta_{n})\mathrm{e}^{-\sum_{i=1}^{n}\beta_{i}z_{i}^{2}}\prod_{i=1}^{n}{\rm d}\beta_{i}.

In the geometric picture, this integral transform
\mathcal{I}
replaces the hyperbolic trumpet in the Riemann surface with wiggly boundaries
by the marked points
depicted as the left arrow in Figure
14
(see
[
86
]
for physical discussions on this geometrical picture for this integral transform).
Furthermore, the twist-elimination map
\mathcal{E}
eliminates the twist factors associated to the boundaries of the bordered Riemann surface
in the twisted correlation function
W_{g,n}^{\mathrm{WP}}\big[\mathsf{f}^{\mathrm{MV}}\big]
.
Finally, adopting the inverse Laplace transform
\mathcal{L}^{-1}
, we obtain the twisted Weil–Petersson volume
V_{g,n}^{\mathrm{WP}}\big[\mathsf{f}^{\mathrm{MV}}\big]
with boundary length variables
L_{i}
.

Figure 14:
A geometrical interpretation of the integral transform
\mathcal{I}
and the twist-elimination map
\mathcal{E}
involved with the inverse Laplace transform
\mathcal{L}^{-1}
.

A.3
Liouville gravity and
\boldsymbol{(2,p)}
minimal string

Consider a matter CFT coupled to the ghost sector and Liouville CFT
which is referred to as the
Liouville gravity
.
When the matter CFT is the minimal model CFT labeled by a pair of relatively prime integers
(p,p^{\prime})
, the Liouville gravity yields
the
(p,p^{\prime})

minimal string

which is a class of non-critical string theory.
In particular, the
(2,p)
minimal string with any odd positive integers
p
is
found by specializing the Liouville parameter
b
in the Liouville gravity as
b=\sqrt{\frac{2}{p}}
.
It is pointed out in
[
88
]
that the partition function of the
(2,p)
minimal string leads to
the partition function of the JT gravity in the
p\to\infty
limit.
Detailed studies on the relation between the Liouville gravity and the JT gravity in terms of the topological expansion of the partition functions
are found in
[
67
]
.

The Liouville gravity partition function on the two-dimensional surface with a simple boundary condition
[
39
,
92
]

which fixes the boundary cosmological constant
\mu_{\mathrm{B}}
is studied in
[
33
,
73
,
89
]
.
Such a boundary condition is known as the FZZT boundary condition.
The inverse Laplace transform of the Liouville gravity partition function with the FZZT boundary conditions gives
the correlation function of macroscopic loop operators of the Liouville gravity, which is the Liouville gravity partition function of the two-dimensional surface with fixed boundary lengths.
In
[
88
]
, it is pointed out that the JT gravity partition function on the hyperbolic surface with wiggly boundaries
coincides with the Liouville gravity partition function with fixed boundary lengths in the
b\to 0
limit. (Detailed derivations are found in
[
67
]
.)

The disk partition function
Z^{\mathrm{L}(b)}_{0,1}(\ell)
of the Liouville gravity with a fixed boundary length
\ell
is given by the integral transform of the density of states
\rho_{0}^{\mathrm{L}(b)}(E)

[
74
]
as
24
24

24

The disk partition function of the Liouville gravity in the JT gravity notation is found such as
[
88
, equation (150)]
and
[
67
, equation (3.11)]
.

\displaystyle Z^{\mathrm{L}(b)}_{0,1}(\ell)\sim\int_{\kappa}^{\infty}\mathrm{e}^{-\ell E}\rho_{0}^{\mathrm{L}(b)}(E){\rm d}E,\qquad\rho_{0}^{\mathrm{L}(b)}(E)=\frac{1}{4\pi^{2}}\sinh\left(\frac{1}{b^{2}}\mathrm{arccosh}\left(\frac{E}{\kappa}\right)\right),

where
\sim
implies a multiplication of a constant factor
N(b)
and a bulk cosmological factor
\mu^{1/(2b^{2})}
(cf.
[
67
, equation (3.9)]
),
and the parameter
\kappa
is given by
\kappa^{2}=\mu/\sin(\pi b^{2})
.
By rescaling the energy
E
and the boundary length
\ell
as
25
25

25

The boundary length
\ell
of the Liouville gravity is identified as the Euclidean time of the JT gravity in this coincidence. Due to this identification, we use the inverse temperature
\beta
for the boundary length
\ell
in the Liouville gravity partition function.

\displaystyle E=\kappa\big(1+2\pi^{2}b^{4}E_{\mathrm{JT}}\big),\qquad\ell=\frac{\beta}{2\pi^{2}\kappa b^{4}},

we obtain the partition function of the JT gravity in the
b\to 0
limit.
By an analytic continuation of the genus zero density of states
\rho_{0}^{\mathrm{L}(b)}(E)
with
E=\kappa\big(1-2\pi^{2}b^{4}z^{2}\big)
, we find the
\mathsf{y}
-coordinate function

\displaystyle\mathsf{y}^{\mathrm{L}(b)}(z)=-\pi\mathrm{i}\rho_{0}^{\mathrm{L}(b)}(E)=\frac{1}{2\pi}\sin\left(\frac{1}{b^{2}}\mathrm{arccos}\big(1-2\pi^{2}b^{4}z^{2}\big)\right),

which defines the Liouville gravity spectral curve
\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{L}(b)}
.
In particular for
b=\sqrt{2/p}
, the coordinate function
\mathsf{y}^{\mathrm{L}(b)}(z)
yields
the coordinate function
\mathsf{y}^{\mathrm{M}(p)}(z)
in equation (
4.25
) of the
{(2,p)}
minimal string spectral curve

\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{M}(p)}=\big({\mathbb{P}}^{1};\mathsf{x},\mathsf{y}^{\mathrm{M}(p)},B\big)
.
26
26

26

The coordinate function is also found in
[
89
]
from the ground ring relation
[
96
]
of the tachyon module.

Now we consider two specializations of the parameter
p
.
One specialization is the limit
p\to\infty
. In this limit, the minimal string reduces to the JT gravity,
and the coordinate functions (
4.22
)
of the Weil–Petersson spectral curve
{\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{WP}}=\big({\mathbb{P}}^{1};\mathsf{x},\mathsf{y}^{\mathrm{WP}},B\big)}
are recovered.
Another specialization is
p=1
.
In this case, the minimal string is identified with the topological gravity
whose matter CFT has central charge
c=-2

[
27
,
43
,
44
]
,
and the coordinate functions (
4.8
)
of the Airy spectral curve
{\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{A}}=\big({\mathbb{P}}^{1};\mathsf{x},\mathsf{y}^{\mathrm{A}},B\big)}

for the Kontsevich–Witten theory are recovered.
In
[
47
]
, the CEO topological recursion for the spectral curve
\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{M}(p)}
is
studied , and the non-perturbative behavior of the minimal string theory is discussed in great detail.

Next, we consider the cylinder partition function of the Liouville gravity with fixed boundary lengths
\ell_{1}
,
\ell_{2}
to find the bidifferential of the spectral curve.
From the detailed analysis
of the two point function of the Liouville gravity, a gluing formula
for the cylinder partition function
Z^{\mathrm{L}(b)}_{0,2}(\ell_{1},\ell_{2})
is obtained in
[
63
,
67
]
such that

\displaystyle Z^{\mathrm{L}(b)}_{0,2}(\ell_{1},\ell_{2})=\frac{2}{\pi}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\tanh(\pi\lambda)K_{\mathrm{i}\lambda}(\kappa\ell_{1})K_{\mathrm{i}\lambda}(\kappa\ell_{2})\lambda{\rm d}\lambda=\frac{\sqrt{\ell_{1}\ell_{2}}}{\ell_{1}+\ell_{2}}\mathrm{e}^{-\kappa(\ell_{1}+\ell_{2})},

(A.11)

where
K_{s}(\ell)
denotes the modified Bessel function of the second kind, and a formula of the Kontorovich–Lebedev transform is adopted to obtain this result (e.g., see
[
34
, Section 12.1]
).
In the Liouville gravity,
the trumpet partition function
Z^{\mathrm{L}(b)}_{\mathrm{trumpet}}(\ell,\lambda)

is computed as the bulk one point function of the disk with a fixed boundary length
\ell
, and given by (see
[
67
, equations (4.8) and (7.57)]
),

\displaystyle Z^{\mathrm{L}(b)}_{\mathrm{trumpet}}(\ell,\lambda)=K_{\mathrm{i}\lambda}(\kappa\ell).

(A.12)

Then, the formula (
A.11
) is considered as an analogue of the gluing formula (
A.2
) for the double trumpet in the JT gravity.
Performing the following integral transform for the
(g,n)=(0,2)
correlation function
Z^{\mathrm{L}(b)}_{0,2}(\ell_{1},\ell_{2})
with

E_{i}=\kappa\big(1-2\pi^{2}b^{4}z_{i}^{2}\big)

(i=1,2)
:

\displaystyle\frac{2^{2}\big(2\pi^{2}b^{4}\kappa\big)^{2}z_{1}z_{2}}{2\pi}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2}}Z^{\mathrm{L}(b)}_{0,2}(\ell_{1},\ell_{2})\mathrm{e}^{\ell_{1}E_{1}+\ell_{2}E_{2}}{\rm d}\ell_{1}{\rm d}\ell_{2}=\frac{1}{(z_{1}+z_{2})^{2}},

(A.13)

we obtain the same answer as the
(g,n)=(0,2)
correlation function (
A.6
) of the JT gravity.
Consequently, by a change of signature
z_{2}\to-z_{2}
of equation (
A.13
),
we find the bidifferential
B
in equation (
1.3
) for the Liouville gravity spectral curve.
Thus, the basic data of the Liouville gravity spectral curve
\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{L}(b)}=\big({\mathbb{P}}^{1};\mathsf{x},\mathsf{y}^{\mathrm{L}(b)},B\big)

are derived from the Liouville gravity partition function, and the spectral curve
\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{L}(b)}
reduces to the
(2,p)
minimal string spectral curve
\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{M}(p)}=\big({\mathbb{P}}^{1};\mathsf{x},\mathsf{y}^{\mathrm{M}(p)},B\big)
in Table
1
by the specialization
b=\sqrt{2/p}
.

Rescaling the parameters in the Liouville gravity such that

\displaystyle\lambda=\frac{L}{2\pi b^{2}},\qquad\ell=\frac{\beta}{2\kappa\pi^{2}b^{4}},

we recover the JT gravity results in the
b\to 0
limit.
Indeed the trumpet partition function (
A.12
)
and the measure factor in equation (
A.11
) reduces to those of the JT gravity in this scaling limit
[
67
]
:

\displaystyle\mathrm{e}^{\kappa\ell}Z^{\mathrm{L}(b)}_{\mathrm{trumpet}}(\beta,\lambda)

\displaystyle\to\pi b^{2}\sqrt{\frac{\pi}{\beta}}\mathrm{e}^{-\frac{L^{2}}{4\beta}},\qquad\tanh(\pi\lambda)\lambda{\rm d}\lambda\to\frac{1}{4\pi^{2}b^{4}}L{\rm d}L,

and the Liouville gravity partition function reduces to the JT gravity partition function.

Having obtained a set of basic data of the CEO topological recursion,
we can consider an analogue of the Weil–Petersson volume for the
(2,p)
minimal string.
Let
V^{\mathrm{L}(b)}_{g,n}(L_{1},\dots,L_{n})
be the volume polynomial
which is the Laplace dual of the solution of the CEO topological recursion for the Liouville gravity spectral curve
\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{L}(b)}
.
By the specialization
b=\sqrt{2/p}
, the volume polynomial
V^{\mathrm{L}(b)}_{g,n}(L_{1},\dots,L_{n})
reduces to the volume polynomial

V^{\mathrm{M}(p)}_{g,n}(L_{1},\dots,L_{n})
for the
(2,p)
minimal string.
27
27

27

The
p
-deformed Weil–Petersson volume is computed independently on the basis of
the
(2,p)
minimal string in
[
67
, Section 7]
, and
we find agreements between the volume polynomials
V^{\mathrm{M}(p)}_{g,n}(L_{1},\dots,L_{n})
in (
C.2
) and the
p
-deformed Weil–Petersson volumes
for
(g,n)=(0,4)
and
(1,1)
under a change of parameters as
b_{i}^{2}=L_{i}^{2}-4\pi^{2}/p^{2}

(see version 5 of
[
67
]
in arXiv).

In particular, for
p=\infty
(resp.
p=1
),
the volume polynomial
V^{\mathrm{M}(p)}_{g,n}(L_{1},\dots,L_{n})
reduces to
the Weil–Petersson volume
V^{\mathrm{WP}}_{g,n}(L_{1},\dots,L_{n})

(resp. the Kontsevich–Witten symplectic volume
V^{\mathrm{A}}_{g,n}(L_{1},\dots,L_{n})
)
which appears in the path integral for the JT gravity (resp. topological gravity) partition functions.
To establish the
p\to\infty
limit, the non-perturbative study of
the
(2,p)
minimal string is also necessary,
and such aspect is addressed in
[
47
]
.

A.4
JT supergravity

Supersymmetric generalizations
28
28

28

A supersymmetric extension of the McShane identity is studied independently in
[
51
]
on the basis of Bowditch’s approach
[
14
]
by Markoff triples.

of Mirzakhani’s recursion relation
[
69
,
70
]
are given by Stanford–Witten’s work
[
91
]
from the study of the JT supergravity defined on a bordered super Riemann surface.
Mathematical aspects of the volume of the moduli space of super hyperbolic surfaces and the CEO topological recursion formalism are further studied in
[
79
]
.
The super Riemann surface is a Riemann surface equipped with a spin structure, and
the partition function of the
\mathcal{N}=1
supersymmetric JT gravity (i.e., JT supergravity abbreviated by SJT) is given by the integral over the moduli space of Riemann surfaces involving the sum over spin structures
[
91
]
.
The topological expansion of the JT supergravity partition function with respect to the underlying Riemann surfaces
is found in the parallel way as the (bosonic) JT gravity, and
a classification of fermionic and non-orientable extensions of the JT gravity is discussed in
[
91
]
.

The basic data for the topological expansion of a fermionic/(non-)orientable JT gravity partition function are also found essentially from the disk, trumpet and double trumpet partition functions.
In the strategy of Stanford–Witten’s work, the disk and trumpet partition functions of the fermionic/(non-)orientable JT gravity are studied on the basis of the dual SYK-like models.
Furthermore, the fermionic/non-orientable JT gravity partition function has a description by a random matrix integral, and
a correspondence between the classification of the fermionic/(non-)orientable JT gravity and
the Dyson
\beta
-ensembles
[
32
]

or the Altland–Zirnbauer
(\alpha,\beta)
-ensembles
[
7
]

is established as Tables 1–4 of
[
91
]
.

The disk and trumpet partition functions for the JT supergravity are
29
29

29

For the disk and trumpet partition functions
Z^{\mathrm{D}}_{\mathrm{SJT}}(\beta)
,
Z^{\mathrm{T}}_{\mathrm{SJT}}(\beta,L)
computed in
[
91
, Appendix C]
from the boundary super Schwarzian path integrals,
a factor
1/2
is multiplied to employ the matrix model normalization
[
82
]
:
Z_{0,1}^{\mathrm{SJT}}(\beta)=\frac{1}{2}Z^{\mathrm{D}}_{\mathrm{SJT}}(\beta)
,

Z_{\mathrm{trumpet}}^{\mathrm{SJT}}(\beta,L)=\frac{1}{2}Z^{\mathrm{T}}_{\mathrm{SJT}}(\beta,L)
.

\displaystyle Z_{0,1}^{\mathrm{SJT}}(\beta)=\sqrt{\frac{1}{2\pi\beta}}\mathrm{e}^{\frac{\pi^{2}}{\beta}},\qquad Z_{\mathrm{trumpet}}^{\mathrm{SJT}}(\beta,L)=\frac{1}{2\sqrt{2\pi\beta}}\mathrm{e}^{-\frac{L^{2}}{4\beta}},

where the parameters
\gamma
and
\mathrm{e}^{S_{0}}
of the super Schwarzian path integrals in
[
91
, Appendix C]
are fixed to be
1/2

and
1
, respectively.
From the disk partition function
Z_{0,1}^{\mathrm{SJT}}(\beta)
, as was done in the JT gravity, we obtain the coordinate functions

\mathsf{x}(z)
and
\mathsf{y}^{\mathrm{SWP}}(z)
in equation (
4.37
) of
the super Weil–Petersson spectral curve
\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{SWP}}
,
where we employed a normalization given in
[
79
]
.
Gluing two trumpet partition functions along the geodesic circle of length
L
, we obtain the double trumpet function

\displaystyle Z_{0,2}^{\mathrm{SJT}}(\beta_{1},\beta_{2})=2\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}Z_{\mathrm{trumpet}}^{\mathrm{SJT}}(\beta_{1},L)Z_{\mathrm{trumpet}}^{\mathrm{SJT}}(\beta_{2},L)L{\rm d}L=\frac{\sqrt{\beta_{1}\beta_{2}}}{2\pi(\beta_{1}+\beta_{2})},

where we assume the JT supergravity on orientable surfaces without time-reversal symmetry,
and the factor
2
in front of the middle integral arises from the sum over the spin structures.
The JT supergravity partition function on an orientable surface with genus
g
and
n
wiggly boundaries without time-reversal symmetry is

\displaystyle Z^{\mathrm{SJT}}_{g,n}(\beta_{1},\dots,\beta_{n})=\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n}}\left(\prod_{i=1}^{n}2Z_{\mathrm{trumpet}}^{\mathrm{SJT}}(\beta_{i},L_{i})\right)V_{g,n}^{\mathrm{SW}}(L_{1},\dots,L_{n})\prod_{i=1}^{n}L_{i}{\rm d}L_{i},

(A.14)

where
V_{g,n}^{\mathrm{SW}}(L_{1},\dots,L_{n})
denotes the Weil–Petersson volume for the moduli space of super Riemann surfaces
computed from the supersymmetric generalization of Mirzakhani’s recursion in
[
91
, Appendix D]
.

From this double trumpet partition function, we also obtain the bidifferential
B(z_{1},z_{2})
of the super Weil–Petersson spectral curve
\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{SWP}}
in the same way as the JT gravity,
and the super Weil–Petersson spectral curve
\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{SWP}}=\big({\mathbb{P}}^{1};\mathsf{x},\mathsf{y}^{\mathrm{SWP}},B\big)
is obtained.
Then, the correlation functions
W^{\mathrm{SWP}}_{g,n}(z_{1},\dots,z_{n})
are
obtained from the CEO topological recursion, and
their inverse Laplace transforms give
the volume polynomials
V_{g,n}^{\mathrm{SWP}}(L_{1},\dots,L_{n})
of the moduli space of super Riemann surfaces which are the supersymmetric analogue of the Weil–Petersson volumes
V_{g,n}^{\mathrm{WP}}(L_{1},\dots,L_{n})

and obey the ABO topological recursion.
The Weil–Petersson volume for the moduli space of super Riemann surfaces in the JT supergravity partition function (
A.14
) and the super Weil–Petersson volume defined in equation (
2.25
) are related by
[
79
, Section 5.3]

\displaystyle V_{g,n}^{\mathrm{SW}}(L_{1},\dots,L_{n})=(-1)^{n}2^{1-g}V_{g,n}^{\mathrm{SWP}}(L_{1},\dots,L_{n}).

A.5
Type 0A minimal superstring

It is conjectured in
[
82
]
that the Bessel generating function
Z^{\mathrm{B}}(\hbar;\textbf{t})
in equation (
5.24
) is obtained from the string equation
[
20
,
21
,
76
]
for the complex matrix model which gives a non-perturbative definition of the free energy of type 0A minimal superstring
[
58
]
.
30
30

30

Proofs of the correspondence between the (generalized) BGW free energy and Kontsevich–Witten free energy
is given in
[
100
,
101
]
.

In particular, when a finite number of variables
t_{a}
is turned on such that

t_{a}\neq 0
(
1\leq a\leq(p-1)/2
),
the string equation gives the free energy of type 0A
(2,2p-2)
minimal superstring.
31
31

31

For the JT supergravity, the free energy is found from the string equation by tuning an infinite number of
t_{a}
’s.
The role of the string equation in the JT supergravity is discussed in
[
55
,
56
,
57
]
.

Heuristically we consider a spectral curve
which interpolates the super Weil–Petersson spectral curve
\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{SWP}}
and the Bessel spectral curve

\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{B}}
with coordinate functions in equation (
4.29
).
We obtain the
z\mathsf{y}^{\mathrm{SWP}}(z)
and
z\mathsf{y}^{\mathrm{B}}(z)
by differentiating
\mathsf{y}^{\mathrm{WP}}(z)
and
\mathsf{y}^{\mathrm{A}}(z)

of the spectral curves for the Weil--Petersson volumes and Kontsevich--Witten symplectic volumes in the bosonic models
32
32

32

This property will be originated from the fact that the supersymmetric formulae for the Mirzakhani–McShane identity found in
[
91
]

allows a superfield representation with fermionic coordinates.

\displaystyle\mathsf{y}^{\mathrm{SWP}}(z)=\frac{1}{z}\frac{{\rm d}\mathsf{y}^{\mathrm{WP}}(z)}{{\rm d}z},\qquad\mathsf{y}^{\mathrm{B}}(z)=\frac{1}{z}\frac{{\rm d}\mathsf{y}^{\mathrm{A}}(z)}{{\rm d}z}.

Adopting this heuristic relation to the
\mathsf{y}
-coordinate in equation (
4.25
)
of the
(2,p)
minimal string spectral curve
\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{M}(p)}
,
we find a supersymmetric analogue of the
(2,p)
minimal string
spectral curve
\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{SM}(p)}
with
the coordinate functions in equation (
4.40
).
The cut-and-join equation obtained from the spectral curve
\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{SM}(p)}

is studied in Section
5.3.3
,
and we find that the generating function

Z^{\mathrm{SM}(p)}(\hbar;\textbf{t})
in equation (
5.31
)
is obtained by the shift (
5.32
) for
the finite number of variables
t_{a}
in
the Bessel generating function
Z^{\mathrm{B}}(\hbar;\textbf{t})
:

\displaystyle t_{a}\to t_{a}+\gamma_{a}\qquad\textrm{with}\quad\begin{cases}\displaystyle\gamma_{a}=-\frac{(-2\pi^{2})^{a}}{(2a+1)!!a!}\prod_{i=1}^{a}\left(1-\frac{(2i-1)^{2}}{p^{2}}\right)&\textrm{for}\ 1\leq a\leq\frac{p-1}{2},\\
\gamma_{a}=0&\textrm{for others}.\end{cases}

Accordingly, we can regard

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{B}}(\beta_{1})\cdots\widehat{\mathcal{B}}(\beta_{n})Z^{\mathrm{SM}(p)}(\hbar;\textbf{t})\Big|_{t_{a}=0}=\widehat{\mathcal{B}}(\beta_{1})\cdots\widehat{\mathcal{B}}(\beta_{n})Z^{\mathrm{B}}(\hbar;\textbf{t})\Big|_{t_{a}=\gamma_{a}}

with an operator

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{B}}(\beta)=\sum_{a\geq 0}\beta^{a}\frac{\partial}{\partial t_{a}},

as a correlation function of type 0A
(2,2p-2)
minimal superstring,
where the operator
\widehat{\mathcal{B}}(\beta)
is considered as a boundary creation operator
[
81
,
82
,
83
]
with a variable
\beta
associated to a boundary.
Although we do not know the appropriate boundary condition for the disk partition function of type 0A
(2,2p-2)
minimal superstring
which leads to the spectral curve
\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{SM}(p)}
,
from these physical observations, we expect that
the spectral curve
\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{SM}(p)}

is obtained from the brane partition functions in type 0A
(2,2p-2)
minimal superstring.
33
33

33

The boundary condition of the branes in type 0A minimal superstring should be clarified from the matrix models and super Liouville field theories (e.g.,
[
89
]
).

Appendix B
Derivation of the ABO topological recursion data

Originally, a derivation of the CEO topological recursion for
\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{WP}}
is given explicitly in
[
38
]
by
Eynard and Orantin
from Mirzakhani’s topological recursion for the Weil–Petersson volume.
We apply their computation to the
(2,p)
minimal string and the
(2,2p-2)
minimal superstring in a reverse way (i.e., inverse Laplace transform of the CEO topological recursion), and derive the ABO topological recursion data given by
the kernel functions
H^{\mathrm{M}(p)}(x,y)
in equation (
2.19
)
and
H^{\mathrm{SM}(p)}(x,y)
in equation (
2.31
) explicitly.

We focus on the spectral curves
\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{M}(p)}
and
\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{SM}(p)}
,
and start from a partially (inverse) Laplace transformed CEO topological recursion for
2g-2+n>1
:

\displaystyle 2\widetilde{W}_{g,n}(z,L_{K})={}

\displaystyle\underset{w=0}{\mathrm{Res}}\Biggl[\frac{{\rm d}w}{\mathsf{y}(w)}\frac{1}{z^{2}-w^{2}}\Biggl(\widetilde{Q}_{g,n}(w,w,L_{K})

\displaystyle+\sum_{m=2}^{n}2\widetilde{W}_{0,2}(w,L_{m})\widetilde{W}_{g,n-1}(w,L_{K\setminus\{m\}})\Biggr)\Biggr],

(B.1)

where
K=\{2,\dots,n\}
.
In this recursion,
\widetilde{W}_{g,n}
are partially inverse Laplace transformed correlation functions for
(g,n)\neq(0,2)
,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n-1}}\widetilde{W}_{g,n}(z,L_{K})\mathrm{e}^{-\sum_{i=2}^{n}z_{i}L_{i}}\prod_{i=2}^{n}L_{i}{\rm d}L_{i}=W_{g,n}(z,z_{K}),

(B.2)

and for
(g,n)=(0,2)
,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\widetilde{W}_{0,2}(z,L_{m})\mathrm{e}^{-z_{m}L_{m}}L_{m}{\rm d}L_{m}=W_{0,2}(z,z_{m})=\frac{1}{(z-z_{m})^{2}},

which gives

\displaystyle\widetilde{W}_{0,2}(z,L_{m})=\mathrm{e}^{zL_{m}}.

(B.3)

In terms of the fully inverse Laplace transformed function
V_{g,n}
for the correlation function
W_{g,n}
,

\displaystyle W_{g,n}(z,z_{K})=\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n}}V_{g,n}(L,L_{K})\mathrm{e}^{-zL-\sum_{i=2}^{n}z_{i}L_{i}}L{\rm d}L\prod_{i=2}^{n}L_{i}{\rm d}L_{i},

the relation (
B.2
) leads to

\displaystyle\widetilde{W}_{g,n}(z,L_{K})=\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}V_{g,n}(L,L_{K})\mathrm{e}^{-zL}L{\rm d}L.

In the recursion (
B.1
),

\widetilde{Q}_{g,n}
is a partial Laplace transform of

P_{g,n}
:

\displaystyle\widetilde{Q}_{g,n}(z,z,L_{K})=\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2}}xyP_{g,n}(x,y,L_{K})\mathrm{e}^{-z(x+y)}{\rm d}x{\rm d}y,

where
P_{g,n}
is the Laplace dual of
Q_{g,n}
given by

\displaystyle Q_{g,n}(z,z,z_{K}):={}

\displaystyle W_{g-1,n+1}(z,z,z_{K})+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}h+h^{\prime}=g\\
J\sqcup J^{\prime}=K\end{subarray}}^{\mathrm{stable}}W_{h,1+|J|}(z,z_{J})W_{h^{\prime},1+|J^{\prime}|}(z,z_{J^{\prime}})

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n+1}}xyP_{g,n}(x,y,L_{K})\mathrm{e}^{-z(x+y)-\sum_{i=2}^{n}z_{i}L_{i}}{\rm d}x{\rm d}y\prod_{i=2}^{n}L_{i}{\rm d}L_{i}.

In the following computations, we will rewrite equation (
B.1
) into the recursion relation for
V_{g,n}
to find the basic data
of the ABO topological recursion.

B.1
Derivation for the
\boldsymbol{(2,p)}
minimal string

We will rewrite the partially Laplace transformed CEO topological recursion (
B.1
) with the coordinate function
\mathsf{y}=\mathsf{y}^{\mathrm{M}(p)}

in equation (
4.25
) into the form of
the Mirzakhani type ABO topological recursion (
2.8
) written in terms of the kernel function in equation (
2.10
) with

H^{\mathrm{M}(p)}(x,y)
:

\displaystyle 2\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}V_{g,n}(L,L_{K})\mathrm{e}^{-zL}L{\rm d}L

\displaystyle\qquad=\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{3}}\left(\int_{0}^{L}H^{\mathrm{M}(p)}(x+y,t){\rm d}t\right)xyP_{g,n}(x,y,L_{K})\mathrm{e}^{-zL}{\rm d}L{\rm d}x{\rm d}y

\displaystyle\qquad\phantom{}{}+\sum_{m=2}^{n}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2}}\left(\int_{0}^{L}\left(H^{\mathrm{M}(p)}(x,t+L_{m})+H^{\mathrm{M}(p)}(x,t-L_{m})\right){\rm d}t\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\phantom{=+}{}\times xV_{g,n-1}(x,L_{K\setminus\{m\}})\mathrm{e}^{-zL}{\rm d}L{\rm d}x

\displaystyle\qquad=\frac{1}{z}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{3}}xyH^{\mathrm{M}(p)}(x+y,t)P_{g,n}(x,y,L_{K})\mathrm{e}^{-zt}{\rm d}t{\rm d}x{\rm d}y

\displaystyle\qquad\phantom{=}{}+\sum_{m=2}^{n}\frac{1}{z}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2}}\left(H^{\mathrm{M}(p)}(x,t+L_{m})+H^{\mathrm{M}(p)}(x,t-L_{m})\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\phantom{=+}{}\times xV_{g,n-1}(x,L_{K\setminus\{m\}})\mathrm{e}^{-zt}{\rm d}t{\rm d}x.

(B.4)

Firstly, we focus on the first term on the right-hand side of equation (
B.1
),

\displaystyle\underset{w=0}{\mathrm{Res}}\frac{{\rm d}w}{2w\mathsf{y}^{\mathrm{M}(p)}(w)}\left(\frac{1}{z-w}-\frac{1}{z+w}\right)\widetilde{Q}_{g,n}(w,w,L_{K}).

(B.5)

Based on the following properties:

•

\widetilde{Q}_{g,n}(w,w,L_{K})
and
w\mathsf{y}^{\mathrm{M}(p)}(w)
are even functions of
w
,

•

{\rm d}w/\mathsf{y}^{\mathrm{M}(p)}(w)
has poles at
w=u_{j}=(p/2\pi)\sin(j\pi/p)

(
j=0,\pm 1,\dots,\pm(p-1)/2
)
with the residue
(-1)^{j}\cos(\pi j/p)
by equation (
4.60
),

equation (
B.5
) is rewritten as

\displaystyle-\left[\sum_{j=1}^{\frac{p-1}{2}}\underset{w=\pm u_{j}}{\mathrm{Res}}+\underset{w=z}{\mathrm{Res}}\right]\frac{{\rm d}w}{w\mathsf{y}^{\mathrm{M}(p)}(w)}\frac{1}{z-w}\widetilde{Q}_{g,n}(w,w,L_{K})

\displaystyle\qquad=-\sum_{j=1}^{\frac{p-1}{2}}(-1)^{j}\cos\left(\frac{\pi j}{p}\right)\frac{2}{z^{2}-u_{j}^{2}}\widetilde{Q}_{g,n}\left(u_{j},u_{j},L_{K}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\phantom{=}{}+\frac{1}{z}\Bigg(\frac{1}{z}+\sum_{j=1}^{\frac{p-1}{2}}(-1)^{j}\cos\left(\frac{\pi j}{p}\right)\left(\frac{1}{z-u_{j}}+\frac{1}{z+u_{j}}\right)\Bigg)\widetilde{Q}_{g,n}(z,z,L_{K})

\displaystyle\qquad{}=\frac{1}{z}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2}}\Biggl(-\sum_{j=1}^{\frac{p-1}{2}}(-1)^{j}\cos\left(\frac{\pi j}{p}\right)\frac{\mathrm{e}^{-u_{j}(x+y)}}{z+u_{j}}+\sum_{j=0}^{\frac{p-1}{2}}(-1)^{j}\cos\left(\frac{\pi j}{p}\right)\frac{\mathrm{e}^{-z(x+y)}}{z+u_{j}}

\displaystyle\qquad\phantom{=}{}-\sum_{j=1}^{\frac{p-1}{2}}(-1)^{j}\cos\left(\frac{\pi j}{p}\right)\frac{\mathrm{e}^{-u_{j}(x+y)}-\mathrm{e}^{-z(x+y)}}{z-u_{j}}\Biggr)xyP_{g,n}(x,y,L_{K}){\rm d}x{\rm d}y

\displaystyle\qquad{}=\frac{1}{z}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2}}\Biggl(-\sum_{j=1}^{\frac{p-1}{2}}(-1)^{j}\cos\left(\frac{\pi j}{p}\right)\left(\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{e}^{-u_{j}(x+y+t)}\mathrm{e}^{-zt}{\rm d}t+\int_{0}^{x+y}\mathrm{e}^{-u_{j}(x+y-t)}\mathrm{e}^{-zt}{\rm d}t\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\phantom{=}{}+\sum_{j=0}^{\frac{p-1}{2}}(-1)^{j}\cos\left(\frac{\pi j}{p}\right)\left(\int_{x+y}^{\infty}\mathrm{e}^{+u_{j}(x+y-t)}\mathrm{e}^{-zt}{\rm d}t\right)\Biggr)xyP_{g,n}(x,y,L_{K}){\rm d}x{\rm d}y.

Comparing the final expression with the first term in equation (
B.4
), we find the kernel function
{H^{\mathrm{M}(p)}(x,y)}
given in equation (
2.19
).
34
34

34

The kernel function
H^{\mathrm{M}(p)}(x,y)
in equation (
2.19
) is symmetrized under the action
y\to-y
.
The extra terms which appear in the anti-symmetrization do not contribute to the integrals in the first term in equation (
B.4
),
because the Heaviside functions in the extra terms vanish in these integrals.

Consistency check.
To check the consistency of the kernel function
H^{\mathrm{M}(p)}(x,y)

derived above, we will focus on the second term on the right-hand side of equation (
B.1
), and show the following relation:

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\underset{w=0}{\mathrm{Res}}\Biggl[\frac{{\rm d}w}{\mathsf{y}^{\mathrm{M}(p)}(w)}\frac{2}{z^{2}-w^{2}}\widetilde{W}_{0,2}(w,L_{m})\widetilde{W}_{g,n-1}(w,L_{K\setminus\{m\}})\Biggr]\mathrm{e}^{-z_{m}L_{m}}{\rm d}L_{m}

\displaystyle\qquad=\frac{1}{z}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{3}}\big(H^{\mathrm{M}(p)}(x,t+L_{m})+H^{\mathrm{M}(p)}(x,t-L_{m})\big)xV_{g,n-1}(x,L_{K\setminus\{m\}}

\displaystyle\qquad\phantom{=}{}\times\mathrm{e}^{-zt-z_{m}L_{m}}{\rm d}t{\rm d}x{\rm d}L_{m}.

(B.6)

Using the expression (
B.3
) of
\widetilde{W}_{0,2}(w,L_{m})
,
we rewrite the left-hand side of equation (
B.6
) as

\displaystyle\underset{w=0}{\mathrm{Res}}\frac{{\rm d}w}{\mathsf{y}^{\mathrm{M}(p)}(w)}\frac{2}{z^{2}-w^{2}}\frac{1}{z_{m}-w}\widetilde{W}_{g,n-1}(w,L_{K\setminus\{m\}})

\displaystyle\quad=\underset{w=0}{\mathrm{Res}}\frac{{\rm d}w}{\mathsf{y}^{\mathrm{M}(p)}(w)}\frac{1}{z^{2}-w^{2}}\left(\frac{1}{z_{m}-w}+\frac{1}{z_{m}+w}\right)\widetilde{W}_{g,n-1}(w,L_{K\setminus\{m\}})

\displaystyle\quad=\underset{w=0}{\mathrm{Res}}\frac{2{\rm d}w}{\mathsf{y}^{\mathrm{M}(p)}(w)}\frac{z_{m}}{(z^{2}-w^{2})(z_{m}^{2}-w^{2})}\widetilde{W}_{g,n-1}(w,L_{K\setminus\{m\}})

\displaystyle\quad=\underset{w=0}{\mathrm{Res}}\frac{2{\rm d}w}{\mathsf{y}^{\mathrm{M}(p)}(w)}\frac{1}{z(z_{m}^{2}-z^{2})}\left(\frac{z_{m}}{z-w}-\frac{z}{z_{m}-w}\right)\widetilde{W}_{g,n-1}(w,L_{K\setminus\{m\}})

\displaystyle\quad=-\left[\sum_{j=1}^{\frac{p-1}{2}}\underset{w=\pm u_{j}}{\mathrm{Res}}+\underset{w=z,z_{m}}{\mathrm{Res}}\right]\frac{2{\rm d}w}{\mathsf{y}^{\mathrm{M}(p)}(w)}\frac{1}{z(z_{m}^{2}-z^{2})}\left(\frac{z_{m}}{z-w}-\frac{z}{z_{m}-w}\right)\widetilde{W}_{g,n-1}(w,L_{K\setminus\{m\}})

\displaystyle\quad=\frac{2}{z(z_{m}^{2}-z^{2})}\Bigg(-\sum_{j=1}^{\frac{p-1}{2}}(-1)^{j}\cos\left(\frac{j\pi}{p}\right)\left(\frac{z_{m}}{z+u_{j}}+\frac{z_{m}}{z-u_{j}}-\frac{z}{z_{m}+u_{j}}-\frac{z}{z_{m}-u_{j}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\phantom{=}{}\times\widetilde{W}_{g,n-1}(u_{j},L_{K\setminus\{m\}})+\sum_{-\frac{p-1}{2}\leq j\leq\frac{p-1}{2}}(-1)^{j}\cos\left(\frac{j\pi}{p}\right)\frac{z_{m}}{z-u_{j}}\widetilde{W}_{g,n-1}(z,L_{K\setminus\{m\}})

\displaystyle\quad\phantom{=\times}{}-\sum_{-\frac{p-1}{2}\leq j\leq\frac{p-1}{2}}(-1)^{j}\cos\left(\frac{j\pi}{p}\right)\frac{z}{z_{m}-u_{j}}\widetilde{W}_{g,n-1}(z_{m},L_{K\setminus\{m\}})\Bigg)

\displaystyle\quad=\frac{2}{z(z_{m}^{2}-z^{2})}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\Bigg[-\sum_{j=1}^{\frac{p-1}{2}}(-1)^{j}\cos\left(\frac{j\pi}{p}\right)\left(\frac{z_{m}\mathrm{e}^{-u_{j}x}}{z+u_{j}}-\frac{z\mathrm{e}^{-u_{j}x}}{z_{m}+u_{j}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\phantom{=}{}-\sum_{j=1}^{\frac{p-1}{2}}(-1)^{j}\cos\left(\frac{j\pi}{p}\right)\left(\frac{z_{m}(\mathrm{e}^{-u_{j}x}-\mathrm{e}^{-zx})}{z-u_{j}}-\frac{z(\mathrm{e}^{-u_{j}x}-\mathrm{e}^{-z_{m}x})}{z_{m}-u_{j}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\phantom{=}{}+\sum_{j=0}^{\frac{p-1}{2}}(-1)^{j}\cos\left(\frac{j\pi}{p}\right)\left(\frac{z_{m}\mathrm{e}^{-zx}}{z+u_{j}}-\frac{z\mathrm{e}^{-z_{m}x}}{z_{m}+u_{j}}\right)\Bigg]xV_{g,n-1}(x,L_{K\setminus\{m\}}){\rm d}x

\displaystyle\quad=\frac{2}{z}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\Bigg[-\sum_{j=1}^{\frac{p-1}{2}}(-1)^{j}\cos\left(\frac{j\pi}{p}\right)\left(\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{e}^{-u_{j}(x+t)}\frac{z_{m}\mathrm{e}^{-zt}-z\mathrm{e}^{-z_{m}t}}{z_{m}^{2}-z^{2}}{\rm d}t\right.

\displaystyle\quad\phantom{=}{}+\left.\int_{0}^{x}\mathrm{e}^{-u_{j}(x-t)}\frac{z_{m}\mathrm{e}^{-zt}-z\mathrm{e}^{-z_{m}t}}{z_{m}^{2}-z^{2}}{\rm d}t\right)

\displaystyle\quad\phantom{=}{}+\sum_{j=0}^{\frac{p-1}{2}}(-1)^{j}\cos\left(\frac{j\pi}{p}\right)\left(\int_{x}^{\infty}\mathrm{e}^{+u_{j}(x-t)}\frac{z_{m}\mathrm{e}^{-zt}-z\mathrm{e}^{-z_{m}t}}{z_{m}^{2}-z^{2}}{\rm d}t\right)\Bigg]

\displaystyle\quad\phantom{=+}{}\times xV_{g,n-1}(x,L_{K\setminus\{m\}}){\rm d}x.

(B.7)

On the other hand, the right-hand side of equation (
B.6
) is rewritten as follows
[
38
]
:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{z}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2}}\left(\int_{L_{m}}^{\infty}\mathrm{e}^{-z(t-L_{m})}H^{\mathrm{M}(p)}(x,t){\rm d}t+\int_{-L_{m}}^{\infty}\mathrm{e}^{-z(t+L_{m})}H^{\mathrm{M}(p)}(x,t){\rm d}t\right)

\displaystyle\phantom{\quad=}{}\times xV_{g,n-1}(x,L_{K\setminus\{m\}})\mathrm{e}^{-z_{m}L_{m}}{\rm d}x{\rm d}L_{m}

\displaystyle\quad=\frac{1}{z}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\Bigg(\int_{0}^{\infty}\left(\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{e}^{-(z_{m}-z)L_{m}}{\rm d}L_{m}+\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{e}^{-(z_{m}+z)L_{m}}{\rm d}L_{m}\right)\mathrm{e}^{-zt}H^{\mathrm{M}(p)}(x,t){\rm d}t

\displaystyle\phantom{\quad=}{}+\int_{-\infty}^{0}\left(\int_{-t}^{\infty}\mathrm{e}^{-(z_{m}+z)L_{m}}{\rm d}L_{m}\right)\mathrm{e}^{-zt}H^{\mathrm{M}(p)}(x,t){\rm d}t\Bigg)xV_{g,n-1}(x,L_{K\setminus\{m\}}){\rm d}x

\displaystyle\phantom{\quad}{}=\frac{1}{z}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2}}\left(\frac{\mathrm{e}^{-zt}-\mathrm{e}^{-z_{m}t}}{z_{m}-z}+\frac{\mathrm{e}^{-zt}+\mathrm{e}^{-z_{m}t}}{z_{m}+z}\right)H^{\mathrm{M}(p)}(x,t)xV_{g,n-1}(x,L_{K\setminus\{m\}}){\rm d}t{\rm d}x,

(B.8)

where
H^{\mathrm{M}(p)}(x,-t)=H^{\mathrm{M}(p)}(x,t)
is used in the last equality.
Applying the kernel function
{H^{\mathrm{M}(p)}(x,y)}
in equation (
2.19
) to the final answer of equation (
B.8
),
we find the final expression of equation (
B.7
).
Thus we derived the kernel function
H^{\mathrm{M}(p)}(x,y)
in equation (
2.19
) of the Mirzakhani type ABO topological recursion
for the
(2,p)
minimal string.

B.2
Derivation for the
\boldsymbol{(2,2p-2)}
minimal superstring

In the same way as the
(2,p)
minimal string, we will rewrite the partially Laplace transformed CEO topological recursion (
B.1
) with the coordinate function
\mathsf{y}=\mathsf{y}^{\mathrm{SM}(p)}

in equation (
4.40
) into the form of the Mirzakhani type ABO topological recursion (
2.8
) written in terms of the kernel function in equation (
2.24
) with
H^{\mathrm{SM}(p)}(x,y)
:

\displaystyle 2\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}V_{g,n}(L,L_{K})\mathrm{e}^{-zL}L{\rm d}L

\displaystyle\qquad=\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{3}}xyH^{\mathrm{SM}(p)}(x+y,L)P_{g,n}(x,y,L_{K})\mathrm{e}^{-zL}{\rm d}L{\rm d}x{\rm d}y

\displaystyle\qquad\phantom{=}{}+\sum_{m=2}^{n}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2}}\big(H^{\mathrm{SM}(p)}(x,L+L_{m})+H^{\mathrm{SM}(p)}(x,L-L_{m})\big)

\displaystyle\qquad\phantom{=+}{}\times xV_{g,n-1}(x,L_{K\setminus\{m\}})\mathrm{e}^{-zL}{\rm d}L{\rm d}x.

(B.9)

Adopting the following properties:

•

\widetilde{Q}_{g,n}(w,w,L_{K})
and
w\mathsf{y}^{\mathrm{SM}(p)}(w)
are even functions of
w
;

•

{\rm d}w/\mathsf{y}^{\mathrm{SM}(p)}(w)
has poles at
w=\pm u_{j}^{\prime}=\pm(p/2\pi)\sin((j-1/2)\pi/p)
,
(j=1,\dots,(p-1)/2)

with the residue

+u_{j}^{\prime}(1/2\pi)(-1)^{j}\cos^{2}(\pi(j-1/2)/p)
by equation (
4.72
);

the first term on the right-hand side of the partially Laplace transformed CEO topological recursion (
B.1
) is rewritten as

\displaystyle-\left[\sum_{j=1}^{\frac{p-1}{2}}\underset{w=\pm u^{\prime}_{j}}{\mathrm{Res}}+\underset{w=z}{\mathrm{Res}}\right]\frac{{\rm d}w}{z-w}\frac{1}{w\mathsf{y}^{\mathrm{SM}(p)}(w)}\widetilde{Q}_{g,n}(w,w,L_{K})

\displaystyle\qquad=-\sum_{j=1}^{\frac{p-1}{2}}\frac{(-1)^{j}}{2\pi}\cos^{2}\left(\frac{\pi}{p}\left(j-\frac{1}{2}\right)\right)\left(\frac{1}{z-u_{j}^{\prime}}-\frac{1}{z+u^{\prime}_{j}}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\phantom{=-}{}\times\big(\widetilde{Q}_{g,n}(u_{j}^{\prime},u_{j}^{\prime},L_{K})-\widetilde{Q}_{g,n}(z,z,L_{K})\big)+\widetilde{Q}_{g,n}(z,z,L_{K})\delta_{p,1}

\displaystyle\qquad{}=\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2}}\left(\sum_{j=1}^{\frac{p-1}{2}}\frac{(-1)^{j}}{2\pi}\cos^{2}\left(\frac{\pi}{p}\left(j-\frac{1}{2}\right)\right)\left(\frac{1}{z+u_{j}^{\prime}}-\frac{1}{z-u^{\prime}_{j}}\right)\big(\mathrm{e}^{-u_{j}^{\prime}(x+y)}-\mathrm{e}^{-z(x+y)}\big)\right.

\displaystyle\left.\qquad\phantom{=}{}+\mathrm{e}^{-z(x+y)}\delta_{p,1}\right)xyP_{g,n}(x,y,L_{K}){\rm d}x{\rm d}y

\displaystyle\qquad{}=\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2}}\left(\sum_{j=1}^{\frac{p-1}{2}}\frac{(-1)^{j}}{2\pi}\cos^{2}\left(\frac{\pi}{p}\left(j-\frac{1}{2}\right)\right)\left(\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{e}^{-u_{j}^{\prime}(x+y+L)}\mathrm{e}^{-zL}{\rm d}L\right.\right.

\displaystyle\left.\left.\qquad\phantom{=}{}-\int_{0}^{x+y}\mathrm{e}^{-u_{j}^{\prime}(x+y-L)}\mathrm{e}^{-zL}{\rm d}L-\int_{x+y}^{\infty}\mathrm{e}^{+u_{j}^{\prime}(x+y-L)}\mathrm{e}^{-zL}{\rm d}L\right)\right.

\displaystyle\left.\qquad\phantom{=}{}+\delta_{p,1}\int_{0}^{\infty}\delta(L-x-y)\mathrm{e}^{-zL}{\rm d}L\right)xyP_{g,n}(x,y,L_{K}){\rm d}x{\rm d}y.

Comparing the final expression with the first term in equation (
B.9
), we find the kernel function
H^{\mathrm{SM}(p)}(x,y)
given in equation (
2.31
).
35
35

35

The kernel function
H^{\mathrm{SM}(p)}(x,y)
in equation (
2.31
) is anti-symmetrized under the action

y\to-y
.
The extra terms which appear in the anti-symmetrization do not contribute to the integrals in the first term in equation (
B.9
),
because the Heaviside functions and delta functions in the extra terms vanish in these integrals.

Consistency check.
As the (bosonic) minimal string, we will show the following relation
to check the consistency of the kernel function
H^{\mathrm{SM}(p)}(x,y)
:

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\underset{w=0}{\mathrm{Res}}\left[\frac{{\rm d}w}{\mathsf{y}^{\mathrm{SM}(p)}(w)}\frac{2}{z^{2}-w^{2}}\widetilde{W}_{0,2}(w,L_{m})\widetilde{W}_{g,n-1}(w,L_{K\setminus\{m\}})\right]\mathrm{e}^{-z_{m}L_{m}}{\rm d}L_{m}

\displaystyle\qquad=\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{3}}\big(H^{\mathrm{SM}(p)}(x,L+L_{m})+H^{\mathrm{SM}(p)}(x,L-L_{m})\big)xV_{g,n-1}(x,L_{K\setminus\{m\}})

\displaystyle\qquad\phantom{=}{}\times\mathrm{e}^{-zL-z_{m}L_{m}}{\rm d}L{\rm d}x{\rm d}L_{m}.

(B.10)

The left-hand side of equation (
B.10
) is rewritten
in the similar way as equation (
B.7
):

\displaystyle-\left[\sum_{j=1}^{\frac{p-1}{2}}\underset{w=\pm u_{j}^{\prime}}{\mathrm{Res}}+\underset{w=z,z_{m}}{\mathrm{Res}}\right]\frac{2{\rm d}w}{\mathsf{y}^{\mathrm{SM}(p)}(w)}\frac{1}{z\big(z_{m}^{2}-z^{2}\big)}\left(\frac{z_{m}}{z-w}-\frac{z}{z_{m}-w}\right)\widetilde{W}_{g,n-1}(w,L_{K\setminus\{m\}})

\displaystyle\qquad=\frac{2}{z_{m}^{2}-z^{2}}\left[-\sum_{j=1}^{\frac{p-1}{2}}\frac{(-1)^{j}}{2\pi}\cos^{2}\left(\frac{\pi}{p}\left(j-\frac{1}{2}\right)\right)\right.

\displaystyle\left.\qquad\phantom{=}{}\times\left(-\frac{z_{m}}{z+u_{j}^{\prime}}+\frac{z_{m}}{z-u_{j}^{\prime}}+\frac{z_{m}}{z_{m}+u_{j}^{\prime}}-\frac{z_{m}}{z_{m}-u_{j}^{\prime}}\right)\widetilde{W}_{g,n-1}(u_{j}^{\prime},L_{K\setminus\{m\}})\right.

\displaystyle\left.\qquad\phantom{=\times}{}+\left(z_{m}\delta_{p,1}+\sum_{j=1}^{\frac{p-1}{2}}\frac{(-1)^{j}}{2\pi}\cos^{2}\left(\frac{\pi}{p}\left(j-\frac{1}{2}\right)\right)\left(\frac{z_{m}}{z-u_{j}^{\prime}}-\frac{z_{m}}{z+u_{j}^{\prime}}\right)\right)\right.

\displaystyle\left.\qquad\phantom{=}{}\times\widetilde{W}_{g,n-1}(z,L_{K\setminus\{m\}})\right.

\displaystyle\left.\left.\qquad\phantom{=\times}{}-\left(z_{m}\delta_{p,1}+\sum_{j=1}^{\frac{p-1}{2}}\frac{(-1)^{j}}{2\pi}\cos^{2}\left(\frac{\pi}{p}\left(j-\frac{1}{2}\right)\right)\left(\frac{z_{m}}{z_{m}-u_{j}^{\prime}}-\frac{z_{m}}{z_{m}+u_{j}^{\prime}}\right)\right)\right.\right.

\displaystyle\left.\qquad\phantom{=}{}\times\widetilde{W}_{g,n-1}(z_{m},L_{K\setminus\{m\}})\right]

\displaystyle\qquad=\frac{2z_{m}}{z_{m}^{2}-z^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\left[\sum_{j=1}^{\frac{p-1}{2}}\frac{(-1)^{j}}{2\pi}\cos^{2}\left(\frac{\pi}{p}\left(j-\frac{1}{2}\right)\right)\right.

\displaystyle\left.\qquad\phantom{=}{}\times\left(\frac{\mathrm{e}^{-u_{j}^{\prime}x}-\mathrm{e}^{-zx}}{z+u_{j}^{\prime}}-\frac{\mathrm{e}^{-u_{j}^{\prime}x}-\mathrm{e}^{-zx}}{z-u_{j}^{\prime}}-\frac{\mathrm{e}^{-u_{j}^{\prime}x}-\mathrm{e}^{-z_{m}x}}{z_{m}+u_{j}^{\prime}}+\frac{\mathrm{e}^{-u_{j}^{\prime}x}-\mathrm{e}^{-z_{m}x}}{z_{m}-u_{j}^{\prime}}\right)\right.

\displaystyle\left.\qquad\phantom{=\times}{}+\left(\mathrm{e}^{-zx}-\mathrm{e}^{-z_{m}x}\right)\delta_{p,1}\right]xV_{g,n-1}(x,L_{K\setminus\{m\}}){\rm d}x

\displaystyle\qquad=2z_{m}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\left[\sum_{j=1}^{\frac{p-1}{2}}\frac{(-1)^{j}}{2\pi}\cos^{2}\left(\frac{\pi}{p}\left(j-\frac{1}{2}\right)\right)\left(\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{e}^{-u_{j}^{\prime}(x+L)}\frac{\mathrm{e}^{-zL}-\mathrm{e}^{-z_{m}L}}{z_{m}^{2}-z^{2}}{\rm d}L\right.\right.

\displaystyle\left.\left.\qquad\phantom{=}{}-\int_{0}^{x}\mathrm{e}^{-u_{j}^{\prime}(x-L)}\frac{\mathrm{e}^{-zL}-\mathrm{e}^{-z_{m}L}}{z_{m}^{2}-z^{2}}{\rm d}L-\int_{x}^{\infty}\mathrm{e}^{+u_{j}^{\prime}(x-L)}\frac{\mathrm{e}^{-zL}-\mathrm{e}^{-z_{m}L}}{z_{m}^{2}-z^{2}}{\rm d}L\right)\right.

\displaystyle\left.\qquad\phantom{=}{}+\delta_{p,1}\int_{0}^{\infty}\delta(L-x)\frac{\mathrm{e}^{-zL}-\mathrm{e}^{-z_{m}L}}{z_{m}^{2}-z^{2}}{\rm d}L\right]xV_{g,n-1}(x,L_{K\setminus\{m\}}){\rm d}x.

(B.11)

On the other hand, similar to equation (
B.8
),
the right-hand side of equation (
B.10
) is rewritten as

\displaystyle 2z_{m}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2}}\left(\frac{\mathrm{e}^{-zt}-\mathrm{e}^{-z_{m}t}}{z_{m}-z}+\frac{\mathrm{e}^{-zt}-\mathrm{e}^{-z_{m}t}}{z_{m}+z}\right)H^{\mathrm{SM}(p)}(x,L)xV_{g,n-1}(x,L_{K\setminus\{m\}}){\rm d}L{\rm d}x,

(B.12)

where
H^{\mathrm{SM}(p)}(x,-L)=-H^{\mathrm{SM}(p)}(x,L)
is used.
Applying the kernel function
H^{\mathrm{SM}(p)}(x,y)
in equation (
2.31
) to equation (
B.12
), the final expression of equation (
B.11
) is found.
Thus,
the consistency equation (
B.10
)
for the kernel function
H^{\mathrm{SM}(p)}(x,y)
is verified, and
the CEO topological recursion for the spectral curve
\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{SM}(p)}
is shown to be equivalent to
the Mirzakhani type ABO topological recursion with the kernel function
H^{\mathrm{SM}(p)}(x,y)
.

Appendix C
Volume polynomials

In this appendix, we give some computational results of volume polynomials
for the 2D gravity models and their Masur–Veech type twist.
